#5201. XRP Ledger Scores Historic First in Global Banking Standards Review

XRP Ledger (XRPL) has marked a significant step in its journey to institutional adoption by becoming the first public permissionless layer-1 blockchain to see its principles of Architecture and Resiliency independently verified by the Global Layer One (GL1) Market Infrastructure Toolkit.

The foundation’s self-assessment was independently verified by the blockchain security firm CertiK on the responses and evidence submitted around the principles of Architecture and Resiliency.

The independent attestation report was released on Nov. 13, further strengthening the assertion of XRP Ledger's compliance with institutional financial infrastructure requirements.

This comes at a time when banks and asset managers are increasingly considering blockchain technology to enable tokenization of deposits, stablecoins, investment funds and other financial instruments.

The Global Layer One initiative is a collaborative effort between prominent financial institutions and policymakers to set standards for digital financial infrastructure.

Its main goal is to make cross-border transactions for tokenised assets seamless while ensuring that blockchain networks adhere to internationally expected standards for financial market infrastructure.

Interestingly, XRPLF self-assessed XRPL for all 10 principles.

CertiK independently verified it only for the principles of Architecture and Resiliency.

XRPLF also provided details on how requirements specific to private/permissioned networks are addressed by managing related risks for XRPL’s decentralized nature, open-source governance model, and protocol amendments.

XRP Ledger Foundation executive director Brett Mollin emphasized the need to give financial institutions tangible evidence instead of pitching ideas.

Mollin added that independent attestation from CertiK also gives financial institutions more confidence while vetting the network.

It does not mean that XRPL meets all the standards mentioned in the toolkit completely.

The XRP Ledger Foundation will continue to update its assessment in line with the development of GL1 principles and XRPL protocol amendments that are activated.

Nonetheless, it's another step towards XRPL's bid to be a possible infrastructure for regulated financial institutions looking for blockchain-based settlement and tokenization.

#5200. Ripple’s Brad Garlinghouse Says Crypto Markets Could Rebound After Anthropic Goes Public

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse believes the recent crypto market sell-off stems largely from investors rotating capital into high-profile initial public offerings. The Ripple boss and crypto billionaire anticipates a recovery once Anthropic’s massive listing is fully absorbed.

Speaking to CNBC on November 19th, Garlinghouse drew a direct parallel to SpaceX’s public debut earlier in the year. He explained that when major IPOs generate intense demand, investors often sell liquid assets such as Bitcoin and XRP to free up cash for share subscriptions.

The same pattern, he said, unfolded ahead of Anthropic’s offering. After SpaceX completed its listing, crypto markets staged a notable rebound. Garlinghouse expects a similar bounce this time.

Anthropic, the artificial-intelligence company behind the Claude chatbot family, has priced its IPO at $83 per share, above the previously marketed range of $70 to $78.

The deal ranks among the largest IPOs in history, raising tens of billions of dollars and valuing the company in the trillion-dollar range, and shares are set to begin trading on Nasdaq under the ticker ANT.

Such blockbuster offerings typically attract enormous institutional and retail interest, temporarily diverting capital from other risk assets. Once the primary allocation is complete and secondary-market trading stabilizes, some of that capital often flows back into crypto and other liquid markets.

Bitcoin had slipped near $62,000 on Wednesday before recovering above $63,000 early Thursday. Garlinghouse framed the dip as a temporary capital-rotation effect rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in digital assets. He also expressed confidence that Anthropic’s shares would trade strongly given the strong demand and pricing above the range.

Looking further ahead, Garlinghouse remained optimistic about blockchain’s long-term role. He predicted that within roughly a decade, blockchain technology will be deeply embedded across global financial infrastructure, much as artificial intelligence has already transformed multiple industries.

His comments highlight how large technology IPOs can create short-term pressure on crypto while potentially reinforcing longer-term narratives around innovation and capital markets.

ChatGPT AI predicts that Pudgy Penguins’ expansion beyond NFTs and into physical trading cards could see a parabolic move for PENGU

Sam Altman’s ChatGPT AI predicts an intriguing move regarding Pudgy Penguins (PENGU). This forecast differs from a typical bullish outlook; instead, it envisions an extreme and imaginative scenario based on a hypothetical major event. While the outcome isn’t impossible, it remains quite unlikely.

The prediction suggests that Pudgy Penguins could secure a significant multi-year partnership with a major global entertainment and consumer brand conglomerate. Such a partnership would elevate the Penguins’ intellectual property (IP) to a mainstream cultural franchise.

This development could involve producing large-scale animated content, launching global retail product lines, and integrating the PENGU token into fan engagement, merchandise, and digital experiences. This transformation would shift the PENGU token from being a crypto-native meme asset to a widely recognized consumer brand token.

SOURCE: ChatGPT AI Predicts a Parabolic PENGU Move

On higher timeframes, PENGU has been consolidating in the $0.0046 to $0.005 range after a prolonged downtrend from earlier peaks. Price continues to hold above recent lows but faces overhead resistance in the $0.006 to $0.007 zone.

In a typical bull market, a sustained breakout above the $0.01 to $0.012 range would indicate intermediate strength and pave the way for higher recovery targets.

If mainstream entertainment and retail franchise adoption become extreme, breaking through key resistance levels could trigger powerful measured-move extensions and multi-cycle Fibonacci projections based on the longer-term foundation.

Currently, momentum indicators are positioned in neutral to oversold territory, leaving substantial room for upward movement into the extreme overbought readings often seen during parabolic advances in meme assets.

Key near-term support levels are around $0.0040 to $0.0042. Holding above these levels would help maintain the broader market structure.

#5197. Hyperliquid Pushes for 7 Key Tweaks Ahead of UK’s 2027 Crypto Overhaul

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The Hyperliquid Policy Center (HPC) sent a letter to the UK Financial Conduct Authority (FCA) on Nov. 18 with seven recommendations on the draft documents CP26/36 and GC26/7. Three of them concern CP26/36, and four concern GC26/7. The letter was signed by HPC chief executive Jake Chervinsky.

The UK's new crypto regime will start to apply on Oct. 25, 2027. The FCA opened its window for crypto license applications on Sept. 30, 2026, and the window will close on Feb. 28, 2027.

According to the letter, "Regulatory obligations belong with persons who have unilateral control over a function that is a regulated activity." HPC argued that attaching obligations to a person who could not discharge them "would offer consumers no additional protection."

In the letter, HPC also compared public blockchains with the internet. "Like a public blockchain, the internet depends on protocols that identifiable persons maintain and upgrade over time," the group wrote.

HPC asked the FCA to assess each participant in a blockchain network separately. Proprietary traders and market makers "supply much of the liquidity on which order books depend," the group wrote. HPC also asked the regulator to describe compliance requirements as outcomes and to hold regulated firms accountable for them.

On Aug. 24, HPC urged the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) and the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) to harmonize their rules for perpetual contracts. The group argued that such products should be classified by their economic structure. According to HPC, a harmonized framework would let exchanges compete on "execution quality and liquidity."

On Sept. 30, HPC sent its response to the European Union's review of the MiCA rules. In it, the group asked the EU to treat crypto perpetual futures as derivatives under MiFID II. "Clarity in application, rather than new legislation, is what would assist in regulating perpetual futures," HPC wrote at the time.

Chervinsky previously served as chief policy officer at the Blockchain Association and as chief legal officer at the crypto venture firm Variant.

#5196. Anthropic’s IPO Succeeds, But AI Stocks, Bitcoin & Asian Markets Sink – What’s Next

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

A passionate cryptocurrency and blockchain author qualified to cover every event in the crypto space. Researching minute occurrences and bringing new insights lie within the prime focus of my task.

Anthropic's record IPO failed to lift AI sentiment, as Wall Street's sell-off spread across technology stocks and global financial markets.

Asian markets tumbled, with Japan, South Korea and Taiwan facing sharp losses amid continued pressure on semiconductor and AI-linked stocks.

Bitcoin fell below $63,000 as broader risk aversion, ETF outflows and macroeconomic uncertainty weighed on the cryptocurrency market.

The most-awaited Anthropic IPO went live, marking a successful debut, but the broader financial markets offered little sign of enthusiasm. Despite translating into renewed optimism across the technology sector, market sentiment remained under pressure. The US stocks, tech shares and Asian markets recorded sharp declines. South Korea’s KOSPI and Japan’s equity markets were among those caught in the broader sell-off. In the meantime, the Bitcoin price also slipped to $61,500, with the global market cap plunging to $2.13 trillion.

The simultaneous weakness across equities, technology stocks and crypto has put risk appetite back under scrutiny, turning what was a major AI industry event into a backdrop for a broader market downturn.

Anthropic delivered a record-setting IPO, raising approximately $81.3 billion through its public offering and pricing shares at $83 a share, above its initial price range of $70 to $78. The offering was expanded to 980 million shares, reflecting strong investor demand, and valued the Claude developer at approximately $1.5 trillion at the IPO price. The scale of the offering made it the largest stock-market debut on record, surpassing the previous record set by SpaceX.

The enthusiasm was also evident during its first trading session on Nasdaq under the ticker ANT. Shares opened at $111.50, roughly 34% above the IPO price, briefly pushing the company’s market value to around $2 trillion. However, the stock later dropped and closed at $97.17, still 17% above its offering price. The debut marked a major milestone for the AI industry, offering investors their first opportunity to gain direct exposure to one of the leading frontier AI developers through public markets.

Anthropic’s debut did little to reverse the selling pressure across Wall Street, as investors continued to pull back from technology and AI-linked stocks. The S&P 500 fell 0.02% to 7,175.83, marking its sixth consecutive session of losses. The Dow Jones Industrial Average dropped 2.19% to 49,097.06, while the Nasdaq Composite declined 2.25% to 23,879.20. The sell-off extended beyond a handful of AI companies, with declining stocks significantly outnumbering advancing shares across both the NYSE and Nasdaq.

The technology sector also remained under pressure, with Nvidia losing 3.3% despite its upbeat revenue forecast, while Broadcam and Micron each fell 2.1%. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index declined 2.4%, and data-centre infrastructure companies CoreWeave and Nebius dropped 6.1% and 9.4%, respectively. The broader technology sector closed 1.9% lower, reflecting continued unease around AI-related investments.

Bitcoin extended its decline as the sell-off across equities and technology stocks coincided with continued weakness in the cryptocurrency market. BTC dropped below $62,000, touching approximately $61,400 during Friday’s session, marking its lowest level since July. The decline puts Bitcoin roughly 13.5% lower over the past week, with the asset struggling to recover amid weakening risk appetite and continued pressure on institutional flows.

The decline has also coincided with persistent outflows from spot Bitcoin ETFs, which have added to selling pressure. US spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded approximately $306 million in net outflows on November 18, while broader November withdrawals have reportedly reached billions of dollars.

At the same time, traders are reassessing the Federal Reserve’s interest-rate outlook, with expectations of another rate hike strengthening following hawkish signals from policymakers. Higher Treasury yields and a stronger US dollar have further complicated the outlook for risk-sensitive assets, including Bitcoin.

The simultaneous declines across US equities, AI-linked stocks, Asian markets and cryptocurrencies have put the broader risk environment under scrutiny. Investors will now look for signs of stabilisation across technology stocks and semiconductor companies, particularly as markets reassess AI valuations and the scale of capital expenditure supporting the sector.

For Bitcoin, reclaiming the $63,000 level could offer an early indication of whether selling pressure is easing, while further declines could expose additional support levels. Upcoming US economic data, Federal Reserve signals, Treasury yields and movements in the dollar may also influence sentiment across risk assets.

Anthropic’s successful IPO remains a significant milestone for the AI industry, but the broader market reaction highlights that a strong corporate debut alone cannot offset prevailing concerns across financial markets.

#5195. Gold and Silver Wipe Out More Than Bitcoin's Entire Market Cap in One Day

Gold and silver have suffered yet another brutal crash, with approximately $1.4 trillion wiped off their combined market cap in just 24 hours. That's more than the entire market cap of Bitcoin.

Bitcoin, for example, currently has a market cap of roughly $1.26 trillion as of Wednesday, Nov. 18.

Gold saw a brutal drop of roughly 3% on Wednesday, Nov. 18, falling to $3,736 per ounce, its lowest price since September 2025, as per Reuters.

The lustrous metal has now shed about one-third of its value from the record high of nearly $5,595 per ounce hit in January.

Silver experienced an even greater decline of 5.3%, dropping to $44.88 per ounce on Wednesday.

Silver is now down by roughly 63% from its all-time high of over $121 in January. So, the crash is devastating for those investors who jumped on the precious metals bandwagon earlier this year.

Additionally, on Wednesday, Nov. 18, those fears were further exacerbated by Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller suggesting that the central bank may opt for a 50-basis-point rate hike in December or signal a more aggressive tightening program for 2027.

The crash in precious metals has also been accompanied by another major crash in the crypto market.

Bitcoin fell to near $62,000 over the weekend, and it was also underperforming on Nov. 18, dropping to around $62,450 during Wednesday's trading session.

The same macroeconomic factors affecting the prices of gold and silver are also affecting crypto.

Meanwhile, crypto market maker Wintermute warned that Bitcoin could potentially head into the $50,000 range after falling below the 200-week moving average (a key long-term technical indicator).

Renowned economist and longtime Bitcoin critic Peter Schiff has a troubling message regarding the potential fallout from the Federal Reserve's more aggressive approach to fighting inflation.

On Nov. 18, Schiff shared his thoughts on X, suggesting that gold is dropping as people believe the Federal Reserve means business when it comes to fighting inflation.

If the Fed actually delivers on its promise, the financial system could face potentially disastrous consequences, Schiff has warned.

As a result, market participants could see a huge recession that could lead to a financial crisis even worse than the one we saw back in 2008.

#5194. BTC Price Drops 15% as Gold and Silver Dump: Can December’s Fed Decision Turn It?

BTC price fell 15% since Nov 5, more than gold and silver, as a December Fed hike nears 95% odds. Could this shift price action?

Gold (XAU) fell 2.5% to about $3,770 an ounce on Wednesday, November 18, as traders bet on a Federal Reserve rate hike in December. Silver dropped 3.3% to $45.70. Both metals are at their lowest since September 2025. The BTC price held near $64,600, but it has fallen even harder than the metals since the hike bets took off.

Gold prices sink beneath $3,800, hitting a 14-month low as the hawkish Fed outlook and firm dollar weigh on bullion – FX

— First Squawk (@FirstSquawk) November 18, 2026

We compared the moves since November 5, the day before a strong October jobs report changed the rate outlook. Bitcoin is down 15%, silver 12.5% and gold 8.4%. Over the same days, the U.S. dollar index rose 2.2%, and the 10-year Treasury yield climbed from 4.84% to 5.10%.

Higher rates hit everything that pays no interest, and gold, silver and bitcoin all fall into that group. So the next big test is the Fed’s December 8-9 meeting.

Gold peaked near $5,320 an ounce on COMEX futures in late January. It has since lost 29%, and silver has lost 60% from its January high of $115. On Monday, gold for immediate delivery touched $3,832, and Wednesday’s drop pushed it lower still.

The reason is the Fed. Markets now treat another hike as almost certain:

A quarter-point hike would lift the Fed’s target range to 4.00%-4.25%. It would be the second this year, after September’s. Waller has gone further, saying a half-point move, or “a 50-basis-point hike in the policy rate should be considered.”

Central banks are still buying gold, 675 tonnes in the first nine months of the year. That hasn’t been enough to offset a stronger dollar and higher yields.

The BTC price stands at $64,590 on Binance, down 48% from its October 2025 record. On the daily chart, it sits below its 50-day exponential moving average (EMA), an average that gives recent prices more weight, at $75,780, and its 200-day EMA at $75,760.

#5193. Cypherpunk Reports 8,967 ZEC Mined as Zcash Treasury Nears 342,000 Tokens

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Publicly traded Cypherpunk Technologies has reported the first results from its Zcash mining operation, producing 8,967.29 ZEC in just 44 days. The company says those tokens represented approximately $9.9 million of mining revenue in the third quarter, while its treasury later grew to about 341,859 ZEC through purchases and continued production. The figures are impressive in isolation. But investors need to separate operating earnings from accounting gains on an appreciated token reserve, especially after ZEC’s volatile price action.

Cypherpunk’s November 16 filing says the mining fleet began operation on August 18 and delivered about 95% uptime through September 30. The company reported revenue corresponding to 8,967.29 ZEC, with a stated average cost of mining revenue of approximately $104 per token before depreciation. Once depreciation of equipment is included, the company reported a higher per-token cost near $237. The distinction is crucial when comparing mining economics across companies because machines lose value over time even if their near-term power and hosting costs look low.

Cypherpunk says holdings reached around 341,858.75 ZEC after adding October mining output and recent market purchases, equivalent to roughly 2% of the circulating supply at its reporting date. Its strategy therefore combines an operating mining business with a substantial asset inventory. Both components are sensitive to the cryptocurrency’s price. Tokens generated through mining may be held rather than sold to cover expenses, which can expand exposure and affect cash needs. A higher coin count is not the same as higher spendable cash in the company’s bank accounts.

For related context, see our earlier report on the recent Zcash NU7 protocol upgrade.

The Block reported approximately $2.6 million of quarterly operating profit and substantially greater net income driven by unrealized increases in the value of the ZEC treasury. Valuation gains can be economically relevant, but they can also reverse without an operational change. Investors should read the quarterly income statement and cash-flow statement together. A business with rising book profits may still need external financing to buy new equipment or meet operating expenses if it is retaining mined tokens. The source of earnings determines how repeatable they are.

The company says its fleet represented a material part of Zcash network hashrate at launch, while broader network competition has already changed that percentage. Management has discussed additional equipment purchases for 2027, subject to commitments and delivery. More hardware can produce more hashes, but miner returns depend on network difficulty, rewards, uptime, electricity and ZEC’s price. The most useful follow-up will compare actual production and operating cash with capital spent, rather than treating a treasury-size target as proof of long-term mining profitability.

Mining economics can shift even when a company executes its own operating plan well. As competitors add machines, a given fleet's fraction of network hashrate may fall, reducing expected token output at the same energy cost. Rewards also depend on protocol rules and equipment performance, while treasury purchasing introduces separate exposure to spot prices. Reporting those risks alongside current revenue helps readers understand why one profitable early period does not establish a permanent advantage. A full-year record will offer a stronger measure of the fleet's competitiveness.

For related context, see our earlier report on Bitcoin mining-difficulty changes.

Cypherpunk now has measurable mining output as well as a sizable ZEC balance. The challenge for investors is to value those as two connected but distinct businesses, avoiding the temptation to treat unrealized token appreciation as recurring operational income.

Reporting sources: Cypherpunk Q3 financial results, SEC exhibit · The Block coverage

#5192. Gonka AI Hit by $1 Million Exploit as Hacker Drains Stablecoins

Decentralized artificial intelligence (AI) network Gonka AI has reportedly suffered a massive security attack in which hackers exploited its Ethereum bridge to withdraw stablecoins and issue unbacked tokens worth over $1 million.

According to blockchain analytics firm Onchain Lens, the hacker was able to gain access to the bridge's BLS group key, allowing the attacker to withdraw funds and issue Wrapped Gonka (WGNK) tokens without backing them with the required assets.

Per the report, the hacker managed to withdraw $884,800 in Tether (USDT) and USD Coin (USDC) stablecoins and also issued $172,000 in unbacked WGNK tokens.

Combined, the total value of the affected assets stood at around $1.06 million, per Onchain Lens.

Following the hack, the funds were transferred through the decentralized exchange Uniswap and multiple crypto wallets, making it challenging to trace their final destination.

The attack targeted Gonka AI's cross-chain bridge. The cross-chain bridge makes it possible to transfer assets from Gonka AI's native chain to Ethereum and vice versa.

According to the project's technical documentation, withdrawing funds from Gonka to Ethereum requires certain cryptographic signatures from the network's validator set.

The cryptographic signatures are checked against the BLS group key, which changes with every network epoch.

Per the report, the attacker was able to change the epoch BLS group key on the bridge, making it possible for the hacker to control the part of the bridge responsible for authorizing fund withdrawals.

Thus, the hacker was able to withdraw funds from the bridge's stablecoin reserves and issue WGNK tokens without locking the required amount of assets on the native blockchain.

Blockchain security firm Blockaid noted that the hack had taken place, with the total amount of funds stolen reaching about $1.05 million across roughly five transactions at the time of the initial alert.

At the time it spotted the suspicious activity, Blockaid noted that the hack was still ongoing.

Onchain Lens's numbers differ slightly, with the firm reporting around $884,800 in stablecoin withdrawals and $172,000 in unbacked WGNK issuance.

Notably, the issuance of unbacked tokens presents an additional problem, given that such tokens could potentially enter circulation without the necessary reserves to back them.

#5191. Grayscale Says November Crypto Slump Reflects Stablecoin Exits and Tech-Sector Stress

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Grayscale’s latest market note offers a two-part explanation for the sharp decline in crypto prices: money moving out of stablecoins and pressure spilling over from technology equities. Head of research Zach Pandl argues that fundamental adoption trends remain intact, even while Bitcoin and other large assets struggle. That is the assessment of an asset manager with a commercial interest in digital assets, not a settled finding that the sell-off is over. Its value lies in examining the mechanisms rather than repeating the optimistic conclusion as fact.

The November 16 research note points to accelerating withdrawals from stablecoins after the late-October Binance security incident. When investors redeem dollar-backed tokens for bank deposits, the supply of on-chain dollars can contract. That may reduce the liquidity available for trading and collateral, although it does not mean each redeemed dollar was about to buy Bitcoin. Other drivers can change supply, including arbitrage and issuer operations. Grayscale views off-ramping as one contributing factor to the November weakness. Confirming its influence requires tracking issuance, redemptions and market activity across venues.

The firm also identifies pressure in Nvidia and other AI-linked stocks as a possible source of portfolio de-risking. Investors holding growth technology and crypto exposure may sell both when financing conditions worsen or their risk limits tighten. There is a further link through physical infrastructure: some Bitcoin mining firms are developing high-performance computing services for AI operators. Those connections do not make Bitcoin a direct claim on AI company earnings. They suggest overlapping investors and capital requirements through which stress can travel across otherwise different sectors.

For related context, see our earlier report on stablecoin supply contraction.

Grayscale notes that Bitcoin fell below its 200-day moving average on November 12, a level followed by some trend-oriented trading strategies. Leveraged positions and thinner weekend order books can make subsequent price moves more abrupt. Those mechanisms may amplify a decline without identifying a single fundamental cause. Importantly, technical signals are not universal commands: different funds use different models and time horizons, and a moving-average breach does not determine the market’s next direction. The research describes a chain of plausible forces, not a proven one-factor explanation.

Grayscale believes medium- and long-term investors may find current valuations attractive because institutional stablecoin and tokenization activity continues to develop. The note also acknowledges inflation, interest-rate uncertainty and geopolitical risk. Those headwinds could persist longer than optimistic scenarios assume. Readers should distinguish the factual observation of a significant drawdown from the manager’s valuation judgment. ETF flows, stablecoin supplies and changes in rates can help test the thesis, but none offers a reliable standalone timing signal.

Another test of the explanation is whether on-chain capital actually returns when equity-market stress eases. A rebound in technology shares accompanied by ongoing stablecoin redemptions would suggest the factors are not moving in lockstep. In contrast, renewed stablecoin issuance without a broader Bitcoin recovery would show that liquidity availability alone is insufficient. Such cross-checks are more informative than assigning every daily price move to the same narrative. They also allow readers to separate Grayscale's interpretation from the specific market data used to support it.

For related context, see our earlier report on recent bitcoin ETF outflows.

The report provides a framework for thinking about November’s crypto decline, not proof that prices have bottomed. Its strongest contribution is highlighting how stablecoin redemptions, shared growth-stock positioning and leveraged market structure can interact during a difficult month.

Reporting sources: Grayscale November 16 research note

#5190. CZ Sounds Alarm as Japan Orders Crackdown on ID Data Storage

Binance founder Changpeng Zhao, widely known as CZ, has expressed his concerns about the storage of sensitive personal data after Japanese authorities called on rental car services to delete customers' identity documents following a massive data breach.

In his latest X post, CZ pointed to a Nov. 18 notice issued by Japan's Personal Information Protection Commission (PPC). The notice urges rental car services to be cautious about unnecessarily storing images of driver’s licenses and other government-issued identification documents.

The recent call by Japanese authorities comes after a massive cybersecurity attack on a popular Japanese car-sharing service called Times Car, operated by Park24 Group.

The incident began when the company noticed suspicious activity on its system on Sept. 25. As a result, it launched an investigation into whether someone had accessed its systems without permission, potentially exposing customer data.

As per the disclosure by Park24 on Oct. 23, about 6.63 million customer accounts were impacted by the breach.

Worse, the investigation uncovered evidence that hackers had transmitted 1,464,800 encrypted images of identification documents, including driver’s licenses, to external locations.

Besides that, another 891,602 encrypted images of identification documents were accessed suspiciously. However, investigators found no evidence that these additional ID images had been transmitted outside the company’s systems.

Japan’s Personal Information Protection Commission issued its notice on Nov. 18, focusing on the risks of retaining sensitive personal data even after its original purpose has been fulfilled.

The regulatory body stressed that storing unnecessary identification images could significantly worsen the consequences of cyberattacks.

Although Japan’s latest warning specifically targets the rental car industry, CZ’s decision to share his concerns about this issue also raises questions about personal data protection in the crypto industry.

Generally, centralized crypto exchanges require users to go through a Know Your Customer (KYC) process to access their trading platforms.

Depending on the platform and jurisdiction, these processes may require users to provide photos of their passports, driver’s licenses, national ID cards, and other sensitive information.

This kind of sensitive data can be a goldmine for cybercriminals, who can exploit stolen personal information for various activities such as identity theft, phishing attacks, fraudulent registrations, and more

#5189. Binance Hack-Linked Wallet Moves 34,279 ETH, but Investigators See No Confirmed Sale

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

A wallet linked by blockchain investigators to the October 29 Binance breach has transferred 34,279.66 ETH to three newly created addresses, ending a period of inactivity. The ether was worth roughly $63 million at prices used in Tuesday’s reporting. That is a significant movement of suspected stolen funds, but a wallet transfer is not automatically a sale, a successful laundering step or proof that the recipient controls are independent of the sender. Investigators had not identified the new addresses as exchanges at the time of their initial reports.

Monitoring groups MistTrack and Solid Intel reported the transfers on November 17. MistTrack described three movements of approximately 12,000 ETH, 12,000 ETH and 10,279.66 ETH. The receiving wallets were newly created, and the monitored funds had not moved again when the analysts published their findings. Public transaction records make it possible to trace the amounts and destinations, but they do not disclose the human operator behind each address. The link to the earlier breach rests on investigators’ attribution of the sending wallet, not a label embedded in the blockchain itself.

The October attack involved vastly larger losses than Tuesday’s movement. US investigators attributed the incident to a North Korean hacking group, and authorities have announced substantial asset restraints and seizures. Blockchain intelligence firms have also tracked funds moved through different assets and protocols. These parallel developments should not be added together casually, because frozen, seized, recovered and still-controlled amounts describe different legal and operational states. The fresh ETH transfers are one piece of a complex post-incident trail rather than an update to the full net-loss number.

For related context, see our earlier report on Coinbase's custody and security precautions.

Crypto trading commentary sometimes treats any transfer from an exploit-linked wallet as preparation to dump tokens. That can happen, but the evidence here does not yet establish a market sale. Transfers may consolidate funds, redistribute control or precede another operation. Until an address is linked to a trading venue, bridge, mixer or other service, the immediate observable fact is the destination change. Even a later exchange deposit would need careful interpretation: deposits are not identical to executed sell orders. Reporting should preserve those steps rather than collapsing them into one dramatic conclusion.

Ethereum’s public ledger lets investigators monitor known addresses and alert service providers if assets reappear at identifiable points. Token issuers, exchanges and law enforcement may each have different powers depending on the assets and jurisdictions involved. Native ETH cannot simply be frozen by an issuer in the way some centrally administered tokens can. That difference complicates recovery when funds leave services that can intervene. The next useful evidence will be further movements, identification of services receiving the funds and official updates to the case.

Investigators can improve attribution confidence by comparing the sequence, amounts and transaction timing with addresses already associated with the incident. Yet public labels can be incomplete, and a newly created wallet may simply be another address controlled by the same actor. Readers should resist claims that funds have reached a criminal group, a particular country or a cash-out desk unless that identification is supported by additional evidence. Future investigative notices should specify their degree of confidence and distinguish observed transfers from hypotheses about their purpose.

For related context, see our earlier report on the Unocoin wallet compromise.

The latest transfer warrants close monitoring, but it should not be reported as the disposal of $63 million in stolen ether without further proof. The distinction between observable transactions and inferred intent is especially important in a security incident of this scale.

#5188. Anthropic IPO Price Could Top $80 And Pre-IPO Traders Are Copying the SpaceX Script: What Traders Should Expect

The Anthropic IPO may price above $80. Hyperliquid’s pre-IPO contract sits 24% higher, the setup SpaceX had before it listed.

The Anthropic IPO could price above $80 a share on Wednesday evening, above the $70 to $78 range the Claude AI maker set last week, The Wall Street Journal reported on Tuesday. The newspaper cited stronger-than-expected investor demand. Its advisers have discussed a valuation of $1.5 trillion to $1.6 trillion.

The shares are due to start trading on Nasdaq on Thursday, November 19, under the ticker ANT.

⚠️ What if $ANT mirrors $SPCX after the IPO? pic.twitter.com/pMd9aC7KQb

— Rand Group (@randgroup) November 18, 2026

Crypto traders have been betting on the Anthropic IPO for weeks. On Hyperliquid, a pre-IPO contract that tracks one ANT share trades near $99, about 23% above $80.

We have seen this setup before. The night before SpaceX listed in June, the same kind of contract traded 28% above its $135 IPO price. SpaceX then closed its first day 19% up, below what the contract had promised.

So what should traders know before the bell rings on Thursday?

Anthropic launched its roadshow, the week when a company pitches its shares to large investors, on November 10. Its announcement set the terms:

Demand has run ahead of that. Pricing above $80 would lift the valuation toward the $1.5 trillion to $1.6 trillion the advisers discussed. The final price is set after the US market closes on Wednesday, and the shares open on Thursday.

That price is what large investors pay in the Anthropic IPO. Most retail traders will buy at the opening price instead, which can be far higher.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best New Crypto to Hodl

Hyperliquid, a crypto exchange, lists contracts that let traders bet on a company’s share price before it lists. We compared its Anthropic contract with the one it ran for SpaceX, counting back from each listing day.

The SpaceX contract was right that the shares would jump, but too optimistic about how far. If Anthropic followed the same pattern, a 7% gap from Wednesday’s contract price would put ANT’s first close in the low $90s. That is a pattern from one listing, not a forecast.

EXPLORE: Bitcoin Price Tumbles 19% Ahead of Anthropic IPO: Is SpaceX Crash Repeating?

On the hourly chart, Hyperliquid’s ANT contract trades at $98.83, down from $138 when it started trading on October 26.

The number to watch before the Anthropic IPO opens is tonight’s final price. Every dollar above $80 shrinks the contract’s 24% premium, and with it the room for the first-day pop that pre-IPO traders are betting on.

#5187. Can AI Hack Smart Contracts? How AI Agents Are Changing Blockchain Security

Smart contract vulnerability scanners typically work by looking for certain patterns. However, because an AI agent can iterate and make use of dependencies, local blockchain forks, and decompilation, it weakens the protection gained by keeping source code secret, and can attempt attacks against complex vulnerabilities such as access control bugs, accounting errors, oracle-related bugs and unexpected contract interactions.

For example, in December 2025, Anthropic released results from SCONE-bench, a smart contract benchmark of 405 contracts that were exploited in the 2020 - 2025 period. They were able to reproduce exploits for 207 contracts, totalling $550.1 million (simulated stolen funds).

To make sure the agent was not simply memorising exploits that were already published, newer vulnerabilities that had been published after their training cutoff were tested on Claude Opus 4.5, Sonnet 4.5, and GPT-5, which were able to generate $4.6 million (simulated stolen funds).

Finally, they analysed 2,849 recent smart contracts with no known issues, discovering two previously unknown vulnerabilities that could have resulted in $3,694 (simulated stolen funds), emphasising the ability of AI agents to discover new vulnerabilities rather than simply exploiting known ones.

Anthropic emphasised that no actual funds were lost during these experiments. It should also be noted that in one of their experiments using GPT-5, it cost $3,476 for API usage, which leaves a slim (simulated) profit.

With automated hacking becoming more effective, how can we be sure that DeFi will be able to stay ahead of these AI agents? Hacken, a blockchain security firm, reported that $1.18 billion was lost in 107 crypto hacks during the third quarter of 2026. 48% of this was from exploiting smart contracts.

Of course, this doesn’t specify the cause of these hacks. Regardless, it emphasises the importance of being able to stay ahead of AIs which could be used for exploitation.

Chainalysis estimates that approximately $36.7 million was stolen in 2026 from DeFi protocols that had not undergone smart contract verification. It also notes AI-assisted reverse engineering as an emerging risk without attributing any particular thefts to AI.

In an experiment by a16z on 20 past DeFi price manipulation exploits, apparent success rates of 50% were reduced to 10% when the researchers restricted access to transactional data that revealed the original exploit. When the researchers gave the AI some high-level information about the DeFi domain, success rates jumped to 70%.

In February 2026, OpenAI and Paradigm released a benchmark for evaluating agents’ abilities in the domains of vulnerability detection, patching, and exploitation using an EVM environment (EVMbench). Results showed that GPT-5.3-Codex achieved a 71% success rate in exploitation (limited to a sandboxed environment) and 33.3% for GPT-5.

In October 2026, CyberChainBench was released, analyzing 541 exploit events across 9 Ethereum-compatible blockchains. The best-performing configuration achieved 37.5%, 43.7%, and 23.4% success rates for detection, exploitation, and patching respectively.

In a simulation, the best-performing agent profited $57.4 million from exploits on 200 exploit events that occurred after the cutoff. Some of the reported high-severity issues were evaluated and determined to not be exploitable by OpenZeppelin. Since much of the data is trained on in the public, results may be exaggerated.

Agents will continue to improve, and while current results are not an indication that AI-driven agents are responsible for large scale on-chain thefts, it is an indication that AI can find and exploit weaknesses in smart contracts given a certain environment. Given the ease with which agents can try attacking many different protocols, smaller protocols may see an uptick in exploit attempts.

Teams that implement rigorous testing and monitoring could have an edge over other teams. For example, AI could be used to test a contract prior to deployment, generate regression tests, and identify weird behavior on-chain. A human would still need to verify any potential issues and sign off on changes.

Teams should continue to perform third-party audits and implement other security best practices such as limiting access to sensitive information, setting up alerts and response plans, restricting the amount of money in a wallet, making sure AI systems do not have permissions to transact without human oversight where necessary, and so on.

#5186. Cathie Wood's ARK Sells $85 Million in Nvidia, Buys $40 Million in Crypto Stocks

Cathie Wood's ARK Sells $85 Million in Nvidia, Buys $40 Million in Crypto Stocks

Cathie Wood's ARK Invest sold roughly $85 million worth of Nvidia shares Monday while buying close to $40 million in crypto-exposed stocks, rebalancing exposure to technology and fintech stocks during a broad selloff.

The firm's daily trade file for November 16 shows sales of 543,092 Nvidia shares across two actively managed exchange-traded funds. Using Monday's $156.14 closing price, those shares were worth approximately $84.8 million; the disclosures list share counts rather than the exact prices ARK received.

ARK bought 277,523 shares of payments company Block, worth about $23 million at the close. It also added 180,534 shares of stablecoin issuer Circle, valued at $10.4 million, and 53,760 shares of crypto exchange Coinbase, worth around $6.4 million.

The purchases were spread across funds rather than concentrated in one vehicle. ARK's flagship Innovation ETF (ARKK) bought Circle and most of the Block shares, while its Next Generation Internet ETF (ARKW) bought Coinbase and the remaining Block stock.

All three crypto-linked stocks fell Monday. Circle dropped 7.3%, Coinbase declined 6.1% and Block lost 1.9%, while Nvidia slid 4.1% as investors continued selling technology shares.

That makes the whole movement seem like a shift in holdings, not evidence that ARK expects cryptocurrencies to recover before AI stocks. The manager says its funds can rebalance every trading day, and its trade notifications don't disclose why each position was changed or establish that particular sales financed particular purchases.

ARK had bought 90,443 Nvidia shares just one week earlier, on November 9, during another selloff. The latest sale therefore reverses some of that buying without necessarily suggesting the firm has abandoned the chipmaker or its long-standing investment case for artificial intelligence.

ARK also purchased 378,515 shares of SpaceX, worth roughly $52 million, while selling 30,659 shares of crypto exchange Bullish and units of Ethereum and Solana staking funds. So in this case, Wood would be selling crypto to buy one single AI stock.

Those sales included 300,000 shares of the 3iQ Ethereum Staking ETF and 350,000 shares of the 3iQ Solana Staking ETF. The firm's buying of crypto companies coincided with cuts to other crypto-related investments, a distinction that matters when trying to read a trading strategy from one day's activity.

Wood has repeatedly bought companies that handle crypto trading and payments during market selloffs. In September, ARK bought $37 million in Block stock in one session, and it previously increased its Circle and Coinbase stakes while reducing exposure to other crypto names.

The companies offer different ways to invest in digital assets without holding tokens directly. Block runs Cash App and Bitcoin-related products; Circle issues the USDC dollar-backed stablecoin; and Coinbase, the only American crypto exchange listed on the stock market, earns revenue from cryptocurrency trading and other services.

ARK's strategy has never been a simple choice between crypto and AI. Its flagship fund already counted Circle, Coinbase and SpaceX among major positions in August, alongside businesses tied to artificial intelligence and financial technology, as earlier holdings showed.

Nvidia's leadership changed five days before Monday's trades. Co-founder Jensen Huang resigned as CEO on November 11, with finance chief Colette Kress appointed interim chief executive, according to the company's SEC filing.

The chipmaker is due to report fiscal third-quarter results after the market closes Tuesday, with a conference call at 5 p.m. Eastern. Those results will give investors their first earnings update since the leadership change—one day after ARK sold more than half a million shares.

#5185. US Senators Question SEC Tokenized-Stock Exemption Over Wallet Transfers and Investor Protection

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Four Democratic US senators have pressed the Securities and Exchange Commission for answers about how its new approach to tokenized stock trading will protect investors and prevent illicit transfers. Their November 16 letter, reported by The Block, takes aim at gaps they see in the SEC’s five-year innovation exemption for certain trading venues. The development is an oversight challenge, not a court ruling or a notice that the exemption has been cancelled. It goes to a central difficulty for tokenized securities: a regulated purchase can become a blockchain transfer to another address.

The SEC introduced its approach in September, allowing qualifying tokenized-securities venues to operate under a defined exemption from some ordinary exchange-registration requirements. The aim is to permit experimentation with trading stocks in token form while the agency assesses a new market structure. A token representing a security still raises questions about ownership, trading rules and investor rights. The exemptions and their precise conditions matter more than a general statement that securities have moved on-chain. Not every blockchain token, broker or venue automatically qualifies for the same regulatory treatment.

Senators Angela Alsobrooks, Ruben Gallego, Mark Warner and Catherine Cortez Masto reportedly told SEC Chair Paul Atkins that the framework lacks sufficiently specific safeguards around know-your-customer and anti-money-laundering procedures. A buyer verified by a regulated venue might later transfer a token to a wallet whose controller has not undergone the same checks. This problem is not solved merely by verifying the identity of the first purchaser. The letter asks how secondary transfers and the reach of compliance obligations would be managed once tokens circulate beyond the initial venue.

For related context, see our earlier report on Hong Kong's developing licensing rules.

The senators raised concerns about retail-investor protections and pricing, and reportedly asked whether companies could opt out of having their shares tokenized. Those questions are distinct from financial-crime screening. A token that references equity needs a clear relationship to the rights and obligations of the underlying security, as well as fair information about execution and ownership. If an issuer’s wishes, the investor’s expectations and the platform’s legal structure do not line up, technical transferability alone cannot resolve the conflict. Policymakers must address those issues in enforceable terms.

The lawmakers asked the SEC to explain its approach and urged a transparent rulemaking process for changes in the regulatory treatment of tokenized securities. Such a letter can affect oversight and public debate, especially as control of congressional committees changes, but it is not itself binding legislation. Venues exploring tokenized equities should continue to follow the actual exemption terms and any subsequent SEC guidance. The next concrete milestones are the agency’s response, proposed revisions if any, and how early trading models implement identity, custody and transfer restrictions.

For related context, see our earlier report on new institutional crypto-custody arrangements.

Tokenization can change how securities move, but it does not automatically settle which rules should accompany them. The dispute now concerns the design of that regulatory perimeter, with no basis to describe the senators’ letter as an immediate prohibition on tokenized stocks.

Reporting sources: The Block report of four senators’ letter

Dogecoin [DOGE] has been more bark than bite lately.

Last week’s sell-off pushed it down to a new yearly low on the charts and unfortunately, it doesn’t look like it’ll get back up anytime soon.

At the time of writing, Dogecoin was valued at $0.058. This followed a 3.3% dip over the day.

Things really fell apart on 14th November. DOGE fell from $0.065 to $0.057 in a single day. It also briefly hit $0.055, the lowest level of 2026. DOGE’s volume jumped too, implying that too many were selling at once.

Now, while it managed a small bounce to about $0.060 later, it wasn’t able to hold on to that level.

For context, DOGE was above $0.10 in late September. That’s 44% gone… in less than two months.

The RSI was at 28.9, hinting at oversold conditions. It even sank close to 18 during the crash. The MACD was still deep in the red, though the bars were shrinking just a tad.

Simply put, while the sellers seemed to be worn out, buyers are yet to show up.

Finally, leverage has been draining out too, with Open Interest sliding from about $475 million on the 11th to $362.5 at press time.

It is a nervous state of things, and one look at the funding rates will tell you why. It was red during the crash of the 14th, flipped a couple more times, and at press time was back to 0.0052%.

Some traders may be betting on a bounce again, but not in big numbers. Not yet.

ETFs have been even quieter. According to data from SoSoValue, Spot DOGE ETFs haven’t seen a single day of inflows or outflows since mid-October. A whole month of nothing.

Their total net assets still dropped though. We went from $17 million in late September to about $9.74 million at the time of writing. In simple words, Dogecoin’s falling price has been shrinking what’s already there.

#5183. Bitcoin Bounces From $62K: Is Gold and Silver’s 14% Crash Sending Money Into Crypto?

BTC bounced from $62K as silver fell 14% since Nov 9. What does fund trading reveal, and is there really a shift from metals into Bitcoin?

In today’s Bitcoin news, on November 19, silver and gold are extending their sell-off, but Bitcoin is holding its ground near $63,800. BTC has bounced off the $62,000 level for the second time in four days, raising the question of whether crypto’s sell-off is finally losing steam and investors are starting to move money back into digital assets.

Silver futures closed Wednesday at $45 an ounce, down 4.8% on the day and 14% since November 9, while gold fell another 3.2%.

JUST IN: $1,400,000,000,000 wiped out from Gold and Silver in the past 24 hours. pic.twitter.com/NwLlK9b1iv

— Whale Insider (@WhaleInsider) November 19, 2026

For traders following Bitcoin news, the obvious explanation is that money is moving out of metals and into crypto. We tested that by comparing how much money changes hands each day in the three largest US funds for silver, gold, and Bitcoin. If investors were switching, Bitcoin’s slice of that trading would grow.

Well, for now it hasn’t: Bitcoin took 30% of the money traded over the past five trading days, about the same as the 31% it took over the 20 days before.

Bitcoin’s bounce also came with less trading than the sell-off before it, a sign that buyers are still cautious.

So is this the start of a shift from metals to crypto, or just a pause in the selling?

Exchange-traded funds (ETFs) give a clean view of where US money goes, because they trade side by side on the same exchanges. We took the three biggest: the iShares Silver Trust (SLV), SPDR Gold Shares (GLD), and BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT). For each, we multiplied the closing price by the shares traded to get the dollars that changed hands each day.

So the money leaving silver and gold isn’t showing up in Bitcoin funds, at least not yet. It looks more like a broad sell-off in both, with metals now taking the heavier hit.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

Binance’s BTCUSDT pair tells the same Bitcoin news story from the crypto side. During the drop from November 13 to 15, it traded an average of $2.0 billion a day. In the three days since, as Bitcoin held above $62,000, the average fell to $1.5 billion.

Lighter trading on a bounce means fewer buyers are stepping in. Sellers have slowed down, but buyers haven’t rushed back.

Bitcoin’s spot ETFs, which hold the coin itself, show a similar picture. IBIT took in $76.5 million on November 16, its first inflow after 11 days of outflows. That’s a start, but one day of buying after two weeks of selling.

What drives both markets is the same: the US Federal Reserve. Traders expect another rate hike in December, and higher rates hurt assets that pay no interest, from silver to Bitcoin. Until that changes, a rotation from one to the other has little to feed on.

EXPLORE: Bitcoin ETF Inflows Return After Seven-Day Losing Streak as IBIT Adds $76.5M: Is the Sell-Off Finally Over?

On the daily chart, Bitcoin trades at $63,713. That is below its 50-day exponential moving average (EMA), an average that gives recent prices more weight, at $75,245, and its 200-day EMA at $75,624.

The Bitcoin news to watch is volume. A move above $65,500 with trading back near $2 billion a day would be a start. If IBIT’s share of fund trading also climbs above a third, money is actually moving from metals to crypto.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Next Crypto to Hit 1 Dollar

#5182. XRP Flashes Biggest Volatility Warning Among Major Cryptocurrencies

XRP stands out as the cryptocurrency with the most alarming volatility warning among major digital assets, according to recent Coinbase data. The options market indicates that traders are now preparing for price swings that could be nearly double XRP's historical seven-day price move.

XRP's seven-day options-implied price move stands at 1.94 times its historical median. This figure far exceeds similar metrics for major cryptocurrencies such as Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH) and Solana (SOL).

XRP exhibits the highest premium above its own historical volatility. Solana shows the lowest premium among these four cryptocurrencies, Coinbase remarked.

As the chart below indicates, the options-implied seven-day price move for XRP is around 9.6%, according to Glassnode and Coinbase data as of Nov. 17. This compares to its historical median absolute price move of about 4.9%.

Basically, this means that the options market is signaling a price swing nearly twice as large as XRP's typical seven-day price movement.

Interestingly, Solana's implied price move is also close to 9.5%, almost the same as XRP's. Yet its historical median weekly price move is much higher at about 6.5%.

The key here is that although both cryptocurrencies exhibit similarly large potential price moves, the magnitude of the expected volatility surprise is significantly greater for XRP relative to its usual trading range.

For comparison, Bitcoin shows an options-implied seven-day move of about 6%, versus its historical median of about 3.5%. For Ethereum, these numbers are about 8% and 5.1%, respectively.

Obviously, this latest figure doesn't necessarily imply that XRP is going to crash. It's a sign that options traders expect a big price move regardless of its direction.

Implied volatility is a forward-looking measure. It's based on options prices. The higher the implied volatility, the more traders pay for exposure to potential price movements or protection against them.

The chart from Coinbase is based on a one-standard-deviation estimate. It's derived by converting annualized at-the-money implied volatility into a seven-day timeframe.

For XRP, the roughly 9.6% implied move represents a statistical range. It's not a guaranteed price target. A one-standard-deviation range suggests about a 68% chance of the price staying within this range under typical modeling assumptions.

Thus, the latest figure of 1.94x implies that XRP's volatility premium remains high despite a slight decrease from the previous reading.

From XRP traders' perspective, this can be a very challenging market environment. For traders using leverage, there could be a high risk of liquidation due to potentially sharp price moves.

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse said on Nov. 19 that crypto markets could rally after Anthropic's IPO, as they did after SpaceX went public. He spoke to CNBC on the day Anthropic's shares started trading on Nasdaq under the ticker ANT.

According to Garlinghouse, investors sold Bitcoin and XRP before the listing to free up cash for shares of the Claude developer.

"We talked earlier about the most recent sell-off in the crypto markets — we also saw a sell-off very similar when SpaceX went public. What happens is you've got risk-on assets and people are taking liquidity out of a very liquid market, crypto included — Bitcoin, XRP — and moving it into SpaceX. The exact same thing happened coming into the Anthropic IPO. What we saw after the SpaceX IPO was a rally in the crypto markets. I think you'll see that happen here as well," the Ripple CEO said.

Anthropic priced its IPO at $83 per share, above the initial range of $70 to $78. The company and its seven co-founders sold 980 million shares, up from the 825 million shares they planned to sell earlier. The offering raised $81.3 billion, and a $26 billion private placement brought the total to $107.3 billion.

At a valuation of about $1.5 trillion, Anthropic became the world's 12th-biggest company, just behind Tesla. SpaceX raised $75 billion in its market debut in June.

"Thirty years ago, the internet was the next big thing. It has changed our lives. Today, artificial intelligence is the next big thing. It will change our lives, too," Ritter said.

Asked about the listing itself, Garlinghouse said that Anthropic's shares would trade well. "I think it's gonna be extremely well received, and we obviously saw a price above the range they had. I think it's gonna trade very well," he said.

In the same interview, Garlinghouse called blockchain-based transactions "a wave of emerging technology." "I think 10 years from now you'll see blockchain implemented across our financial infrastructure," he said.

After the SpaceX IPO in June, Bitcoin took almost three weeks to turn higher. It started to rise after the U.S. Treasury announced that it would buy back government bonds.

#5178. WSDT introduces prop trading for crypto and tokenized US stocks

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

DUBAI, UAE, November 18, 2026 – WSDT is introducing its prop trading platform for cryptocurrency perpetual futures, gold and tokenized US stocks. Traders can access these markets through a single simulated account, with one-step evaluations starting at a one-time fee of $19 for a $1,500 account.

The platform combines crypto perpetual futures, including Bitcoin and Ethereum, with xStocks instruments tracking companies such as Apple, NVIDIA and Tesla, alongside stock-index ETFs. Perpetual futures are contracts without an expiry date; xStocks track the prices of underlying shares or ETFs. On WSDT, these instruments are traded in a simulated environment, without ownership of the underlying shares or tokens.

Positions across the markets share the same account balance and count toward the same evaluation. Traders can work across crypto and equity-linked instruments in one terminal while managing a single set of account limits.

A WSDT challenge tests trading performance against a 10% profit target, a 4% daily loss limit and a 6% maximum drawdown fixed against the starting account size. Traders must trade on at least four separate days, with no deadline to complete the evaluation. Account sizes range from $1,500 to $200,000, with fees varying by size.

Traders who pass move to a funded-stage account, which is also simulated and retains the loss limits. They receive 80% of eligible profits under WSDT’s Provider Service Agreement. The evaluation fee pays for access to the challenge; it is not a trading deposit.

Registered users can open a free 14-day demo with a $100,000 simulated balance, without providing a payment card or making a deposit. The demo uses the same terminal as challenges, allowing traders to practice before entering an evaluation. Demo results do not qualify for payouts.

WSDT also runs an ongoing weekly competition with free entry. Every participant starts with a $240,000 simulated balance, and the top three finishers receive challenge accounts. A new round opens each Monday at noon New York time. Winners must pass their awarded challenge before progressing to the funded stage.

Registration, account options and full challenge rules are available on the WSDT website.

WSDT provides trader evaluation and educational services across crypto, gold and tokenized equity instruments. Built by TEFS, a prop trading firm established in 2017, it is operated by TEFS Marketing Management L.L.C. in Dubai, UAE. All WSDT trading accounts are simulated.

GTA 6 meme coins are here: DacityCoin hit $18M as the game launched. PumpSwap volume doubled for a day, but meme coins and PUMP stay weak.

DacityCoin (DCTY) hit an $18 million market cap, the day Rockstar Games released Grand Theft Auto VI. But what is DacityCoin? Traders were racing to find GTA 6 meme coins before the game had even finished downloading.

The Solana meme is named after a fake cryptocurrency inside the game. Life imitating GTA, you could say. DCTY traded near $0.019 at 11:00 UTC, up more than 150x in 24 hours.

What the fuck? pic.twitter.com/cWJV4vGKM2

DacityCoin’s fomo doubled PumpSwap’s daily volume on Wednesday, yet the exchange was busier than it was on 12 days since October 1. Dogecoin, Pepe and Bonk barely blinked, and Pump.fun’s own token, PUMP, is sitting near a one-month low.

So is this the spark for a new run in meme coins, or just a fun one-day side quest?

EXPLORE: Bitcoin Bounces From $62K: Is Gold and Silver’s 14% Crash Sending Money Into Crypto?

DacityCoin started as a background joke. In Rockstar’s launch trailer, the name is painted on a stunt plane. Then this week, gameplay footage from Brazilian streamer Combo Infinito showed an in-game phone app listing “DacityCoin (DCTY)”, complete with buy and sell buttons.

Crypto traders did what crypto traders do: within hours, the Solana token with the same ticker went vertical.

Before the footage, DCTY had never been worth more than about $54,000. Here’s what its main pool on DEX Screener shows now:

One important detail: Rockstar hasn’t linked the in-game coin to any real token, and DCTY has no official tie to GTA 6 or Take-Two. The name game is getting messy, too.

A separate Pump.fun token called “Dacity Coin” hit a $10.8 million market value on Wednesday, then slid to about $800,000. Exchange Bitrue joined in as well, listing DacityCoin futures with up to 10x leverage on Wednesday.

If a fresh meme coin season were kicking off, Pump.fun is where you’d see it first, since that’s where most new Solana tokens of this kind are born. So we pulled PumpSwap’s daily trading from DefiLlama, which tracks exchange data across blockchains.

Outside the GTA hype, the bigger meme coins are having a rough year. All eight large meme coins we tracked on Binance are down for 2026, by a median of 54%, while Bitcoin is down 28%. The past week stung too, with drops between 11% and 23%:

Source: Binance daily closes, Coinspeaker calculation, November 19.

Zoom out, and DCTY is still tiny. The whole meme coin sector is worth $22.5 billion on CoinGecko, with Solana’s share at only $2 billion. One token can steal the spotlight for a day, but a real run for meme coins needs the big names and overall trading to rise together. So far, that hasn’t happened.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Top Solana Meme Coins to Bid

Pump.fun did get a payday out of the DacityCoin rush. The platform earned $1.62 million in revenue on Wednesday, up from a $1.28 million average over the five days before. Since April, Pump.fun says it spends half its revenue buying PUMP on the open market, so a day like Wednesday means roughly $800,000 of buying.

The worst day was November 14, when PUMP dropped 15%. Token trackers list a scheduled release of locked PUMP tokens in mid-November, though they don’t agree on the exact date. So far, buybacks of under $1 million a day haven’t been enough to soak up that selling.

GTA 6 meme coins are hot now, but the thing to watch is breadth, meaning how many coins move together. If PumpSwap volume stays above $300 million a day after launch week and Dogecoin or Pepe join in, DacityCoin could be the start of something bigger.

If DCTY fades and volume slips back to $150 million, it’ll go down as a fun launch-day story and not much more.

#5176. Bitcoin Investors Are Capitulating—That’s Historically Been a Bottom Signal

Bitcoin Investors Are Capitulating—That’s Historically Been a Bottom Signal

Bitcoin’s latest crash has pushed investors into the kind of loss-taking that has historically shown up near market bottoms.

Realized losses, which investors lock in when they sell coins for less than they paid, have climbed relative to the profits taken over the past year.

That measure now sits at levels seen only near the lows of 2018, 2020, 2022 and early 2023, according to a report published Tuesday by onchain analytics firm Glassnode. It’s the first such reading since then.

Most of the selling came from recent buyers. Holders of coins less than six months old took losses into early 2026, cashed in gains during the rally into September and October, and are now selling at a loss again, Glassnode said. Leverage has been flushed too, with Nov. 13 and 14 bringing some of the largest long liquidations relative to open interest since 2023.

Bitcoin fell to about $62,000 on Nov. 15 before bouncing, and it traded near $64,970 on Tuesday. More than $2 billion in liquidations over the weekend left traders watching $60,000 as a potential next stop, Decrypt reported. The slide deepened last week as fears of further Federal Reserve rate hikes erased Bitcoin’s summer rally. Rising interest rates generally do not bode well for risk assets like Bitcoin, since investors typically rotate to safer bets like U.S. Treasuries when rates elevate.

But there are early signs of support, according to Glassnode. Buyers absorbed about 66,000 BTC in the $62,000 to $66,000 range during the two days of heaviest selling, adding to a zone where recent buyers accumulated around the August low.

Resting bids on Binance and Coinbase within 5% of the price now outweigh asks by about 2.3 to 1. The Coinbase Premium, which compares Bitcoin’s price on Coinbase with global markets, briefly turned positive at the low as U.S. buyers stepped in.

Still, Glassnode stopped short of calling a bottom. Broad demand remains thin: stablecoin supply has shrunk 4.2% over 90 days, and the Coinbase Premium has slipped back to a small discount.

Meanwhile, U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs extended their outflow streak to eight sessions on Monday and have bled about $2.2 billion so far in November, according to Decrypt’s ETF tracker.

That makes the $62,000 to $66,000 range the line to watch. Short-term holders bought very few coins below $62,000, and a large cluster of leveraged long positions sits just beneath it, between about $59,500 and $62,200.

A daily close below $62,000 could trigger a fast drop toward the June low of $58,500 and Bitcoin’s realized price of $53,700, the average price at which every coin last moved, Glassnode said.

On the upside, a move back above $74,100, the average cost basis of short-term holders, would confirm that a bottom is forming, according to the firm. Options traders on Deribit, meanwhile, price roughly a one-in-three chance that Bitcoin settles below $53,700 at the September 2027 expiry.

The Fed’s next rate decision is Dec. 9, a day before November inflation data is released.

#5175. Bitcoin (BTC) Demand Hits 2026 Low as Price Retests $62,000

Bitcoin is facing increased fear and panic among traders as its price volatility continues to intensify, causing both retail and institutional investors to move with caution.

While Bitcoin is still struggling to regain momentum amid the persistent market downturn, the latest on-chain data from CryptoQuant shows that Bitcoin's demand has dropped to its weakest level in 2026.

The data shows that Bitcoin's apparent demand is rapidly showing signs of weakness, with the metric dropping below -290,000 BTC before seeing a brief recovery to about -255,000 BTC as of November 18.

However, this does not provide any relief, as Bitcoin’s apparent demand remains substantially below its monthly average of around -172,000 BTC.

As such, it appears that investors’ interest in accumulating Bitcoin remains significantly subdued by the fear of potential losses. Hence, investors are growing increasingly cautious about entering the market amid the rising selling pressure.

The data shows that investors are growing increasingly wary of their Bitcoin positions amid growing uncertainty about its potential price movements, as Bitcoin’s price downturn has only intensified over the past few days.

While the asset’s price has dipped further, Bitcoin has suddenly retested $62,000 as of November 18, a price level last seen during the severe price crash in July.

Nonetheless, further data shows that it has briefly recovered to $63,817.43 as of the time of writing, showing a decline of about 1.12% over the past 24 hours.

Despite Bitcoin’s extremely negative demand and weak price movements, market analysts are still positive about a potential reversal, describing the moment as an opportunity to buy at a lower price.

It has been previously observed that while many investors are becoming increasingly bearish, periods of widespread fear can sometimes flush out weaker hands, thereby triggering a change in market direction.

Mutsamudu, Comoros, November 19, 2026 – MEXC, a pioneer in 0-fee digital asset trading, has released its November 2026 Proof of Reserves (PoR) report, independently audited by Hacken. The report shows MEXC’s solid reserve position, with user assets in every major asset backed by reserves above 1:1, demonstrating MEXC’s ongoing commitment to safeguarding user assets.

Spanning Proof of Liabilities, Proof of Ownership, Reserves Calculation, and Merkle Tree verification, Hacken’s November 2026 audit confirms substantial over-collateralisation across core holdings, standing at 318% for BTC, 122% for USDT, 112% for ETH, and 110% for USDC. Guided by its user-first philosophy, MEXC continues to enhance transparency to protect user assets and build trust. #5173. CoinShares sells $19.3M in ETH and SOL – What’s behind the move?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

CoinShares has revealed that in October, it sold its entire Ethereum [ETH] treasury position and half of its Solana [SOL] holdings.

The European digital asset manager’s ETH sale brought in $16.6 million, and its SOL sale added another $2.7 million. That’s $19.3 million put together, and none of it is going back to crypto.

Instead, CoinShares is using it to buy back its own stock.

On the 22nd of October, the board cleared up to $40 million for buybacks. This one sale covers close to half of that budget.

The company shared the details in its Q3 results this week. CEO Jean-Maria Mognetti described the sale as a way to keep cash on hand, so CoinShares can step in and buy when the moment is right.

…nearly half of the $40 million authorized by the Board and gives us the flexibility to act when conditions allow.

Revenue at $27.7 million is about 45% lower than a year ago. What rescued the quarter was the company’s own crypto. Holdings gained $14.4 million in value; it was a nice turnaround from the $5.4 million hit it took in Q2.

However, there were some bright spots. Lending and trading picked up, so capital markets income went up to $17.8 million. That’s nearly 4x the previous quarter! Operating income also bounced back after a loss in Q2.

Zooming out, though, makes things appear weaker. EBITDA fell 61.4% YoY to $20.6 million. Notably, net income for the first nine months was $15.6 million, compared with $108.8 million at this point last year.

Money kept coming in, even if fees didn’t show it.

CoinShares’ investment products had inflows worth $125 million in Q3. Management fees stayed flat at $19.1 million, since crypto prices were sluggish until late September.

They launched a Bitcoin mining ETF for European institutions in July and bought Quant Manager Bastion in September.

December’s the next big step, though. CoinShares is set to join nine Russell indices, including the Russell 2000. Funds worth about $789.9 billion track these indices. If confirmed, the change takes effect after markets close on the 11th of December.
