#5172. 17 Years Ago, Satoshi Nakamoto Opens His BitcoinTalk Account: What Happened Next

On this day, 17 years ago, Satoshi Nakamoto opened an account on BitcoinTalk, the place that would become the home of early Bitcoin enthusiasts and then turn into a public forum for crypto in general, until social media platforms took over the internet.

The network itself was barely 1 year old, and its future raised more questions than answers. Satoshi Nakamoto welcomed users on the forum and invited any questions people had regarding the cryptocurrency.

In 2010, the revolution began: 10,000 BTC was used to buy two pizzas, one of the first uses of the digital asset in exchange for a commodity. In May, Laszlo Hanyecz probably had the most expensive pizza dinner in history, considering how much BTC rallied after that. By December, Satoshi made their final BitcoinTalk post and announced their farewell.

The 2011-2014 period turned out to be the foundation for the network as a whole. Bitcoin became an open-source project maintained by others. The first halving happened in November 2012 and reduced the block issuance from 50 to 25 BTC per block.

The first-ever exchange implosion, Mt.Gox in 2014, raised serious questions about the security and stability of centralized exchanges, an issue we haven't solved 17 years later.

2017-2021 were the years when the industry truly blossomed. We had our first bull run on Bitcoin, which pushed the price above expected levels, to almost $20,000 in December 2017, along with the first altcoin bull run and the birth of new technologies like smart contracts, NFTs and many more. The next cycle took Bitcoin to around $69,000 in November 2021.

However, with the growth of the industry, multiple conflicts arose around safety, integrity and usage. The first country in the world to adopt Bitcoin was El Salvador, which implemented the digital currency as a true means of payment, in addition to accumulating it in its treasury.

2022 was the pivotal year, marked by the FTX and Terra crashes that changed how investors treat markets in general. FTX wasn't just an exchange: it was the backbone of the cryptocurrency market in those years. It aimed to rival Binance and become the bridge between institutional investors and crypto.

However, poor risk management, issues with fund allocation and many other factors caused an implosion that ended the bull run.

In 2024, Wall Street arrived in crypto with ETFs, ETPs, perpetual futures and a regulatory framework. The digital assets industry became a part of a larger market. Whether that is good or bad is a question to consider, but one thing is clear: Bitcoin has come a very long way since 2010 and is evolving to this day.

The next step is the arrival of AI and rising cybersecurity risks, which go toe-to-toe with the quantum danger. How the first cryptocurrency handles them is a question that directly affects its future.

#5171. OmenX announces $2 million in funding from Informed Ventures and launches on-demand 30-second Bitcoin predictions

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Toronto, November 19, 2026 – OmenX, a Base-native leveraged prediction market platform, today announced $2 million in funding from Informed Ventures alongside the launch of its on-demand, 30-second Bitcoin price prediction product.

The new product lets users predict whether Bitcoin’s price will rise or fall over the next 30 seconds, with each trade starting its own prediction window. A position opened at 10:00:12 runs until 10:00:42. Users can act when their view forms, with a full 30-second horizon, rather than adapting to the remaining time in a shared market round.

Featuring zero-slippage execution and rapid settlement, the product extends the familiar five- and fifteen-minute crypto prediction format to a shorter, individually timed experience. It adds to OmenX’s intraday offering, which spans prediction horizons from 30 seconds to 24 hours, depending on the market.

Beyond crypto, OmenX offers directional prediction markets linked to more than 100 global assets, including U.S. equities, futures, ETFs and indices. Its event markets also cover live sports and esports, with sports coverage spanning major football leagues, basketball, American football, ice hockey, baseball and UFC.

Leverage has been central to OmenX’s platform from the outset, allowing traders to amplify their exposure on supported event markets. The latest launch builds on that foundation while advancing a broader goal: making prediction markets a practical financial tool for retail users.

OmenX organizes the trading decision around a defined question: will an asset’s price be higher or lower at the end of a specified period? Users can express a directional view without purchasing the underlying asset, targeting an exact closing price or constructing an options position that requires decisions about strikes, implied volatility and time decay.

“Leverage has been part of OmenX from the start, but our ambition goes beyond adding leverage to prediction markets,” said James, founder and CEO of OmenX. “We want to make global markets easier to participate in. A trader should be able to take a view on Bitcoin, a stock or a sporting event and express it through a clear contract and a timeframe that fits their judgment.”

The platform’s emphasis on short, recurring markets provides faster feedback on trading decisions and can reduce the time capital remains committed to individual outcomes. OmenX plans to expand its coverage of traditional and crypto assets while deepening the trading experience across supported markets.

OmenX is a Base-native leveraged prediction market platform covering crypto, global financial assets, live sports and esports. Through defined outcomes, short settlement cycles and leverage on supported markets, OmenX is building a more accessible way to trade market views.

Learn more at omenx.com. Media contact James Zhang

#5170. Top 5 presales to look out for in November 2026

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Presales are an important part of the crypto space, and for any investors or users looking to become a part of the ecosystem beyond trading, presales provide an opportunity to access emerging projects before exchange listings.

Here is a list of the top 5 crypto presales for the month of November 2026:

Bitcoin Hyper intends to expand Bitcoin’s capabilities through a Layer 2 network by incorporating the Solana Virtual Machine technology. This helps to execute faster transactions, lower fees, and provide support for smart contracts.

It also uses a canonical bridge and ZK proofs/rollups to let users move BTC into wrapped equivalents on the Layer-2 network and anchor transactions back to Bitcoin for settlement. The presale for HYPER is currently ongoing and is said to be extended until Q2 2027.

A Layer 3 blockchain that is the first-ever unified execution layer linking the world’s largest blockchains, LiquidChain combines Bitcoin’s capital with Solana’s speed and Ethereum’s DeFi depth. It gives its users access to combined liquidity pools across all major blockchains to provide better prices, deeper pools, and faster execution.

$LIQUID token is the native token of the ecosystem and can be useful for governance, staking, network participation, and accessing exclusive features within the platform. Its presale is currently ongoing and its interoperability gives an important solution to the DeFi infrastructure problem.

BMIC is another interesting project in the crypto space that is related to quantum computing and the future security of digital assets. It intends to build cryptocurrency wallets and other financial infrastructure with the help of post-quantum cryptography.

BMC aims to work towards the idea that, by developing a wallet infrastructure that uses newer cryptographic standards, it can create a quantum-resistant digital asset ecosystem. BMIC’s presale is currently live.

Maxi Doge is a meme-inspired cryptocurrency project that has gained a lot of attention due to its branding, social media momentum, and community engagement. It combines the familiar Doge-inspired branding with bodybuilding culture and speculative trading memes.

It also represents high-risk humor trading and community-driven competitions with the $MAXI token being at the center of this ecosystem. It is a community-driven project that does not really have any conventional revenue-generating business model.

Divine Ray connects blockchain technology, social media, and the creator economy into one ecosystem. $DRC is creating a social platform where creators, educators, wellness communities, and individuals can interact, monetize content, and take part in a blockchain-powered digital economy.

It also operates a Cosmos SDK blockchain with IBC interoperability. The $DRC token is useful for staking, governance, creator rewards, in-app advertising, NFT mining, premium memberships, and event listings. The presale is currently live, and traders can invest in the token.

Presales are a great way for traders to diversify their investments and become a part of the cryptosphere. They bridge the gap between the company and its customers by helping the company understand the customer’s requirements and present the right use cases. It is important for users to do their own research before investing.

#5169. Coinbase Data Shows XRP Traders Pricing In Unusually Big Move

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Major crypto exchange Coinbase shared the options-implied seven-day ratios for major cryptocurrencies, highlighting that options traders might be preparing for an unusually large price move for XRP.

In an X post, Coinbase Markets shared the outcome of dividing the options-implied 7-day move by the median 7-day move for XRP, Bitcoin, Ethereum, and Solana.

Options-implied 7d move, divided by each asset's own median 7d move: $XRP 1.94× $BTC 1.69× $ETH 1.56× $SOL 1.46× $XRP is priced furthest above its own history, $SOL the least. Which one is asking for more than its own history delivered? https://t.co/WgWbqjcgwI

This suggests how much movement options markets are pricing in relative to each asset's own historical behavior. However, it does not compare their expected dollar or percentage moves directly.

According to the figures shared by Coinbase, XRP's options-implied seven-day move stands at 1.94 times its own historical median seven-day move. Bitcoin follows at 1.69 times, while Ethereum records 1.56 times and Solana comes in at 1.46 times.

XRP, therefore has the highest relative reading among the four assets, suggesting options traders are pricing in a more pronounced move than the token has typically delivered over a comparable period.

At the time of writing, XRP was down 1.93% in the last 24 hours to $0.98 amid the continued profit-taking in the market. XRP briefly reached $1 on November 18, 2026, but fell afterwards as traders took profits. XRP is down 21.57% so far in November, but its history says more than its current price action does.

XRP’s average November gain is skewed upward by massive outlier rallies—specifically +550% in 2013 and +282% in 2024. Its median November return is about -10%, meaning it has closed lower in most cycles in history. XRP dropped roughly 23.42% in November 2019 and lost 12.18% in November 2025 (opening at around $2.51 and closing at around $2.20).

A weak November doesn’t always mean a weak December: for instance, after falling 9.1% in November 2015, XRP jumped 46.8% in December 2015.

In the near term, XRP’s price recovery will target $1 before moving to the $1.24–$1.26 range. Alternatively, if the price falls further, it is likely to find support at $0.89.

#5168. World Bank Taps Circle and Citi to Test Stablecoins for Development Payments

World Bank Taps Circle and Citi to Test Stablecoins for Development Payments

The World Bank is turning to stablecoins to modernize how it moves development money, announcing Tuesday it will explore using Circle’s USDC and blockchain-based payment rails to speed up and cut the cost of disbursing funds around the world.

The institution said it will work with Citi and Circle to test how dollar-denominated, regulated stablecoins could operate alongside or improve its existing funds-distribution system, which runs on correspondent banking, the slow and costly network of intermediary banks that routes international payments.

Under the arrangement, Citi would supply its connections to established banking rails while Circle provides the infrastructure to mint and settle funds in USDC.

The logic is that even small efficiency gains, applied across the vast sums the World Bank moves, could free up meaningful resources for development. “This initiative is about exploring ways to make the financial plumbing of development work better for the people who need it most,” said World Bank Group President Ajay Banga, arguing that faster, cheaper money movement means “a health clinic gets built on time, a farmer gets paid before the harvest season ends.”

The effort builds on FundsChain, a World Bank blockchain platform designed to make fund disbursements more traceable. More than 350 projects across 96 countries are already onboarded to its digital ledgers, letting everyone involved in a project see how money is being spent.

The stablecoin exploration would focus initially on an intermediated, interoperable model, limited to borrower countries where digital assets are permitted, with plans to involve other banks over time.

Circle CEO Jeremy Allaire framed development finance as one of the most important applications for his company’s technology, saying money that moves “at the speed of the internet shouldn’t be reserved for the world’s biggest banks and corporations.”

Today, the @WorldBankGroup announced work with @circle and @Citi to bring the benefits of USDC to global funds distribution. A big step towards a more efficient and inclusive financial system.https://t.co/hq436tA5IR

— Jeremy Allaire (@jerallaire) November 17, 2026

Citi’s Shahmir Khaliq said the collaboration would deliver “24/7 capabilities at scale so that critical funds move in real time.”

The move hands the stablecoin industry a powerful institutional validator, and it fits a broader wave of traditional finance embracing the technology.

Wall Street is currently racing to claim the onchain dollar, with Goldman Sachs, Citi and other banks committing roughly $270 million to a joint stablecoin venture, while Deutsche Bank recently went live with crypto custody after a five-year buildout.

For Circle, whose USDC is the second-largest stablecoin, a role in the World Bank’s payment plumbing marks a notable step toward mainstream legitimacy, even as the broader crypto market endures a rough stretch of ETF outflows and falling prices.

#5167. Hyperliquid Hits Hong Kong Alert List as HYPE Erases 5% Drop

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

On Nov. 18, Hong Kong's Securities and Futures Commission (SFC) added the decentralized exchange Hyperliquid to its list of suspicious virtual asset trading platforms. The listing covers the web interface of the Hyperliquid trading terminal at app.hyperliquid.xyz/trade. The HYPE token dropped to around $65.50 on the evening of Nov. 18, and it recovered to $68.79 by midday Nov. 19, according to TradingView data.

According to the entry, the company claims to provide digital asset trading services on the website, and it has no address on record. Hyperliquid is not licensed by the SFC, and it is suspected of operating a virtual asset trading platform in Hong Kong or actively promoting its services to the public, the regulator said.

In an accompanying warning, the SFC reminded investors that such platforms often lure users with "incredible investment opportunities." The SFC also reminded investors that clients of unlicensed platforms have no legal protection if a platform is hacked or misappropriates their assets.

On the same day, the Hyperliquid Policy Center (HPC) sent a letter to the U.K. Financial Conduct Authority with seven recommendations on the country's new crypto rules. "Regulatory obligations belong with persons who have unilateral control over a function that is a regulated activity," the group wrote.

On Nov. 16, two days before the SFC added Hyperliquid to the list, Hong Kong Financial Secretary Paul Chan spoke at the Bloomberg Global Regulatory Forum. "Before the end of this year, we will introduce a bill to complete the regulatory perimeter," Chan said.

According to Chan, the bill will cover crypto dealers, custodians, advisers and asset managers. "Decentralisation does not mean less accountability," he said.

In April, the Hong Kong Monetary Authority licensed HSBC and Anchorpoint Financial as the first stablecoin issuers in the city, out of 36 applicants in the first round.

Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. The 86 trillion threshold has been a sinister cloud over Shiba Inu. For now, it might take a step back, thanks to decreasing selling pressure on the market according to CryptoQuant. The token's technical state isn't great, unfortunately, as it's struggling to find fuel for a recovery.

Shiba Inu printed only 67 billion in exchange inflows, which is a 67% decrease from the local peak of 250 billion. The decreasing exchange inflows are a signal of decreasing selling pressure, even though the available supply on exchanges is still enormous.

According to CryptoQuant, exchange reserves currently sit at around 85.74 trillion SHIB, worth approximately $332.68 million. They're still up by 0.04% over the last 24 hours.

The real question here is obviously the recovery potential: a decrease in selling pressure quite often precedes recoveries, but it's important to keep in mind that SHIB has barely found the fuel to decelerate the bearish trend, let alone retrace in price.

SHIB's price is stagnant at around $0.000004 and is unlikely to find a footing for a move up anytime soon.

The RSI dropped into the oversold zone on the day of that drop and hasn't climbed far from it since. Momentum remains bearish: SHIB's RSI is at 25, a clear oversold signal, even though there's barely any buying volume on the market.

#5163. Bitcoin miner fees hit a 15-year low – Why profit margins are shrinking

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

As 2026 is nearing its end, Bitcoin [BTC] miners are facing growing financial pressure. This, in turn has resulted in miners increasingly selling their Bitcoin reserves instead of holding them for long-term appreciation.

According to Glassnode’s recent update, publicly listed Bitcoin miners have sold nearly half of their Bitcoin holdings since January 2026.

This means that miners are increasingly treating their Bitcoin reserves as a source of liquidity to fund business operations and maybe for expansion into AI data centers.

At the same time, there is also a decline in transaction fee revenue. With 11 months into 2026, Bitcoin miners earned just 912 BTC in transaction fees, the lowest annual total since at least 2011. This is a huge drop compared to 1,720 BTC in 2025 and 15,178 BTC in 2024.

In other words, transaction fees accounted for only 0.63% of total miner revenue in 2026, down from 1% in 2025 and 6.5% in 2023. This is enough proof that demand for Bitcoin block space has weakened, reducing the contribution of this revenue source.

Remarking on the same, CleanSpark CBO Harry Sudock said,

Like with any commodity production business, it should get harder and thinner margin over time because the lowest cost operators should be the most successful and durable.

In other words, Sudock believes Bitcoin mining will become increasingly competitive over the next decade, with shrinking profit margins favoring operators with the lowest production and electricity costs.

He expects the global hashrate to continue growing, but at a slower pace than in the previous ten years, as mining hardware becomes more efficient and large publicly listed miners explore AI data centers.

He also argued that Bitcoin mining will not depend solely on large public companies, as smaller businesses and privately held miners can operate profitably.

At the same time, CryptoQuant CEO Ki Young Ju noted that Bitcoin’s average mining cost reached approximately $62,862 in Q3 2026, based on Marathon Digital’s filings. With BTC trading near this level, miners are facing shrinking profit margins and could face potential losses if prices fall further.

However, the relatively high hashrate suggests miners remain committed to the network despite growing profitability concerns.

This comes as Bitcoin miners are shifting their focus towards AI and high-performance computing (HPC), as investors increasingly value their power infrastructure and data centers.

Companies such as Core Scientific, IREN, and TeraWulf are generating significant revenue from AI infrastructure and HPC services, in some cases surpassing their Bitcoin mining income.

However, the Miner Outflow chart suggests that compared with the rest of the year, November outflows are calm.

At the same time, the Miner Reserve chart was at about 1.1925 million BTC at press time. In fact, through October and into November, it moved in a narrow band, roughly between 1.192 million and 1.1926 million.

All in all, miners are roughly holding steady, with small accumulation or lower net selling.

Binance will delist five crypto spot trading pairs within the next 24 hours on Nov. 20, 2026, following a review.

To protect its users and to ensure a high-quality trading market, Binance conducts regular reviews of all listed spot trading pairs; it may delist certain spot trading pairs for various reasons, including low liquidity and trading volume.

Binance will delist GAS/TRY, HBAR/BNB, HIVE/TRY, KSM/TRY and ZAMA/TRY pairs on November 20, 2026 at 03:00 UTC, according to an official announcement published on November 18.

Four of the five affected pairs are related to the Turkish lira (TRY), while the last one is HBAR/BNB. The decision affects trading between these specific assets and does not mean the cryptocurrencies themselves are being completely delisted from Binance.

The exchange clarified that users will still be able to trade the affected tokens through other available spot trading pairs on its platform.

Binance will terminate Spot Trading Bots services for the spot trading pairs simultaneously where applicable. Users are strongly advised to update and/or cancel their Spot Trading Bots prior to the cessation of services for Spot Trading Bots to avoid any potential losses.

In related news, Binance Margin will initiate a routine cleanup of Cross Margin accounts with collateral valued at less than 1 USD (or equivalent in other assets), effective from November 30, 2026 at 00:00 (UTC). The cleanup will be executed on a daily basis as an ongoing operational process.

Affected accounts include Cross Margin Accounts with a collateral value of less than 1 USD equivalent across all assets and no outstanding liabilities, as well as Margin Accounts with no capital activity in the past 6 months. Capital activity refers to any action that generates a capital flow.

For each eligible Small-Asset Account, the system will automatically transfer the collateral in users’ Margin Accounts to their Spot Accounts while the Margin Account functions remain normal, allowing users to transfer, borrow, or trade at any time after the cleanup.

Accounts with collateral equal to or exceeding 1 USD equivalent are not affected by this cleanup.

#5160. OpenAI's GPT 6.2 Astra Challenges Anthropic on Price, But Not Every Benchmark

OpenAI's GPT 6.2 Astra Challenges Anthropic on Price, But Not Every Benchmark

OpenAI launched GPT-6.2 Astra and Sol on Tuesday, updating its flagship and everyday AI models with better professional output, cheaper processing and stronger abilities to operate software on their own.

Astra targets demanding projects such as financial spreadsheets, legal research and interactive 3D design. Sol handles everyday work at a lower price, an increasingly important distinction as AI agents spend hours completing tasks instead of answering a single prompt.

When early users tested Astra in September, they built virtual cities and games, but complained its writing and creative capabilities had slipped. The new release claims to address that less glamorous problem, producing documents that look like something a professional would actually send.

Astra costs $8 per million input tokens and $40 per million output tokens through OpenAI's API, down 20% from the original model. Sol retains its $2 and $10 rates. Tokens are the chunks of text models process and generate, so fewer tokens can mean a smaller bill even when the listed rates stay the same.

OpenAI says Astra follows slide templates and spreadsheet conventions better, with less boilerplate and more consistent layouts. Those are company claims, not guarantees for every assignment.

Independent testing by Artificial Analysis puts Astra at 62 on its Intelligence Index, just a bit behind Anthropic's Claude Fable 5.2 at 65. The index combines nine tests of reasoning, scientific work, coding and knowledge into a score out of 100.

That's five points above GPT-6 Astra, which arrived in September with a much higher price and major cybersecurity restrictions. Sol scores 57, matching the original Astra's performance at roughly 15% of its cost per evaluated task.

Those mixed results follow Anthropic's Fable 5.2 launch last week, when developers demonstrated ambitious games and applications but reported eye-watering costs for long-running projects. Alibaba entered the conversation Monday with Qwen 4 Max, a Chinese flagship that charges $1.50 per million input tokens and $4.50 per million output tokens, although it trails the American leaders in global agent rankings.

OpenAI says Astra is its first externally released model to reach the High threshold for AI self-improvement, a safety classification measuring how much it can accelerate AI research. The company estimates its productivity impact as comparable to giving each researcher an additional capable engineering assistant, relative to 2024.

Both new models also meet OpenAI's Critical cybersecurity threshold. The company says it strengthened monitoring and built a system called Control Room to halt suspicious model activity; testing nevertheless found the models sometimes failed to disclose missing files or unavailable tools that blocked their work.

OpenAI says the models are available through ChatGPT Work and Codex for Plus, Pro, Enterprise and Edu users, and through the API for developers, but our GPT Plus subscription still doesn’t show those models, so this may be a slow rollout.

OpenAI has not yet added GPT-6.2 to its Daybreak program for vetted cybersecurity researchers.

#5159. PEPE’s Small Bounce Leaves Horizontal Support in Focus

PEPE’s price hangs below major thresholds: traders now got their eyes peeled on this accumulation zone.

PEPE coin has staged a modest rebound, but the move has done little to settle the larger question hanging over the memecoin: whether buyers can defend the $0.00000383 support area.

The meme token’s recovery has offered a brief reprieve after weakness, yet a small price uptick alone does not establish a durable reversal.

For now, the market’s attention is fixed less on the size of the bounce than on whether PEPE coin can hold above the level that has become its immediate technical line of defense.

Memecoins often produce sharp short-term moves that can fade quickly when broader risk appetite remains uncertain. PEPE’s latest recovery may indicate that some buyers are stepping in near lower levels, but it remains too early to treat that activity as evidence of sustained demand.

A firm hold above $0.00000383 would give bulls a stronger foundation for extending the rebound. A loss of that level, however, could expose the token to renewed selling pressure and reinforce concerns that the latest move was only a temporary pause in a broader market dip.

Price action in highly speculative tokens can turn rapidly, particularly when trading is driven by short-term positioning rather than fresh fundamental developments. PEPE coin remains especially sensitive to shifts in market mood, with traders likely to watch whether buying volume follows the price recovery.

The immediate setup is therefore straightforward: consolidation above support would suggest that sellers are losing some control, while another rejection could leave the token vulnerable to further downside. Neither PEPE’s price outcome is guaranteed: volatility remains the central risk.

#5158. Top 9 crypto exchanges of November 2026

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Cryptocurrency exchanges operate by acting as a connecting platform between buyers and sellers. They provide users with a trading platform to create accounts, complete their KYC, deposit and withdraw funds, and start trading in the supported cryptocurrencies.

Users can access features like spot, futures, and derivatives trading. There are wallets available that allow them to store, deposit, and withdraw digital assets. Crypto exchanges make markets accessible to individuals and institutions around the world and provide liquidity. Here is our list of the top 9 crypto exchanges of November 2026:

Binance was established in 2017 and is one of the biggest crypto exchanges in the world. It has evolved into one of the most comprehensive ecosystems that caters to retail investors, developers, and institutional customers.

It has a lot of trading services, including spot, margin, futures, and peer-to-peer trading in supported markets. There are also advanced charting tools, trading indicators, various order types, and market analysis that support different types of trading.

Founded in 2017, OKX has developed a broader ecosystem over time that includes Web3 services, derivatives, cryptocurrency trading, and blockchain-related products. The platform is a great pick for retail users, and professional traders alike.

It has the options of spot trading, margin trading, futures, and perpetual swaps. There are market charts, technical indicators, order management features and tools that are designed for amateur and experienced traders.

Kraken was founded in 2011 and is one of the oldest exchanges in the space. It supports cryptocurrency buying, selling, and spot trading while the Kraken Pro platform has advanced trading options that include market charts, order books, technical analysis and different order execution options.

There are margin trading, futures, staking options also available on the platform. They have a strong focus on security which includes measures like encryption, account protection features, two-factor authentication, and secure digital asset custody arrangements.

Bitget has spot and futures trading along with other digital asset trading options. It has real-time market information, technical charts, trading indicators, and order management features. Copy trading is one of its major distinctive products that lets users replicate trading strategies from other traders on the platform.

There are security measures like withdrawal controls, risk monitoring and asset protection mechanisms in place to ensure user safety. They also support a lot of crypto and trading pairs letting users access to different digital asset markets.

KuCoin has a wide range of cryptocurrencies and trading services and provides traders access to digital assets, spot trading, margin trading, and futures. There are trading charts, technical indicators, multiple order types, trading charts and market information.

There are also trading bots available that allow users to automate selected trading strategies. Lending-related products, and wallet integrations are also part of the KuCoin platform.

Crypto.com was established in 2016 and created a broad ecosystem over the years that includes cryptocurrency trading, payments, digital wallets, and other blockchain-related products. It also has a crypto wallet, payment-related products and eligible digital asset earning programmes.

The security measures on Crypto.com include multi-factor authentication, withdrawal protection, digital asset custody controls and account monitoring. The exchange is also known for its customer-oriented approach and efforts to connect crypto services with everyday financial activities.

Bitfinex offers margin trading, spot trading and derivatives products along with lending and funding related services in supported markets. There are detailed order books, customizable trading layouts, advanced charts and multiple order charts.

The advanced trading interface and liquidity infrastructure distinguish it from platforms designed primarily for beginners. In terms of security measures as well, Bitfinex has monitoring mechanisms in place and multi-factor authentication as well.

Coinbase started out in 2012 and has developed its services beyond cryptocurrency buying and selling to include institutional custody, and many blockchain-related products that are useful for advanced and beginner users. It was created with the vision of making crypto accessible through a secure and user-friendly digital platform.

The platform has an Advanced trading experience that lets traders use features such as real-time market charts, order books, price indicators, and advanced order execution options. Coinbase Prime gives eligible institutions access to trading, custody, and other digital asset infrastructure.

Gate supports a wide range of digital assets and crypto trading pairs. Its ecosystem has options like spot trading, margin trading, futures trading, digital asset management, Web3 services, and derivatives.

Gate supports 5,400 digital assets and has over 63 million users around the world with lightning-fast listings for new and trending tokens.

Crypto exchanges are an essential part of the global financial system and important towards driving mainstream crypto adoption. Exchanges connect individuals, businesses, institutional investors and financial markets and aren’t just trading platforms but also enablers of digital adoption.

As with any financial decision, users should conduct their own research, review an exchange’s security measures and fee structure, and fully understand the risks involved before committing significant funds.

The Teucrium fund gives investors double leveraged exposure to XRP.

U.S. spot XRP ETFs held $1.08 billion in total assets as of Nov. 17, according to SoSoValue.

#5156. Google Warns North Korean Hackers Are Poisoning AI Tools to Drain Crypto Firms

Home » Crypto Market News » Google Warns North Korean Hackers Are Poisoning AI Tools to Drain Crypto Firms

State-backed hackers are using fake recruiters and tampered Claude Code settings to breach crypto networks.

Cyberattacks against the cryptocurrency industry have crossed a dangerous threshold as threat actors shift from basic AI prompts to autonomous AI agents capable of running intrusions from start to finish.

According to the Google Threat Intelligence Group (GTIG) and Mandiant report, state-backed hackers are actively targeting crypto firms in North America and Europe by turning popular AI developer tools into malware delivery traps.

Researchers have linked a suspected North Korean hacking campaign to attacks on cryptocurrency companies. The group, known as Midnight Neptune, uses fake recruiter profiles and tampered software settings to break into company networks.

The attackers approach targets on professional networking platforms, posing as recruiters with AI-generated profile photos.

They then invite victims to fake online meetings, where simulated audio or video problems prompt them to run scripts to fix the issue. This social engineering trick, known as ClickFix, gets victims to execute malicious commands themselves.

Once inside a company’s network, the attackers tamper with its internal code repositories, modifying the configuration file used by Anthropic’s Claude Code.

The next time a developer launches Claude Code, the altered settings silently download a backdoor called SOMBERMEME.

The attackers also create GitHub Personal Access Tokens disguised as Microsoft Copilot to make their activity look like normal developer traffic.

The report also revealed a major advance in automated hacking that puts open-source Web3 infrastructure at risk.

Google researchers discovered an active tool called exploit_agent on an exposed testing server. It uses large language models (LLMs) to compare software code before and after security updates, identify newly patched vulnerabilities, write code to exploit them, and launch attacks — all without human involvement.

That puts open-source protocols at risk. Since code updates are publicly available, hackers can spot newly fixed vulnerabilities and exploit them before operators have a chance to install the patches. Even a brief delay can leave networks vulnerable.

Google Threat Intelligence Group (GTIG) reported that disclosed vulnerabilities and exploits nearly doubled between January and August 2026.

Exploited zero-day vulnerabilities also rose from an average of eight per month in 2025 to 11 in 2026, reaching 22 in August alone.

Beyond traditional cyberattacks, Mandiant warns of a growing threat called LLMJacking. Criminals hijack corporate cloud accounts, steal AI computing power, and resell it through underground marketplaces known as LLM Mixers. The resulting fraud can exceed $100,000 in a single day and reach $1 million per victim.

Meanwhile, influence networks use more than 100,000 fake social media accounts to spread misinformation, fuel token hype, and trigger artificial market panic.

To counter these threats, Google is deploying AI security tools such as Big Sleep and CodeMender to find and fix software vulnerabilities before hackers exploit them.

The GTIG report highlights several security risks for the crypto industry.

Companies using AI coding tools should check their code repositories for unauthorized commands and keep third-party access tokens secure.

They also need to install software updates quickly, as AI-powered hackers can turn newly discovered vulnerabilities into working attacks.

Employees should never follow unexpected browser or video-call instructions asking them to copy and run computer commands.

Investors, meanwhile, should be cautious of sudden waves of online hype or panic, which fake social media accounts can create at scale.

#5155. Nvidia Shares Rise After Earnings Beat in First Report Since Jensen Huang's Exit

Nvidia Shares Rise After Earnings Beat in First Report Since Jensen Huang's Exit

Nvidia shares climbed roughly 2% in after-hours trading Tuesday after the AI chipmaker delivered another record quarter, beating Wall Street expectations on revenue and earnings while forecasting stronger-than-expected sales for the current period.

The results offered some relief to investors after a brutal selloff that wiped hundreds of billions of dollars from Nvidia's market value following the resignation of co-founder Jensen Huang.

According to Nvidia's earnings release, revenue reached $115.8 billion for the fiscal third quarter ended October 25, up 103% from a year earlier and comfortably above analysts' expectations of approximately $109.1 billion.

Adjusted earnings per share came in at $2.68, beating the roughly $2.47 consensus estimate. Net income under generally accepted accounting principles reached $65.3 billion, more than double the $31.9 billion reported a year earlier.

Nvidia's data center division generated $107.6 billion in revenue, up 110% year over year and accounting for approximately 93% of total sales. The company also confirmed that its Vera Rubin NVL72 computing platform has begun shipping, extending the hardware roadmap that helped make Nvidia the dominant supplier of AI processors.

The company expects fourth-quarter revenue of $130 billion, plus or minus 3%, compared with Wall Street estimates of approximately $125.3 billion, per Reuters. That forecast includes an additional week in the reporting period and assumes no data center computing revenue from China.

Tuesday's report marks Nvidia's first financial results since Huang resigned November 12, ending his 33-year tenure following revelations of extramarital affairs involving company employees and a businesswoman suspected by U.S. intelligence officials of having ties to China. Those intelligence allegations remain unproven, and an independent investigation is ongoing.

To add more drama to the story, the Securities and Exchange Commission opened a civil investigation into whether Nvidia violated investor-disclosure laws.

Colette Kress, Nvidia's longtime chief financial officer and interim CEO, sought to reassure investors about the company's future. "Our momentum is real," she said in the earnings announcement, adding that the Vera Rubin rollout was off to a strong start and the product roadmap remained on track.

Huang said Tuesday that he would be honored to return as CEO if the board believed it was in Nvidia's best interests. His statement followed public support from Elon Musk and President Donald Trump, although the board is not considering reinstatement and continues searching for a permanent replacement.

Nvidia's fundamentals look considerably better than its stock chart. Despite Tuesday's rebound, shares remain roughly 35% below their October peak near $240, and the technical indicators suggest sellers still control the broader trend.

The daily chart shows immediate support around $150, close to the $148.80 low reached during last week's selloff. A decisive break below that area could expose further downside, while a sustained recovery would first need to reclaim resistance around $164.40 to $165.60.

That former support area has become resistance following the breakdown, as Decrypt's earlier technical analysis identified. Above it, the chart shows additional hurdles near $176 and between $184 and $189, making a return to the previous trading range far from automatic.

The Relative Strength Index, or RSI, is around 22.5 on the supplied daily chart, well below the conventional oversold threshold of 30. That makes a short-term rebound plausible, but oversold readings are not proof that a bottom has formed.

The Average Directional Index, or ADX, sits near 45.4, indicating a strong prevailing trend, with directional signals favoring sellers. The faster exponential moving average remains above the slower one, a positive longer-term configuration that has not prevented the recent collapse.

Tuesday's earnings beat gives bulls a potential catalyst, but the after-hours bounce has yet to reclaim the chart's first major resistance around $164.40. Kress is scheduled to lead the company's 5 p.m. Eastern earnings call, the first without Huang, as investors look for answers about Nvidia's leadership and its next phase of growth.

#5154. XRP Outperforms Solana and Dogecoin in Japan

Although XRP is at risk of losing its top-four position in the global cryptocurrency market, the asset still ranks among the three largest crypto assets in Japan.

In a recent post shared by the renowned Ripple-backed XRP treasury firm Evernorth, the company highlighted XRP’s growing performance in the Japan crypto market.

Earlier today, Evernorth shared monthly data from the Japan Virtual and Crypto Assets Exchange Association (JVCEA) revealing that XRP recorded about ¥132.8 billion in spot trading volume in September 2026.

While this accounted for about 15% of Japan's total crypto spot trading volume during the month, XRP emerged as the third most-traded cryptocurrency in Japan after Bitcoin and Ethereum.

This shows that XRP has outperformed other major altcoins that are currently trading in the Japan crypto market, hitting about $880 million in trading volume.

While further data showed Solana and Dogecoin ranking next to XRP on the list as the fourth- and fifth-largest cryptocurrencies in Japan, respectively, the data also showed a wide gap from XRP’s ¥132.8 billion spot volume.

This means that XRP is significantly ahead of Solana and Dogecoin in the Japan crypto trading market, as Solana recorded only about ¥19.47 billion in trading volume during the same month.

Dogecoin, on the other hand, saw less participation, as it recorded only ¥11.27 billion in spot volume during the same period.

With XRP sitting in the third spot, the asset has become one of the most actively traded cryptocurrencies in Japan despite its persistent price volatility.

#5152. MARA prices $185M debt offering after $1.1B Bitcoin sale

The Bitcoin miner is taking on new debt to finance its AI infrastructure expansion, with the Long Ridge acquisition adding nearly $69 million in annual interest costs.

Bitcoin miner MARA Holdings has priced a $185 million secured debt offering through its US subsidiary to help finance its $1.5 billion acquisition of Long Ridge Energy & Power.

According to a Tuesday filing with the US Securities and Exchange Commission, the new notes carry an 8.75% interest rate and mature in 2032. They will be added to $600 million in existing Long Ridge notes carrying the same rate, bringing the combined principal to $785 million and annual interest obligations to around $68.7 million.

The offering is expected to close on Nov. 27, alongside MARA’s acquisition of Long Ridge, which operates a 485-megawatt natural gas power plant in Ohio. MARA announced the $1.5 billion acquisition on April 30 as part of its expansion into AI and high-performance computing infrastructure. The Long Ridge property includes land that MARA says could support more than 1 gigawatt of data center capacity, although the company has yet to announce a major AI tenant for the proposed development.

The financing follows MARA’s Nov. 9 disclosure that it sold 13,597 Bitcoin after quarter-end for approximately $1.1 billion. It used $750 million to repay Bitcoin (BTC)-backed loans, releasing 17,477 BTC previously pledged as collateral.

MARA has sold billions of dollars in Bitcoin this year to reduce its debt. In March, the company sold 15,133 BTC for approximately $1.1 billion, using the proceeds to help repurchase $1 billion in convertible notes at a roughly 9% discount, saving approximately $88 million before transaction costs.

In its Nov. 9 shareholder letter, MARA said it had reduced debt by approximately $1.4 billion, or 37%, since the beginning of the year. However, the Long Ridge acquisition will bring additional debt onto its consolidated balance sheet.

Related: Luxor raises $16M from CMT Digital amid AI-Bitcoin miner convergence

According to MARA’s April 30 acquisition announcement, Long Ridge generated approximately $144 million in annualized adjusted EBITDA, extrapolated from the second half of 2025. The roughly $68.7 million in annual interest payments on the secured notes would equal nearly half that figure.

MARA also faces potential debt repayments next year. According to its third-quarter 10-Q, holders of approximately $924 million in convertible notes have the right to demand repayment in 2027, potentially creating another cash requirement as the company finances its AI expansion.

MARA shares traded at $6.57 at 6:53 pm UTC on Wednesday, up 2.34% on the day, but down 46% over the past six months, according to Yahoo Finance data.

#5151. Revolut Lists GoMining Token for UK and European Users

The move brings the niche utility token to more than 15 million fintech app users under the EU’s MiCA regulatory framework.

Retail fintech giant Revolut has added GOMINING, the native utility token of the digital Bitcoin mining platform GoMining, to its in-app cryptocurrency trading service. The listing enables over 15 million Revolut crypto users across the United Kingdom and the European Economic Area (EEA) to buy, hold, and trade the token alongside everyday banking services.

Prior to this integration, GOMINING was primarily accessible through crypto-native exchanges such as Kraken, KuCoin, and Bitget.

The listing builds on an existing payment integration established in 2024 that allowed European users to fund GoMining accounts via Revolut Pay. It also adds GOMINING to Revolut’s cryptocurrency trading service.

Revolut reviews digital assets before listing them, assessing their risks, compliance requirements and potential financial crime exposure. It also monitors listed tokens on an ongoing basis as part of its procedures under the EU’s Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation (MiCA).

“For years, there has been a clear line between crypto and traditional finance, and most people stayed on one side of it,” said Mark Zalan, CEO of GoMining, in a statement announcing the listing. “For GoMining, this listing means our token is no longer only for people who already live in the crypto world.”

GOMINING has several uses within the GoMining platform. Token holders can use it to pay digital miner maintenance fees, with discounts of up to 20%, buy digital miners on the platform’s marketplace and purchase boosts in Miner Wars, GoMining’s mining game.

The token also has a governance function. Users can lock their GOMINING tokens to receive veGOMINING, which gives them voting rights on the allocation of ecosystem rewards.

Revolut customers can now buy GOMINING directly through the app. To use the token on GoMining, they must first transfer it to an external GoMining account.

The Revolut listing gives GOMINING exposure to a wider retail audience through a mainstream financial app. It also provides another way for users to acquire the token without first opening an account on a crypto-native exchange.

However, buying GOMINING through Revolut does not provide direct access to GoMining’s platform features. Users must transfer their tokens to an external wallet before using them within the GoMining ecosystem.

#5150. What’s Coming for Solana in 2027? Upgrade Planned Across Four Key Areas

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

At the 2026 Breakpoint event, Brennan Watt, CEO of Anza, gave a walkthrough of Solana’s wins this year, while sharing what is in store for 2027.

According to Watt, 2026 was a major year for Solana as it migrated its consensus to Alpenglow, slashed the block times in half from 400 ms to 200 ms while doubling capacity.

The Alpenglow upgrade went live on mainnet on October 21, 2026, cutting transaction finality from about 12.8 seconds down to roughly 150 milliseconds. It replaced TowerBFT with Votor, a faster path to reaching agreement.

After Alpenglow, @bw_solana laid out @anza_xyz roadmap for Solana in 2027 Performance: network compute capacity doubles, transactions cut 80% CU units for token accounts Expressivity: vector processing for models running fully onchain, 64 → 96 accounts per transaction, read account data instead of copying it so less data moves per transaction Market Structure: Alpenglow. Next with faster block distribution and Constellation for clearer ordering rules and more competition among validators Resilience: simpler protocol with fewer special cases, safer to change https://t.co/EpvDdSyjln

Solana recently transitioned to 1.0, marking the network’s graduation from “mainnet beta” to “mainnet” and retiring the beta designation it has carried since launch. Solana 1.0 recognizes the progress on network performance, resilience, and developer experience.

According to Watt, Solana is eyeing significant improvements in 2027 across four categories. First is Resilience, which involves simplifying the protocol, reducing special cases, and making it safer to change.

“So, we're going to go ahead and simplify the protocol, replacing legacy abstractions and interfaces, pushing more of the execution on chain, and modernizing some of the wire formats. And all of this, you know, enhances resiliency, but it also allows us to move quicker and be more agile and deploy things and evolve the protocol more quickly,” Watt stated.

Second is Market Structure, which centers on Alpenglow, which aims for faster block distribution, and Constellation, which aims for clearer ordering rules and more competition among validators.

“Several important Alpenglow extensions are coming,” Watt stated. Alpenglow rolls out in two phases: first is Votor, its voting protocol, which is already live on the platform; second is Rotor, a single relay layer planned for a later release that will replace Turbine, the protocol that spreads a block’s data across the network.

Third is Performance, where network compute capacity doubles and transactions use 80% fewer compute units for token accounts. Last is Expressivity, which involves vector processing for models running fully onchain and an increase from 64 to 96 accounts per transaction.

Watt also spoke about improvements in onchain development: “The cost of storing data on Solana came down by 27% this year. And just in the coming two weeks, we're going to bring this thing down by another 7x.”

#5149. Tether’s USDT stuck at S&P’s lowest stability score despite a $7.95B buffer – Why?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

S&P Global Ratings assigned a score of 5 to Tether’s USDT, the weakest in its five-point stablecoin stability scale. What’s more surprising here is the fact that the assessment, which was published on the 18th of November, 2026, has remained unchanged since November 2025.

This was mainly because there was a growing proportion of higher-risk assets in Tether’s reserves. According to S&P Global, these assets accounted for 28% of circulating USDT as of the 30th of September 2026, compared with 24% in 2025.

Notably, the agency also warned that a significant decline in their market value could erode Tether’s reserve surplus and potentially leave it undercollateralized.

That said, Tether’s reserve composition has also shifted over the past year, with gold holdings rising from $12.9 billion in September 2025 to $19 billion in September 2026. Meanwhile, the company also added $4.2 billion in publicly traded equities, while its Bitcoin holdings declined from $9.9 billion to $8.4 billion.

Yet, despite the growing exposure to riskier assets, Tether reported $191.45 billion in reserves against $183.50 billion in liabilities as of the 30th of September. This in turn left a reserve buffer of approximately $7.95 billion and a collateralization ratio of 104.3%, up from 103.9% in 2025.

Still, above all of this, USDT traded close to its $1 peg over the past year, while its market capitalization grew from $158 billion to approximately $184 billion between September 2025 and September 2026.

However, according to a recent AMBCrypto report, Tether’s market capitalization dropped by $6.6 billion over the course of sixty days, ending on the 7th of November.

#5148. SHIB Targets Multiple Billions Via America’s Strike Event

Home » Shiba Inu (SHIB) » SHIB Targets Multiple Billions Via America’s Strike Event

Despite substantial deleveraging across markets, Shiba Inu earns a comfortable spot at USA’s price-calling giants.

Shiba Inu’s (SHIB) meme currency just got critical acclaim in major United States Perpetuals markets. Nadex, short for North American Derivatives Exchange, filed a motion to the United States Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC), pursuing time-capped price events.

Conducting business as Crypto.com Derivatives North America, the exchange self-certified a Shiba Inu Strike Event Contract under Commodity Futures Trading Commission regulations. This now allows traditional Derivatives market players exposure to cash-settled event bids.

These SHIB price-tracking contracts do not use traditional margin leverage. They are fully prepaid or collateralized, so the maximum loss per contract is limited to the price paid to enter (plus fees). There is no margin call or unlimited downside in the style of Perpetual Futures.

By this strategy, Shiba Inu’s been placed within an honorable roster of major-cap altcoins. The exchange also submitted strike event contract notices covering blue-chips like XRP, Solana, Stellar (XLM), Dogecoin (DOGE), Litecoin (LTC), Hedera (HBAR), Ondo & Polkadot (DOT).

The new Shiba Inu (SHIB) price-linked product is a binary-style swap that lets traders take a position on whether SHIB’s benchmark price finishes above a chosen strike at time expiration. Contracts are eligible for listing since November 13, 2026. Will that lift SHIB’s sloppy volumes?

The popular Japanese guard dog-encrusted meme currency saw a tremendous drop in Spot and Futures markets during Q3 of 2026, pushing the meme coin to dwell at new yearly lows. With the Derivatives markets piquing their interest in SHIB, a multi-billion influx could come.

Right now, the trading volumes present a grimy picture of $44.56 million in daily Spot trading volume. This puts Shiba Inu at the sixth position in the meme coin category, ranked by 24-hour trading volume. The canine coin’s down 24% in 30 days, while parent chain ETH slid by 25%.

Further on, the 24-hour turnover rate stood below 2%. On Spot markets, turnover counts SHIB trades already completed, while depth describes orders displayed at the moment of the reading. The same capital can trade repeatedly during a day. Low volumes resemble vague demand.

Next up is the major demand retest: if Shiba Inu’s (SHIB) $0.00000353 support bubble holds, the next checkpoint revolves around $0.00000515, with the mid-range extending to $0.000006. Traders need to stay alarmed due to the Parabolic Stop & Reverse (SAR) flashing a ‘sell’ signal on the one-day price charts: bottom may not be in yet.

Meanwhile, key data on Perpetual markets showcase a gradually rising total Open Interest (OI), as leveraged players increase their positions before a presumed drastic price swing. The soft volume hikes haven’t flipped the switch back to bullish.

Long price plays still account for most of liquidations, soaking up $79.81K out of $87.20K in liquidations on SHIB’s regular smart contract.

#5147. XRP and Solana ETF Plans Abruptly Scrapped After Yearlong Wait

Investment firm Roundhill has suddenly terminated plans to launch two new XRP- and Solana (SOL)-based exchange-traded funds (ETFs).

This decision comes after over a year of regulatory filings and multiple delays.

As per the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) filings on Nov. 18, Roundhill has officially withdrawn plans to launch the Roundhill XRP Covered Call Strategy ETF and Roundhill Solana Covered Call Strategy ETF.

Interestingly, it submitted the applications for these two funds on Oct. 17, 2025. Therefore, these two funds had been awaiting launch for over 13 months.

As per the latest regulatory filings, Roundhill has been consistently delaying the effective dates for these two funds.

However, instead of launching them, the investment firm has decided to drop both products.

In its requests to withdraw the filings for these funds, Roundhill revealed that it "has no further intention to pursue effectiveness of the Fund." The firm noted that it had not sold any shares under the registrations for these two funds.

Intriguingly, Roundhill failed to elaborate on the sudden decision to axe its plans to launch these two ETFs.

Roundhill was planning to launch income-generating XRP and Solana ETFs using a covered call strategy. In such a strategy, investors sell options to earn premiums while also maintaining exposure to the price movements of XRP and Solana.

Unlike spot-based ETFs, these funds would not have held the cryptocurrencies directly. Instead, they would have used options written against existing cryptocurrency ETFs.

A covered call strategy can be useful for generating regular income. However, it can also cap profits during strong market rallies.

Notably, there has been relatively low demand for income-generating cryptocurrency investment products. This raises concerns about investors' interest in these ETFs.

Surprisingly, this decision came amid substantial net inflows into spot-based XRP and Solana ETFs.

U.S. spot XRP and Solana ETFs recorded net inflows of roughly $969 million between May and October 2026, according to data from SoSoValue.

Overall, these spot ETFs held around $3.26 billion in assets under management as of the end of October.

#5146. Nvidia Board Shuts Down Jensen Huang Comeback, Launches Search for New CEO

Nvidia Board Shuts Down Jensen Huang Comeback, Launches Search for New CEO

Traders, fanboys, investors and believers. It’s time to stop coping.

Nvidia's board has publicly shut down hopes of Jensen Huang returning as CEO, delivering its strongest statement yet against reinstating the company's co-founder following his resignation last week.

"The Nvidia board does not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO," board chair Harvey Jones said during Tuesday's earnings call.

Jones said the board unanimously accepted Huang's resignation and would not answer questions about the circumstances surrounding his departure or the ongoing independent investigation.

The company has hired executive recruitment firm Heidrick & Struggles to find a permanent replacement. Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress, who took over as interim CEO, received the board's full backing.

"We have every confidence in Colette's leadership, integrity and ability to deliver as interim CEO," Jones said.

The declaration comes just hours after Huang publicly offered to return if directors believed his reinstatement would benefit shareholders and employees. Earlier today, before Huang’s public statement, sources told CNBC the board was not considering bringing Huang back.

Huang resigned after revelations about extramarital relationships, including one involving a businesswoman whose alleged Chinese intelligence connections have attracted scrutiny from U.S. authorities. An independent investigation is examining his conduct and potential national security implications.

His departure prompted an unusual campaign for reinstatement, with President Donald Trump and Elon Musk among those publicly supporting his return.

Jones also defended Nvidia's newly established National Security and Export Control Oversight Committee, saying it would improve engagement with U.S. officials as Washington investigates how restricted AI chips have reached China.

The committee, chaired by director Suzanne Nora Johnson, will oversee compliance programs and receive reports directly from Nvidia's national security and trade compliance teams.

Nvidia reported $2.5 billion in quarterly revenue from sales to Chinese business units under the Trump administration's limited export permissions.

Those arrangements face renewed uncertainty following reports that billions of dollars in restricted Nvidia hardware reached mainland China through overseas intermediaries.

The leadership confrontation overshadowed otherwise impressive quarterly results. Nvidia reported $115.8 billion in revenue, up 103% year over year, with data center sales reaching $107.6 billion.

The company forecast $130 billion in revenue for its fourth fiscal quarter, while assuming no data center computing revenue from China.

Ripple has also integrated RLUSD with payment providers Arf, Convera and Cross River Bank, and the stablecoin is now used in dollar settlements between companies.

In a CNBC interview on Nov. 19, Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse called blockchain-based transactions "a wave of emerging technology." "I think 10 years from now you'll see blockchain implemented across our financial infrastructure," he said.

#5143. Anthropic’s Record $81B IPO Is Here—Will It Test Crypto?

Home » Blitz news » Anthropic’s Record $81B IPO Is Here—Will It Test Crypto?

The Claude maker priced shares at $83 for a $1.5 trillion valuation as Bitcoin dips 1.5% ahead of Nasdaq trading.

Anthropic has priced its initial public offering (IPO) at $83 per share, raising roughly $81.3 billion in the largest public market debut in history—just as digital asset markets pull back and traders weigh whether the historic AI listing will siphon retail and institutional liquidity away from crypto.

According to Anthropic’s official announcement, the Claude AI developer priced its shares roughly 6.4% above its previously marketed range of $70 to $78, assigning the company a valuation of approximately $1.5 trillion. Trading on the Nasdaq under the ticker ANT is set to begin Thursday, November 19.

While Wall Street absorbed the massive tech offering, the broader cryptocurrency market traded lower on Thursday morning.

Total crypto market capitalization dropped 1.3% over the past 24 hours to $2.253 trillion, with Bitcoin (BTC) falling 1.5%, the most among the other top 10 biggest cryptos. Bitcoin is currently trading at $63,585.

Anthropic has expanded its IPO to 980 million shares, up from the initially planned 825 million, following exceptionally strong demand from institutional and retail investors.

Priced at $83 per share, the public offering is expected to raise approximately $81.3 billion, surpassing SpaceX’s $75 billion base offering in June and setting a new record.

The company is also raising an additional $26 billion through a private placement of non-voting shares, priced at the same $83 per share. Reported strategic investors include Nvidia, Microsoft, Google, AMD, Broadcom, Micron and TSMC. Combined, the public offering and private placement are expected to generate more than $107 billion.

Retail investors have been allocated up to 20% of the public offering, representing approximately $16.2 billion. Demand has been particularly strong, with total orders reportedly reaching nearly seven times the number of shares available, according to CNBC.

The deal values Anthropic at approximately $1.5 trillion, marking a dramatic increase from its $965 billion private valuation when it confidentially filed for an IPO in June.

Anthropic’s IPO could have implications for crypto markets beyond the AI sector. The $155 billion in combined IPO capital raised by SpaceX and Anthropic within five months highlights growing competition for institutional liquidity, potentially diverting funds from risk assets such as Bitcoin and altcoins.

The listing also gives retail investors a direct way to bet on AI, potentially reducing demand for crypto-native AI tokens that have attracted investors seeking similar exposure.

ANT’s market debut will also be closely watched as a gauge of risk appetite. A strong opening could lift sentiment across speculative assets, while a weak performance could reinforce risk-off sentiment in both equities and crypto.

#5142. Bitcoin’s $2.58B ETF outflows deepen November sell-off – Is $60K next?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Bitcoin’s [BTC] price slide is accelerating, with the king coin falling below $63,000 amid a new wave of ETF outflows and a broader rotation of capital into the AI sector.

The drop extends a brutal month that has seen roughly $2.58 billion flee U.S. Spot Bitcoin ETFs since the start of November.

On the 19th of November, Spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded a net outflow of $306.50 million, with BlackRock’s IBIT accounting for $209.5 million, roughly 87% of the day’s redemptions.

The price action followed the outflows, as Bitcoin traded near $63,071 at the time of writing, down nearly 3% on the day and hovering at its lowest levels since mid-August. Ethereum fared worse, though, dropping below $1,800 at press time.

Another massive factor behind Bitcoin’s fall was noted by the X account Why Markets Move. It stated that currently, investors are seeing more potential in the AI boom and the upcoming Anthropic IPO, compared to crypto.

For the uninitiated, Anthropic is currently targeting a valuation of up to $1.4 trillion in its Nasdaq listing, with investors such as Nvidia and Microsoft. This is undoubtedly good news for AI, but not such much for digital assets.

Raj Kamal, founder and CEO of TransFi, told AMBCrypto how policy shifts and macro conditions can crush crypto sentiment. He said,

I think the market read the result as a short-term negative for crypto. Much of the rise in bitcoin and in stablecoin adoption came after the 2024 US election, and investors now expect closer scrutiny of consumer protection, anti-money-laundering rules and the crypto interests of President Trump’s family.

Kamal also outlined how ETF redemptions became another salient point for Bitcoin’s fall.

U.S. Spot bitcoin ETFs recorded about $1.91 billion of net outflows over seven straight sessions from 5 to 13 November. Institutional money began to leave as the price fell, and once Bitcoin went below $70,000, leveraged positions were flushed out.

His outlook was playing out in real life, with November’s cumulative ETF outflows now exceeding $2.5 billion until press time. The CEO ended with a warning.

I don’t think that flushing out is over yet.

On a similar note, stablecoin supply has plunged by roughly $14.28 billion over the past thirty days, signaling a broader drain of dollar liquidity from the crypto ecosystem.

The AI rotation has to simmer down, and regulatory clarity needs to improve for Bitcoin, otherwise its downward spiral will not stop. Therefore, traders have to now watch the $62,000 support level, but a sustained break below will lead BTC to $60,000.

#5141. Binance says it obtained $6.5B worldwide freezing order after hack

The court orders allow legal notices to be served via blockchain and provide for a continuously updated list of 26,000 wallet addresses linked to stolen assets, Binance’s lawyers said.

Binance said it has secured a $6.5 billion worldwide freezing injunction from the Abu Dhabi Global Market Courts as it seeks to recover assets stolen in its record Oct. 29 hack.

The court orders target unidentified perpetrators of the attack and are intended to help issuers, exchanges and other partners freeze and return stolen assets, the company said in a statement shared with Cointelegraph on Wednesday.

The orders can be served via blockchain, according to M. Kendall Day of Gibson Dunn, which represents Binance. This allows legal notices to be delivered to wallet addresses rather than through traditional channels. Day said market participants would also be notified of updates to a “living list” of 26,000 wallet addresses identified as holding stolen assets.

Binance said some businesses require formal court orders before they can release, transfer or return frozen funds. The injunction’s $6.5 billion amount is not a tally of recovered assets, and the statement did not disclose how much had so far been returned under the orders.

Binance also said it obtained orders requiring stolen assets to be handed over. Separate interim orders cover assets frozen by certain named parties against whom no wrongdoing is alleged.

The legal action follows other efforts to secure the stolen funds in the wake of the hack, the largest-ever in the crypto industry. As Cointelegraph reported on Nov. 13, the US Justice Department said it had obtained court authorization to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion in assets stolen from Binance. That amount was part of more than $2.7 billion frozen through government and private sector efforts, the DOJ said.

Related: Binance hack may push institutions to outside custody, FalconX exec says

As Cointelegraph previously reported, the attackers converted stolen stablecoins into assets such as Bitcoin (BTC) and Ether (ETH), which cannot be frozen, as they lack a central issuer. Blocking the stolen tokens also does not mean they’ll be returned to Binance.

Binance said last week it had since completed coverage of affected assets after committing more than $6.34 billion from its various treasury, Secure Asset Fund for Users and insurance funds. Still, replenishing customer assets is separate from recovering the stolen cryptocurrencies from the hack.

Related: North Korea denies role in $6.3B Binance hack, points finger at US

Separately, Binance’s Asia-Pacific head SB Seker told Reuters on Wednesday that the exchange plans to enter five Asian markets in 2027, prioritizing South and Southeast Asia. He did not name the markets and said details were expected in the first quarter.

Seker also said Binance did not need fresh capital to absorb the losses from the October hack.

#5140. $169 Million of Unstaked Solana (SOL) Hits Major US Crypto Exchange

Solana has remained one of the major cryptocurrencies that have been heavily affected by the persistent crypto market volatility, stalling demand from both retail and institutional investors.

Recent data showcased by blockchain monitoring platform Lookonchain shows that two Solana whales unstaked a total of 2.31 million SOL on November 19.

While the total amount of SOL unstaked was worth about $169.4 million, further data showed that the whales proceeded to transfer the tokens to Coinbase, a leading cryptocurrency exchange in the U.S.

It is important to note that the act of withdrawing SOL previously committed to the network's staking mechanism is regarded as unstaking.

While this move automatically gives holders the ability to transfer, sell, or use the unstaked tokens, the massive unstaking of 2.31 million SOL from large holders has drawn attention within the crypto community regarding the intention behind the move.

With curiosity on the rise about the whales' decision to move such a large amount of SOL to Coinbase Prime, the timing of the move has triggered speculation that the whales may be intending to sell the tokens.

Notably, the move arrived when Solana was trading around $74, briefly shifting into green territory just a few days after it retested $67, the lowest level seen since June 2026.

Since the prolonged price correction has continued to weaken investor confidence, it appears that the whales have acted upon the growing fear and withdrawn the assets they had initially committed to the Solana network to hedge against further losses.
