#5139. Why Microsoft Thinks Smaller, Cheaper AI Can Beat Claude and GPT

Why Microsoft Thinks Smaller, Cheaper AI Can Beat Claude and GPT

Microsoft unveiled a new lineup of specialized AI models at its Ignite conference Tuesday, making a direct run at Anthropic and OpenAI in coding, cybersecurity, and image generation.

Instead of trying to build a single chatbot that beats everyone at everything, it is pitching models trained to do particular jobs at substantially lower cost.

The biggest claim involves MAI Code 2, which Microsoft says delivers 96% of Claude Opus 5.5's performance on common enterprise coding tasks for just 5% of the cost. It also claims 98% of GPT-6.1 Sol's performance at 40% of the price.

OpenAI also released GPT-6.2 on Tuesday, so Microsoft’s Sol comparison already concerns an older generation.

Those figures average results from four evaluations focused on practical software development, including fixing code and completing multi-file changes. Microsoft ran three of the tests on its own evaluation platform, used outside results for the fourth, and calculated MAI Code 2's costs using a 50% promotional discount.

A lighter Code 2 Flash model targets routine fixes and quick edits, with Microsoft claiming performance comparable to Claude Haiku 5.5 at 20% of the price, so extremely cheap without sacrificing quality. Both coding models are already in GitHub Copilot, putting Microsoft's in-house technology directly into a product that has relied heavily on outside AI suppliers.

Security is another target. Microsoft says MAI Cyber 2 Flash succeeded on 92.3% of CyberGym-E2E's evaluated vulnerability-discovery tasks, compared with 91.5% for GPT-6 Astra, at less than 2% of the cost per task. CyberGym-E2E scores the percentage of software-security investigations an AI completes successfully; Microsoft excluded 92 of 905 tasks after identifying overlap between their source material and its training data.

The company has not yet published a direct comparison against OpenAI's restricted GPT-6 Cyber model. Its security system will begin replacing an older MAI model for eligible MDASH customers in mid-December, building on the vulnerability-scanning approach Decrypt previously covered.

Can a small model beat a big one? Definitely—in specific cases.

A general-purpose model has to learn to write poetry, explain physics, debug software and answer questions about almost anything. A model tuned for coding or cyber defense can spend more of its training and computational budget on the patterns that matter for that job, rather than carrying the same breadth of knowledge into every request.

That doesn't make smaller models universally smarter. It can make them faster and more economical when the task is well defined, particularly in a business where the same work runs thousands of times a day.

A security scanner analyzing every new code change does not need an expensive frontier model to explain the history of the Roman Empire. If a model can dedicate its full power to generate Python code, it will make more sense to pay for its API calls instead of using a super powerful generalistic model.

Specialization can also improve the whole system around the model: the tools it calls, the checks that verify its answers and the data used to refine it. OpenAI has taken a related approach with its Mini and Nano models, letting cheaper workers handle routine tasks while larger systems tackle the exceptions.

However, there are trade-offs. Microsoft's own documentation says Code 2 is not yet optimized for fundamental scientific research or very long-running autonomous assignments. And Anthropic competes in the cheaper end of the market with Haiku, not just its premium Opus models, so Microsoft is entering a race its rivals already understand.

Microsoft's MAI Image 2.7 may offer the clearest evidence that its strategy is working. In our quick side-by-side tests, its images beat GPT Image and Google's Nano Banana on realism, creativity, and overall quality.

These were just first impressions based on a few generations, but they broadly track its strong showing in independent rankings.

On Artificial Analysis's image-editing test, which asks people to choose between anonymously generated edits and turns those preferences into an Elo rating, MAI Image 2.7 currently leads with 1,215 points against GPT Image 2.6 Sunburst's 1,211.

The four-point gap is small enough that their reported uncertainty ranges overlap; the separate text-to-image generation ranking, scored using the same preference-based method, puts Microsoft in fourth place.

The pricing is harder to ignore. Microsoft quotes about $35 per 1,000 images for MAI Image 2.7 and $10 for its Flash version, versus roughly $211 for GPT Image 2.6 Sunburst at maximum quality in Artificial Analysis's comparison. Google's Nano Banana 2.1 is about $34 per 1,000 there, while Nano Banana Pro is about $134; actual bills depend on the provider, resolution and settings.

Our earlier testing of MAI Image 2 found excellent realism but frustrating limits on editing and output formats. The newer release adds interactive editing through Microsoft's Canvas workspace, transparent backgrounds and broader access through Foundry, although image quality and a usable product are still two different things. That said the model is quite censored too.

Microsoft is also offering selected business customers the ability to customize and run its models' licensed weights inside Azure, reducing reliance on outside vendors without making the technology open source. For the next test of its specialist strategy, Code 2 is already available in GitHub Copilot, and the Cyber 2 Flash rollout is scheduled to start in mid-December.

#5138. Trading Bots No Longer a Niche: Now It’s Web3’s Default

Home » Exchanges » Trading Bots No Longer a Niche: Now It’s Web3’s Default

AI-powered trading tools are gradually becoming the default interface for Web3 trading, new research reveals.

A new report from Bitrue Research Institute tracks how trading bots have shifted from specialist software for technical users into everyday infrastructure across crypto markets.

The research paper combines public GitHub data on open-source projects, a survey of Bitrue users, and profiles of eight active bots to show where automation is heading.

Public GitHub activity shows a clear change in focus. New repositories describing themselves as “trading agents” jumped from 110 in 2024 to 1,133 in 2025 and reached 6,121 through November 15, 2026, more than five times the prior year’s total.

Classic crypto trading bot projects also grew sharply, rising from 551 in 2024 to 2,399 in 2025 (a 4.4× increase) with another 2,948 created so far in 2026.

The longest-running open-source tools still dominate stars: Freqtrade (56,933), Hummingbot (21,231), Jesse (8,807), and OctoBot (6,951).

An industry survey of 3,708 crypto traders published by MEXC in October 2026 found that 84.8% had used AI for research or trading in the previous six months, with 53.1% doing so at least weekly.

🤖 Here are more ways to earn with #BitrueAI!With #BitrueAI, select from a list of #crypto like #Bitcoin, #Solana and #XRP🔸 30 days backtested results🔸 Choose your own grid direction🔸 Customize your own settings with #BitrueAI👉 Start Now: https://t.co/3PW15XTAvd pic.twitter.com/ohHDBirMmQ

Among AI users, 34.6% had used an exchange’s own strategies or bots and 17.1% had connected an external agent.

On one major multi-exchange platform, average daily bot launches rose 29% from July to September 2026, reaching 862 per day, with real-fund launches up 36% and DCA bots up 80%.

The report identifies five structural changes:

In Bitrue’s survey of users who had not yet tried an AI trading bot, concern about losses and risk control was the top reason for hesitation (45.5%). Other common reasons included not trading futures yet (27.3%) and simply not knowing the feature existed (22.7%).

Willingness to let AI trade rose from 55% when only notifications were offered to 71.8% when limits on funds, positions, leverage, and losses were in place. Only 6.5% of respondents said they would allow AI to control more than half their account.

Among non-users, the most requested first steps were a clearer explanation of what the bot does (27.3%) and a trial reward for the first trade (22.7%). Half of those who had not tried an AI bot said they were ready to do so; another 36.4% wanted to learn more first.

Users who had already tried Bitrue AI most often cited time saved from watching charts, automatic execution, and market analysis as the main benefits. Requests for improvement centered on more trading pairs, flexible parameters & alerts tied to indicators such as RSI.

The report profiles eight systems that illustrate different approaches:

AI automation continues to expand, but adoption still hinges on control. Crypto traders want to see why a strategy was chosen, know the maximum loss, test it first, and understand connection security and costs.

Tools that explain their reasoning, enforce user-set boundaries, and allow small or reversible starts are the ones positioned to convert interest into regular use. The report does not forecast specific outcomes beyond these observed trends.

The numbers show both rapid builder activity and cautious but growing trader demand. The gap between the two is being closed by guardrails, explanations, and simpler interfaces rather than by handing full control to AI.

Gram [GRAM] fell by 5.28% on Wednesday, the 18th of October. On the same day, Bitcoin [BTC] faced a 2.89% slide, while the cumulative altcoin market cap dipped by 1.62%.

Could the token be a buying opportunity after the recent volatility, or should traders maintain their bearish bias?

The 1-month liquidation heatmap for the altcoin presented an interesting situation for traders. Earlier this month, GRAM had rallied to $1.27 as Telegram founder Pavel Durov called the token “ridiculously undervalued.

This rally left behind magnetic zones to either side of the move. The $1 is a psychologically important level that has technical confluence with the rally’s floor.

A bounce toward $1.045-$1.09, or a breakdown below $0.98, might offer a more likely trading opportunity for sellers looking to enter.

#5136. Bain Capital Crypto leads $110M Variational round at $1.5B valuation

Variational raised $110 million at a $1.5 billion valuation to expand Omni’s derivatives markets and increase trading limits.

Onchain derivatives trading platform Variational has raised $110 million in a funding round led by Bain Capital Crypto, valuing the company at $1.5 billion as it seeks to challenge traditional brokerages by connecting crypto traders with liquidity from established financial markets.

The Series B round also received backing from Dragonfly, HSG, Peak XV and 50T Funds, according to the company’s Wednesday announcement.

Variational powers Omni, a platform offering leveraged exposure to more than 550 crypto and traditional markets. Unlike Hyperliquid, which maintains separate order books for its markets, Variational aggregates liquidity from onchain and off-chain sources, including Hyperliquid itself, a company spokesperson told Cointelegraph.

“Our unique model allows us to bridge off-chain liquidity to on-chain traders,” the spokesperson said, adding that liquidity from its unnamed tier-one market maker remains off-chain.

Since launching in early 2025, Omni has processed more than $475 billion in trading volume across over 100,000 accounts, the company said. Traditional markets now account for more than half of its daily trading volume.

Variational also launched its swaps product in September, which has since generated more than $80 billion in trading volume, according to the company.

Despite traditional assets accounting for most daily trading volume, Variational’s customers remain primarily crypto-native retail traders, a company spokesperson told Cointelegraph. Asked about competing with traditional brokerages such as Robinhood and Interactive Brokers, the spokesperson pointed to the need to make the platform more accessible to users unfamiliar with cryptocurrency.

Related: Hyperliquid Strategies seeks futures registration for perps

“The platform needs to abstract away the ‘crypto’ feel,” the spokesperson said, adding that the company is developing email-based account registration and alternative deposit methods to remove the current requirements for crypto wallets and USDC.

Variational faces stiff competition in onchain derivatives trading. Hyperliquid recorded approximately $274 billion in reported trading volume over the past 30 days, compared with $82 billion for Lighter and $62 billion for Variational, according to DeFiLlama data at the time of writing on Wednesday. Variational also ranked third in open interest at approximately $688 million, behind Hyperliquid’s $5.7 billion and Aster’s $1.2 billion.

#5134. Bitcoin Is Calmer Than 19% of S&P 500 Stocks, But Here’s a Catch

Home » Bitcoin (BTC) » Bitcoin Is Calmer Than 19% of S&P 500 Stocks, But Here’s a Catch

Grayscale’s latest report highlights a hidden risk that still catches market-timing investors off guard.

New research from Grayscale shows Bitcoin’s (BTC) volatility has fallen to roughly 43%—lower than nearly one in five S&P 500 stocks.

The catch? Bitcoin’s calmer day-to-day trading hides a massive trap for anyone trying to time dips: almost all of its multi-year gains still come from just a handful of explosive trading days.

In its report, “Beyond Volatility: Rethinking Cryptocurrency Risks,” US digital asset manager Grayscale says Bitcoin’s long-term performance is heavily influenced by a handful of trading days. Its returns have “fat tails,” meaning sharp gains and losses occur more frequently than in traditional stocks.

Over the past three years, Bitcoin delivered an annualized return of roughly 60%. Excluding its 10 best days would have cut that figure to 7%, while excluding its 10 worst days would have raised it to 197%.

The report also notes that Bitcoin has historically rebounded sharply after major declines. Since 2011, it has experienced seven drawdowns exceeding 50%, although Grayscale says these declines are not unusually severe compared with stocks and bonds when adjusted for volatility.

Grayscale also sees Bitcoin as a potential portfolio diversifier because it is less exposed to traditional market factors.

The company points out that Bitcoin has historically recovered sharply after significant declines. Since 2011, Bitcoin has experienced seven drawdowns of more than 50% from previous highs.

The report cites Bitcoin’s inclusion in J.P. Morgan Asset Management’s 2027 Long-Term Capital Market Assumptions as a milestone.

Grayscale describes JPMorgan as a $5 trillion investment manager that now examines Bitcoin alongside established asset classes.

The banks’ 10-year base case for Bitcoin projects a 10% compound annualized return for Bitcoin over the next decade, the highest among the asset classes covered. The forecast also assumes 55% annualized volatility and a 33% correlation with US large-cap stocks, the number below international equities, corporate bonds and hedge funds.

In Bitcoin’s early years, volatility routinely exceeded 100% and at times approached 200%, with a market cap in the single-digit billions.

Grayscale ties the decline to roughly 43% to larger market value and liquidity, greater institutional participation, Bitcoin exchange-traded products and regulatory clarity.

In historical tests, modest Bitcoin allocations raised annualized return and Sharpe ratio in a representative institutional portfolio.

Over three years, Bitcoin explained about half of return variation across FTSE Grayscale Crypto Sectors. Among the 10 largest non-stablecoin assets, six led quarterly performance over the past two years, while Bitcoin led none.

JPMorgan’s decision to include Bitcoin in its long-term forecasts, along with declining volatility, could shape how institutions approach crypto investments. At the same time, Grayscale’s data shows how much Bitcoin’s long-term performance depends on a small number of trading days, highlighting the risks of trying to time the market.

#5133. Anthropic (ANT) Debuts on Coinbase Amid Volatile IPO

Leading cryptocurrency exchange Coinbase has announced the official listing of artificial intelligence (AI) giant Anthropic (ANT) shares on its trading platform in the U.S. and U.K.

The listing comes as Anthropic, the company behind the popular AI chatbot Claude, officially made its much-anticipated stock market debut on Thursday, Nov. 19, with its shares seeing massive volatility.

As per the latest X post from Coinbase, the exchange announced the availability of Anthropic (ANT) shares for eligible users in the U.S. and U.K.

It's important to mention that ANT isn't a cryptocurrency but a traditional stock. Coinbase's support for ANT shares shows the company's efforts to expand its offerings beyond crypto into traditional financial markets.

Anthropic has set a new historic precedent, having successfully raised about $81 billion in its initial public offering (IPO). This is the biggest IPO ever.

The artificial intelligence company sold 980 million shares at $83 per share, significantly higher than its previously announced price range of $70 to $78.

This brought Anthropic's initial valuation to about $1.5 trillion, making the company one of the most valuable publicly listed firms in the world right from the get-go.

But as Anthropic shares started trading on the Nasdaq exchange, the ANT ticker saw massive volatility.

As per Reuters, Anthropic shares began trading at $111.50. This represented an enormous 34% premium over the IPO price.

At the initial peak, Anthropic's market cap exceeded $2 trillion, surpassing SpaceX's valuation when it debuted on the market in June.

Anthropic shares slipped to around $95 as the stock gave back a significant chunk of its gains. This was partly due to investors looking to book their profits.

Nonetheless, Anthropic shares were still trading comfortably above their $83 IPO price.

The volatility surrounding Anthropic's stock market debut signals increasing doubts about the AI investment craze.

The listing of Anthropic shares is just one of Coinbase's moves to enter traditional financial markets.

Instead of just providing cryptocurrency trading, the exchange is looking to become a platform for multiple asset classes.

Interestingly, Coinbase had already listed Anthropic pre-IPO perpetual futures for eligible non-U.S. users on Nov. 18.

The perpetual futures were derivative contracts that allowed traders to speculate on Anthropic's valuation before its shares officially started trading on Nasdaq.

With the latest listing, eligible Coinbase users in the U.S. and U.K. can now trade the publicly listed Anthropic shares.

Anthropic's blockbuster debut comes as financial markets have been rocked by recent volatility, with tech stocks and crypto facing selling pressure.

#5132. ‘Fall now, rally later’ – Ripple CEO sees crypto rebound after Anthropic IPO

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

On the 19th of November, Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse said in an interview with CNBC that the recent crypto market sell-off is a result of capital rotating into IPOs like Anthropic. However, he does expect the markets to rebound once the listing is complete.

Still on-chain data for XRP suggests that despite on-chain indicators looking bullish, the altcoin is struggling to move beyond the $1 psychological level.

Garlinghouse argued that the recent bloodbath across digital assets is less about a fundamental collapse in crypto and more about investors shifting funds to chase the AI boom.

He pointed to a previous sell-off in the market, acknowledging,

We also saw a sell-off very similar when SpaceX went public. What happens is… people are taking liquidity out of a very liquid market in crypto, including Bitcoin and XRP, and moving it into SpaceX. The exact same thing happened coming into the Athropic IPO… What we saw after the SpaceX IPO is a rally in the crypto market.

He believes that the same story will play out with the Anthropic IPO too.

Garlinghouse’s bullish thesis does have a solid footing. Most notably, CryptoQuant’s data shows that the XRP Exchange Reserve on Binance has been declining over the last thirty days.

In late October 2026, Binance held roughly 2.7 billion XRP, but the figure dropped to 2.47 billion XRP as of the 19th of November.

When Exchange Reserve falls, it typically signals that investors are moving coins to self-custody, reducing the immediate supply available for selling.

Despite the tightening supply on exchanges, XRP’s price action remains volatile. At press time, XRP was trading at $0.9834, down 1.4% in the last 24 hours.

The chart further revealed a market struggling to find firm footing. XRP managed to hold above $1 during November 12-14 before a steep drop dragged it down to the $0.90 region.

The last 24 hours saw the asset sail briefly above $1 but faced an immediate rejection, slumping back into the high $0.90s.

As XRP remains in a tug-of-war between falling exchange reserves and repeated price rejections at $1.00, investors are uncertain about its next direction.

If Garlinghouse is correct and digital assets do rally after the Anthropic IPO, the rotation will easily cause prices to soar beyond $1. However, until that macroeconomic shift occurs, XRP holders appear content to move their coins off exchanges and wait out the storm.

#5131. Scattered Spider Hacker Sentenced to 6 Years Over $11.6M in Stolen Crypto

Scattered Spider Hacker Sentenced to 6 Years Over $11.6M in Stolen Crypto

A Scottish hacker who was part of the Scattered Spider hacking collective has been sentenced to six years in U.S. federal prison for his role in a scheme that stole at least $11.6 million in crypto.

Tyler Robert Buchanan, 24, of Dundee, was sentenced to 72 months by a federal judge in Santa Ana, California, on Tuesday, the U.S. Attorney's Office for the Central District of California said. The judge set a restitution hearing for December 11. Buchanan began hacking as a teenager and was part of Scattered Spider, the group of young, English-speaking hackers blamed for a string of high-profile cyberattacks, the BBC reported.

British national sentenced to six years in federal prison for hacking into companies and stealing at least $11 million in virtual currency https://t.co/h7oprrXcVK

— US Attorney L.A. (@USAO_LosAngeles) November 18, 2026

Buchanan pleaded guilty in April to conspiracy to commit wire fraud and aggravated identity theft. At the time, prosecutors said the scheme had stolen at least $8 million in crypto. That figure has since risen to at least $11.6 million.

Between September 2021 and April 2023, Buchanan and his co-conspirators sent hundreds of phishing text messages to employees of at least a dozen companies, including telecoms, technology firms and crypto companies, according to the indictment and Buchanan's plea agreement. The messages linked to fake websites that captured employees' login details, which were sent to a Telegram channel run by Buchanan and another co-conspirator.

The group used information stolen from those companies to identify individual crypto holders, then carried out SIM swaps, tricking mobile carriers into moving victims' phone numbers to devices they controlled so they could intercept two-factor authentication codes and empty victims' wallets.

In the largest single theft, one victim lost $1.67 million in December 2022, according to the BBC. When authorities searched Buchanan's home in Scotland in April 2023, they found the names and addresses of numerous victims, and a text file containing crypto seed phrases and login details for one victim's account, prosecutors said.

"Those thefts had severe, life-altering consequences for many victims," prosecutors argued in a sentencing memo.

Buchanan is the fifth and final defendant to be sentenced in the case, which the Justice Department charged in November 2024. A Justice Department spokesperson confirmed at the time that the defendants were suspected members of Scattered Spider, the group behind high-profile attacks including the 2023 breach of MGM Resorts. Buchanan was arrested at Palma de Mallorca Airport in Spain in May 2024 while trying to board a private flight to Naples, the BBC reported, and has been in U.S. custody since April 2025.

His co-defendant Noah Urban, of Palm Coast, Florida, is serving 10 years in prison, while Ahmed Hossam Eldin Elbadawy and Evans Onyeaka Osiebo were each sentenced to 45 months, and Joel Martin Evans to two years.

U.S. authorities have continued to pursue alleged Scattered Spider members. In July, a 19-year-old suspect was extradited from Finland over an $8 million crypto ransom demand against a luxury jeweler.

XRP could rebound toward $1.06–$1.07 as tentative signs of a bottom emerge, according to Cilinix.

XRP may have room to recover toward $1.06 and $1.07, according to the host of Cilinix Crypto, whose latest XRP analysis points to tentative signs of a bottom. The forecast is conditional: renewed weakness in equities could instead send the token back toward $0.90 and $0.85.

The distinction matters. The host sees enough improvement to support a short-term rebound, but not enough to call an end to the larger downtrend. “The higher timeframe structure is still very bad,” the analyst said.

The technical argument rests partly on XRP reclaiming its seven-day rolling volume-weighted average price, or VWAP, after a period of declines. The host noted that earlier recoveries above that measure had preceded upward moves, while cautioning that the indicator does not always carry much significance.

XRP had also moved back into the developing monthly value area, with an initially favorable reaction. Together, those developments gave the analyst more confidence that the market could stabilize and gradually push higher.

There is a weakness in that setup, however. XRP broke above a previous swing high but quickly fell back below it and had not reclaimed it at the time of the analysis. The host described that part of the structure as “slightly bearish.”

Funding rates offered another supporting signal. They turned sharply negative around the recent lows before becoming positive again, which the analyst viewed as consistent with a potentially defended bottom, not confirmation of a lasting reversal.

The broader backdrop kept the host cautious. Oil prices remained low and the volatility index had opened lower, but the S&P 500 was still near its lows.

Cilinix also cited a lack of ETF inflows as one reason not to adopt an XRP outlook that ran against the wider market.

The $1.06–$1.07 area is therefore a potential recovery zone, not a confirmed breakout target. Cilinix would become more conservative there because of the weak higher-timeframe structure.

#5125. Ledger warns Berlin customers as hardware wallet tampering investigation expands

Ledger advised customers who purchased devices in Berlin since January 2026 to contact support as it investigates suspected hardware implants in Germany and Dubai.

Ledger has warned customers who purchased hardware wallets in Berlin since January 2026 to contact its support team as the company investigates reports of tampered devices in Germany and the United Arab Emirates.

In a Wednesday update, Ledger said its investigation into a hardware wallet tampering campaign had identified several suspicious devices in Berlin. The company also acknowledged reports of tampered devices in Dubai, where two customers recently reported losing a combined $604,000 in cryptocurrency.

Ledger said the incidents were connected to the same distribution-channel tampering campaign first investigated in Southeast Asia in October, when the company asked authorized reseller CryptoBilis to suspend sales and shipments amid reports of cryptocurrency thefts. Ledger also confirmed that one Ledger Nano Gen 5 had been affected in the region.

According to the hardware wallet maker, all confirmed tampered devices contained similar physical modifications designed to capture users’ recovery phrases during setup. The company said it had found no vulnerability in its secure chip or operating system and no confirmed cases involving devices purchased directly from its website.

Ledger is also offering physical inspections, including X-ray analysis, at its factory in Vierzon, France, for customers concerned their devices may have been compromised.

#5124. Eight Years: From a Crypto Exchange to a Global Ecosystem Serving 40 Million Users

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

What started eight years ago as a cryptocurrency exchange has evolved into an international business ecosystem of 11 companies serving approximately 40 million users worldwide. As WhiteBIT celebrates its eighth anniversary and W Group marks its first year, the milestones highlight a transformation from a single trading platform into a diversified business connecting traditional finance, digital assets, and emerging technologies.

The scale of this growth is reflected in WhiteBIT’s $2.8 trillion trading volume in 2026 and more than 5,500 institutional clients. Meanwhile, W Group has expanded from eight to 11 companies in one year, growing its combined user base from 35 million to approximately 40 million.

Established in 2025, W Group brought together businesses developed around WhiteBIT under a broader strategy: connecting traditional finance and digital assets while extending their applications into new industries.

The group aims to build more than a portfolio of independent companies. By combining financial infrastructure, technology and expertise, its businesses can develop new services, reach new markets and create opportunities across industries.

In 2026, W Group expanded well beyond financial services. Dutch sports car manufacturer Spyker joined the group in June, opening opportunities to apply digital finance and blockchain technology to luxury automotive.

W Group has also made a $400 million investment commitment in SignSplit, the signed-data platform for the AI era. The seed round values SignSplit at $1 billion.

In banking, Georgia-based hashbank, a fully licensed digital bank, combines traditional banking services with cryptocurrency functionality, allowing customers to manage fiat and digital assets within one environment.

Together, these businesses illustrate W Group’s strategy of connecting financial services, technology, and consumer-facing products into an ecosystem where each company can contribute to the growth of the others.

WhiteBIT remains a central pillar of W Group, providing trading infrastructure and digital asset services for retail and institutional clients.

In 2026, the exchange recorded $2.8 trillion in trading volume, with derivatives generating approximately $3.5 billion in daily activity. Its platform now offers nearly 1,200 trading markets.

Trading conditions have also improved. Spot trading volume across WhiteBIT’s flagship BTC, ETH and WBT pairs increased more than fivefold, from $53 billion in 2020 to $276 billion in 2025. Meanwhile, bid-ask spreads across its major markets have narrowed approximately threefold since 2021, reflecting deeper liquidity.

WhiteBIT also serves more than 5,500 institutional clients, including funds, brokers and payment providers, through over 20 dedicated products and services. Businesses can access crypto liquidity, manage digital asset transactions and move between fiat currencies and cryptocurrencies through a single platform.

WhiteBIT’s eighth year brought significant regulatory milestones that strengthen the group’s international presence.

In June 2026, WhiteBIT EU secured authorisation under the EU’s Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation (MiCA) from Austria’s Financial Market Authority, establishing a regulatory foundation for providing crypto-asset services across the European Union.

In May, WhiteBIT launched a dedicated platform for users in the United Kingdom, expanding into another major financial market with demanding regulatory requirements. The company also joined Ghana’s regulatory sandbox in March, contributing to the development of the country’s digital asset regulatory framework.

These developments reflect WhiteBIT’s investment in compliance as a foundation for sustainable international growth.

The growth of the ecosystem is also reflected in WBT, WhiteBIT’s native coin, which reached an all-time high of nearly $88 in September 2026, up from its launch price of $1.62 in 2022.*The milestone reflects WBT’s strong long-term performance alongside the expansion of WhiteBIT and its ecosystem.

WBT’s role is also expanding beyond trading. In November, Whitechain transitioned into an Ethereum Layer 2 network built on the OP Stack in partnership with Optimism, with WBT used to pay transaction fees. Designed to support blockchain applications and financial services, Whitechain has already attracted interest from developers, with more than 140 teams applying to its Builders Program ahead of the network’s public launch planned for later in November.

For W Group, the next stage of growth will focus on connecting its businesses more closely and turning their combined capabilities into new products and services.

“Eight years ago, we started with one cryptocurrency exchange. Today, we’ve grown into an international group of 11 companies serving around 40 million users. But our ambition has never been simply to build more businesses. We want to connect them in ways that create real value — bringing traditional finance and digital assets closer together, making new technologies more accessible and opening opportunities across industries,” said Volodymyr Nosov, Founder and President of W Group.

Looking ahead, W Group aims to strengthen its presence in regulated markets, expand institutional financial services and accelerate blockchain infrastructure development. Its long-term vision is an interconnected ecosystem where banking, payments, digital assets and emerging technologies work together, making financial services more accessible to people and businesses worldwide.

#5123. Peter Brandt Predicts More Pain for Bitcoin

Veteran commodity trader Peter Brandt has provided yet another bearish outlook for the world's largest cryptocurrency, arguing that the crypto asset could see much more misery if its historical market trends play out.

Bitcoin Bitcoin Every bull market phase in BTC's history has ended with at least a 75% correction of the previous parabolic bull trend. Could this be what's happening in Bitcoin? https://t.co/PVXJpetSyO

Brandt's latest doomsday prediction comes at a time when Bitcoin is still trying to recover from its biggest sell-off to date, which has wiped out close to half of its market value from its October 2025 peak.

The trader's latest post on X highlights a pattern he has found throughout Bitcoin's price history: previous bull runs have always ended with devastating market corrections.

"Every bull market phase in the history of BTC has ended with at least a 75% correction of the prior parabolic bull trend," Brandt's X post reads.

Continuing his warning, Brandt raised an alarming question: "Is this what is happening with Bitcoin?"

The trading veteran has not put a number on the possible crash. But his words point toward the possibility that the current drop in Bitcoin might be part of a larger correction and not just a temporary dip.

A look at Bitcoin's historical crashes could paint a scary picture.

Bitcoin has a history of sharp corrections following major bull runs.

For example, the crypto asset dropped by around 84% from its December 2017 high to its December 2018 low. Another example of such a deep price correction can be found in the 2022 bear market, when BTC saw a drop of around 77% from its November 2021 all-time high.

These historic crashes have shown the extreme losses that Bitcoin investors have faced during such market corrections.

At the time of writing on Nov. 20, Bitcoin is currently trading at around $62,000, which is almost 51% below its October 2025 all-time high.

If it were to undergo a 75% price correction from its all-time high, then it would drop to around $31,550.

But it's worth mentioning that Brandt referred to a correction of the prior parabolic bull trend, which may not be the same as a 75% correction from the peak.

Interestingly enough, this is not the first time the trader has warned about an even darker future for Bitcoin's price.

Earlier, on Nov. 15, Brandt shared a weekly BTC chart showing a potential head-and-shoulders formation, a technical pattern that usually signals a bearish reversal.

According to Brandt, if the pattern is confirmed, there's a chance that Bitcoin could never revisit its previous all-time high in his lifetime.

The chart he shared also indicates an important zone between $60,000 and $65,000. A confirmed breakdown below this zone could further strengthen the bearish outlook.

Moreover, there is still plenty of selling pressure in the crypto market. The latest update from Lookonchain on Nov. 19 revealed that U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs had seen net outflows of around $975 million over the past seven days. The biggest net outflows came from BlackRock's iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT).

#5122. AI Will Make the Next 12 Months 'a Slaughterhouse' for Crypto Security: Anchorage Exec

AI Will Make the Next 12 Months 'a Slaughterhouse' for Crypto Security: Anchorage Exec

Crypto companies face a wave of AI-powered attacks over the coming year, on top of an already relentless threat from North Korea, security leaders warned at Solana's Breakpoint conference in London.

"The next 12 months are going to be absolutely brutal," said Diogo Mónica, co-founder of crypto bank Anchorage Digital. "They're going to be a slaughterhouse."

Over the past three to six months, AI systems have gone from finding vulnerabilities to building full exploits and carrying out every step of an attack on their own, Mónica said on a panel chaired by Gordon Corera, co-host of The Rest Is Classified podcast. Aggressive hacking tools now let anyone with no security knowledge simply tell a model to "please hack this website," he said.

Michael Coates, chief information security officer at the Solana Foundation, said AI has "dramatically lowered the cost and the barrier to hacking." Projects that once escaped attention will now be targeted opportunistically, he said.

North Korea remains the biggest threat to crypto firms, Coates said in a separate presentation. More than $13 billion in crypto has been stolen over the past decade, over $7.5 billion of it this year alone, with some $6 billion drained in a single breach: last month's hack of Binance, which the FBI has blamed on North Korea. "This is how they fund their programs in North Korea," he said.

Coates described the Binance attack as a chain of familiar tactics. A developer downloaded poisoned software from a sponsored search result, and an engineer later took a job interview on a work device and downloaded software as part of it. That gave the attackers a foothold to move through the network and reach signing services, which they used to authorize malicious transactions rather than steal the keys themselves, he said.

Fake job interviews are a "telltale tactic of North Korea," he said, along with poisoned tools and deepfake video calls. North Korean hackers have stolen $400 million using deepfake calls alone. When North Korean operatives can't get hired directly, they use people in the U.S. to take jobs on their behalf, then connect remote keyboards and mice so attackers can access the company's network, Mónica said.

The U.S. Treasury recently sanctioned 11 people over just such a North Korean IT worker scheme. Anchorage now onboards new staff in person at its U.S. office, Mónica added, and every employee works on a locked-down Chromebook, logging into remote machines for development, which he said removes 90% to 95% of its corporate attack surface.

Coates pointed teams to STRIDE, a free security framework the Solana Foundation launched in the spring. It sets out 18 core controls tailored for crypto, and protocols covering more than 74% of DeFi's total value locked on Solana, including 10 of the top 11, have gone through it, he said. The foundation is also using AI to reverse-engineer newly launched programs and find flaws before attackers do, which he said has prevented more than $5 million in potential losses in the past month.

Both executives warned against basic mistakes. Connecting email inboxes to AI agents invites prompt injection attacks, Mónica said, while Coates urged teams to "protect the keys," using hardware security keys for logins and properly configured multisig wallets, with plans for key rotation and loss. "That's where we see the attackers going," Coates said. "They're getting the entry points through the basics. They're pivoting around to get the keys, and that's where they're unleashing their fury."

#5121. XRP & XLM Hit Closest To ‘Value Area’ Among Major-Caps

Home » Altcoins » XRP & XLM Hit Closest To ‘Value Area’ Among Major-Caps

These two major-cap altcoin peers are hitting close to historically-undervalued territory: a highly-awaited bottom sign?

French analyst EGRAG compared the 4-hour charts of XRP, Ethereum, Hedera, Bitcoin, Solana, and Stellar to assess which assets are showing relative strength after recent lows.

The mainstream French crypto chart analyst’s review centers on price reaction, volume-profile location, and the distance remaining to each coin’s Value Area Low (VAL).

XRP is holding above its recent low without returning for a full retest. Solana is behaving in a similar fashion, maintaining its recovery structure. Stellar (XLM) is tracking XRP but appears slightly weaker. Ethereum (ETH) is retesting its lows, with a potential higher low developing.

Bitcoin (BTC) also retested and printed a slightly higher high. Hedera’s HBAR was retested with a lower high, the most shaky local structure among the six.

Distance to reclaim the VAL currently ranks as follows:

XRP and Stellar (XLM) are the nearest to re-entering their value areas. Bitcoin shows the more constructive local recovery structure among the larger-cap assets, even though it remains farther from its VAL than the two payment-focused names.

A double bottom is not confirmed simply because price holds above a prior low. The analysis treats bottoming as a process rather than a single candle. Early strength is marked by reclaiming the VAL.

Structural confirmation requires a break of the local high. Stronger bullish conviction needs a reclaim of the Point of Control (POC) accompanied by volume.

The 4-hour volume profiles show each asset still trading below or near the lower boundary of its recent value area. Acceptance back inside the value area, followed by a move toward the POC, would shift the local bias.

Until that occurs, the charts remain in a recovery attempt rather than a confirmed reversal.

#5120. Cardano’s $5M conference budget has a $1M problem — Details!

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The Cardano [ADA] Treasury Foundation wants 25 million ADA to host 12 events in 2027. Its last $22 million proposal (to buy copper) barely got any votes at all.

Hence, the question — Will this one fare any better?

On 16th November, the Cardano Foundation filed a proposal to pull 24,995,775 ADA from the network’s treasury. The money would pay for Cardano’s presence at 12 crypto events next year.

Some of the biggest names on the circuit are on the list. Consensus in Miami, TOKEN2049 in both Singapore and New York, Web Summit in Lisbon, and India Blockchain Week in Bangalore.

The run kicks off in February with the Africa Tech Summit in Nairobi.

$2 million will go towards sponsorships and $1.06 million for booths and equipment. Travel is said to cost $702K, while side events will account for $695,000.

According to the Foundation, none of it covers salaries or profit. Whatever isn’t spent goes back to the treasury.

However, here’s what’s tricky. The Foundation priced ADA at $0.20 to reach its $5 million budget. ADA, however, was valued at $0.15 at press time. At that price, the request is worth about $3.85 million. That is a gap of over $1 million.

For the budget to work as planned, ADA needs to climb by almost 30%. Not impossible, but far from guaranteed.

DReps, the delegates who vote on treasury spending, have until 20th December to decide.

Will 25 million ADA move the price? Probably not. There are about 37.6 billion ADA in circulation. So, this withdrawal will merely be a drop in the ocean.

However, it’s worth pointing out that the chart had bigger problems at press time.

ADA fell from $0.25 in October to $0.155 following a price drop on the 14th.

Is a pause imminent? We might be in the early stages, yes. The RSI fell to 21 after that drop, with the same since climbing to 32, just crossing above its average. The OI has also recovered from about $145 million to $153.5 million. Finally, the funding rates were positive yesterday.

On the contrary, the Chaikin Money Flow was negative, implying that sellers might still have a slight edge.

#5119. 'Doomed From the Start': President Trump's Meme Coin Hits New Lows After Midterms

'Doomed From the Start': President Trump's Meme Coin Hits New Lows After Midterms

President Trump's official meme coin has reached new lows since he suffered a major blow in the U.S. midterm elections. The token has dropped almost 40% since election day, and analysts warn that new congressional scrutiny could push it lower.

Over the weekend, the TRUMP coin touched $0.896, marking the lowest point the token had ever stooped. It has since bumped 8%, sitting at $0.965, which is still down almost 40% from election day and 98.7% from its all-time high of $73.43, set the day after it launched, per Decrypt data.

Luke Nolan, senior researcher at CoinShares, told Decrypt the midterms "added pressure to an already struggling token." Trading volume increased around the vote, and he believes some traders had bet on a Republican surprise win. He said there was a "visible unwind" that led to a "sharp drawdown" after the election results rolled out.

"Democrats take both chambers in January and plan to use their subpoena power on his crypto ventures. His net approval rating has almost never been lower," Max Shannon, senior associate at Bitwise Europe, told Decrypt. "It is still possible this continues to decline and, therefore, has a negative effect on these Trump-affiliated assets."

Other Trump-linked assets have also taken a hit. Since the election, World Liberty Financial's WLFI token has dropped 7.5%, per Decrypt data, and shares of the Trump Media & Technology Group have dropped 32%.

Trump launched TRUMP on January 17, 2025, three days before his second inauguration, which Shannon described as "close to the peak of his popularity." Since then, the coin has faced significant scrutiny over how much the President and his family have profited from it. (The First Lady following suit and launching her own meme coin didn't help matters.)

The president’s crypto ventures also became a sticking point for the Clarity Act, a bill that aimed to provide a formal legal structure for most cryptocurrency activity. Democrats scrutinized the Trump family's involvement in the crypto industry, which profited them more than $1 billion in a year.

A July draft would have barred public officials and employees, along with their spouses, from issuing or sponsoring digital assets while in office. That draft didn’t extend to officials’ children, including Don Jr. and Eric Trump, and the provision would have expired in January 2029.

The bill failed a key Senate procedural vote in September. Patrick Witt, former executive director of the President’s Council of Advisers for Digital Assets, later said the “political issue of ethics really consumed everything else.”

TRUMP has "no value accrual, and it has been left in the dust of this narrative," Shannon said. "Google Trends shows searches for $TRUMP essentially flatlining since June 2025."

Nolan agreed, saying the coin’s appeal was "largely speculative," with no clear link between holding it and sharing in any underlying business success. The most it offered, he said, was the exclusive dinner to the top holders, which was both popular and controversial, depending on who you asked.

"In my view, that was just an attempt to prop up the token without actually creating any long-term demand for it," he said.

Interest in these dinners has dried up too. Ahead of the third gala scheduled for the event of the month, 185 holders of the token were invited to register, but only 130 wallets showed on the final leaderboard. Those wallets held a combined $8.6 billion worth of TRUMP, according to Reuters, which is a 94% decline from the first dinner.

Then there's the "unappealing" token supply structure, Nolan said. The majority of tokens were allocated to Trump-linked entities and are being slowly unlocked, which means demand needs to keep growing to soak up newly injected supply.

"That is a difficult position for an asset whose main attraction is attention around one individual," he said.

And what happens when Trump loses power? He has said he "would love" to serve a third term, but that remains unconstitutional—so the clock is ticking on his presidency.

"I think overall Trump leaving office probably means the token continues its gradual decline," Nolan told Decrypt. "Any big Trump headlines probably make it experience the same dead cat bounces (sharp rallies) we have seen it have during short periods of time."

Shannon thinks Trump doesn't even need to lose power for the token to keep going down. He said that it is "incredibly unlikely" that the token reclaims its highs, as it would require it to boost 82x, too many investors have been burned, and the majority of the supply is still to be unlocked.

"I think the meme coin was doomed from the start, and like with most meme coins, creating long-term demand is a Sisyphean task," Nolan told Decrypt.

#5118. Tether invokes The Big Short in swipe at S&P over USDT reserve risks

S&P Global made no downward adjustment in its latest Stablecoin Stability Assessment, as 5 is the lowest available score.

S&P Global Ratings has kept Tether’s USDt at the lowest level on its stablecoin stability scale as riskier assets take up a growing share of the token’s reserves.

In a report published Nov. 18, S&P scored USDt’s ability to hold its dollar peg at 5 (weak). The assessment is unchanged from November 2025.

Higher-risk holdings, including gold, Bitcoin (BTC), public equities, secured loans and corporate bonds, were equivalent to 28% of USDt (USDT) in circulation as of Sept. 30, up from 24% a year earlier. “A significant drop in the value of these assets could lead to USDT being undercollateralized,” S&P said in the report.

“The problem is S&P’s framework, not our reserves,” Tether told Cointelegraph. “Their own history shows the underlying problem,” it added, referencing The Big Short, the film about the 2008 financial crisis.

Tether’s Sept. 30 reserve report, attested by BDO, showed approximately $191.5 billion in assets against $183.5 billion in liabilities, leaving roughly $7.95 billion in excess assets. The agency added that it found further weaknesses but made no downward adjustment “because our assessment cannot be lower than 5.”

Among the additional weaknesses highlighted by S&P was the regulatory oversight of El Salvador, where Tether relocated in January 2025.

According to the report, the National Commission of Digital Assets of El Salvador (CNAD), the regulatory body which oversees Tether in the country, requires 70% of the reserves to be held in assets that can be liquidated within 30 days. The company said they considered the framework “broad,” allowing for the inclusion of relatively high-risk assets like gold, BTC and loans.

The S&P also pointed to the lack of a requirement to segregate the reserve assets, unlike those expected by regulators in the US and Europe. As there is no publicly available information about segregation, the report states, there is no insight into how they are insulated from broader corporate risks.

USDT’s breakdown of reserve assets. Source S&P Global

In August 2026, KPMG confirmed they had issued an unqualified opinion on Tether’s 2025 accounts, in the company’s first “full independent audit”. The issuer highlighted that the audit supported the financial statements issued by the company.

However, S&P said the audited statements and the auditor’s report have not been published, which limits its ability to check the underlying information. Reserve reports also do not name the custodians and counterparties holding Tether’s assets, S&P said, and attestations remain quarterly while other stablecoins generally report monthly.

Related: Tether backs Bolivian fintech bringing USDT payments to food delivery and banking

At the time of S&P’s initial downgrade in 2025, Tether disputed the result. CEO Paolo Ardoino wrote on X, “we wear your loathing with pride,” pointing to past financial collapses as “proof” that traditional systems are broken.

Although the stability rating remained at 5, S&P ended the Nov. 18 report noting that Tether had maintained a good history of price stability, particularly in the last 12 months.

#5117. Morning Minute: Nvidia Beats, Board Says ‘No’ on Jensen

Morning Minute: Nvidia Beats, Board Says ‘No’ on Jensen

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

🖥️ Nvidia Beats, Board Says ‘No’ on Jensen

Six days after Jensen Huang resigned as CEO of the company he co-founded 33 years ago, Nvidia delivered the best quarter in its history. And told him he isn’t coming back.

Fourth-quarter guidance came in at $130 billion against the $125.3 billion Wall Street wanted, with China assumed at zero.

And the CFO raised their 2028 revenue outlook to 75% year-over-year growth.

Then board chair Harvey Jones got on the call and said it plainly. “The Nvidia board does not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO.” The directors unanimously accepted his resignation, and Jones declined to answer anything about the circumstances or the independent investigation still running.

Huang had publicly offered to come back hours earlier, saying he would do it if directors believed it served shareholders and employees. Trump and Elon Musk both backed the idea. A petition passed 182,000 signatures including nearly 9,000 self-identified Nvidia employees. None of it moved the room.

Nvidia has hired Heidrick & Struggles to run the permanent search. Colette Kress stays interim CEO while remaining CFO, with Jones saying the board has “every confidence in Colette’s leadership, integrity and ability to deliver.” The board also created a National Security and Export Control Oversight Committee, chaired by director Suzanne Nora Johnson, to receive reports from the company’s national security and trade compliance teams.

And after all of that—the stock rose about 3%.

A 6% revenue beat, guidance $5 billion above consensus, a new product cycle shipping, and the market gave it two percent. Shares sit roughly 35% below their October peak near $240. Nvidia perps on Hyperliquid were doing nearly a billion dollars of daily volume through the week, bouncing off $150 before settling near $162.

As for impact on the broader AI trade, Nvidia proved AI demand is not collapsing. You cannot manufacture $107.6 billion of data center revenue, and margins at 74.8% mean nobody is discounting to move product. The demand-collapse thesis died on Tuesday.

Two details do belong to the bears though. Growth is decelerating from 103% to roughly 77% once you strip the extra week out of Q4 guidance. And gross margin guides down about three points, from 74.8% to between 71% and 72%. Slowing growth plus compressing margins is how multiples come down regardless of how big the absolute numbers get.

The honest read is that the AI business is fine and the AI trade has stopped responding to it. For crypto that matters more than it should, because the correlation that dragged Bitcoin from $87,000 to the low $60s over six weeks ran through this exact trade.

Now we wait for the Fed on December 9 and insight into the 2027 rate hike cycle (or lack thereof) to determine the likely near-term fate.

#5116. Threshold’s 11-day Bitcoin redemption freeze exposes a hidden risk in BTC-backed tokens

The protocol held more Bitcoin than its outstanding tBTC supply, yet security patches and bridge failures temporarily blocked redemptions.

Threshold Network suspended tBTC redemptions for 11 days despite holding more Bitcoin than tokens in circulation.

The protocol said in a Nov. 18 post-mortem that its app stopped accepting redemption requests on Oct. 30 after unexpected bridge activity raised security concerns. Redemptions resumed Nov. 10 following technical fixes.

The interruption prevented holders from converting tBTC, a Bitcoin-backed token used across decentralized finance (DeFi), into native Bitcoin through Threshold's app. Users could still transfer and trade their existing tokens, while lending platform Aave restricted activity in its tBTC market.

However, the disruption did not appear to stem from insufficient Bitcoin reserves.

Threshold reported that its bridge wallets held 4,320.46 BTC against 4,185.155 tBTC in circulation on Oct. 31, leaving approximately 135 BTC more than the outstanding token supply.

Threshold attributed the interruption to separate technical and security issues that affected its ability to process Bitcoin transactions.

The team had identified a problem with how the bridge accounted for Bitcoin deposits before the suspension. Meanwhile, another security issue forced some node operators responsible for signing Bitcoin transactions to take their systems offline for patches.

Under Threshold's design, a bridge wallet requires signatures from more than half its designated signers to authorize a Bitcoin transaction. This meant withdrawals could not proceed until enough operators returned online.

The protocol also needed to restore Bitcoin transaction proof submissions, which allow the bridge to verify deposits and redemption payouts.

Threshold said it fixed the deposit-accounting issue and upgraded the bridge before its Council restored permission to submit Bitcoin proofs on Nov. 7.

The app subsequently reopened around 03:00 UTC on Nov. 10, allowing holders to initiate redemptions again.

Proof submission was reauthorized on Nov. 7. New redemption requests through the app resumed on Nov. 10.

The team also reviewed 57 registered tBTC wallet addresses and the bridge contracts for activity between Oct. 25 and Nov. 12.

It matched 31 BTC redemption payouts to their corresponding bridge proofs and reported finding no unexplained Bitcoin outflows in the wallets examined.

Essentially, the findings indicate the interruption was an operational failure rather than a depletion of BTC reserves.

However, the incident exposed a broader vulnerability for Bitcoin-backed assets used in DeFi: even when reserves are sufficient, technical failures can temporarily prevent holders from accessing their underlying BTC.

#5115. CLARITY Act Likely Dead Until 2027, Top Crypto Lawmakers Warn

House Financial Services Committee Chairman French Hill said the CLARITY Act is unlikely to pass before the year ends, leaving the crypto market structure bill to a new Congress that Democrats will control.

Hill spoke Thursday at the Capitol Crypto Forum in Washington. “We never give up hope in a lame duck, but it is lame, and therefore the chances are not high,” the Arkansas Republican said. He added that he plans to make market structure legislation his pitch in the next Congress, which begins in January.

“I regret that it was not something we could get done effectively in this Congress,” Hill said. He said 78 Democrats voted for the CLARITY Act in the House and that he expects that support to continue in the new Congress.

Hill also called it encouraging that Rep. Maxine Waters, the committee’s top Democrat, circulated a memo to House Democrats naming digital asset market structure as a topic she intends to work on if she becomes chair.

Sen. Bernie Moreno, the lead sponsor of the CLARITY Act in the Senate, told the same forum there is “no scenario” in which crypto legislation passes the chamber during the lame-duck session. Funding the government, the National Defense Authorization Act and the farm bill will take priority, according to The Hill, and the session is set to end in less than a month.

The Senate blocked the CLARITY Act on Sept. 15, voting 49-50 on a procedural motion that needed 60 votes. All Democrats and four Republicans opposed it, CBS News reported. Sen. Thom Tillis entered a motion to reconsider, which keeps the option of another vote alive, but the bill dies when this Congress ends Jan. 3 unless the lame-duck session revives it.

The House passed the legislation 294-134 in July 2025. President Donald Trump urged lawmakers in August to send it to his desk, but Democrats objected that its ethics provisions did not go far enough to address his potential conflicts of interest in crypto, and regulators have since moved ahead on their own, including an SEC exemption for tokenized stocks.

The industry is now scrambling to repair its relationship with Democrats, Politico reported Thursday. The industry spent $55 million trying to defeat Sen.-elect Sherrod Brown of Ohio, who won his seat back, and Democrats are set to retake both the House and Senate in January.

Sen. Ruben Gallego, a crypto-friendly Arizona Democrat who spent months negotiating the Senate bill, gave Politico’s Jasper Goodman a blunt assessment of the industry’s political prospects. “They’re f***ed,” Gallego said, according to a post from journalist Eleanor Terrett.

Terrett has reported that House Democrats are discussing turning Waters’ Crypto Investor and Consumer Bill of Rights into their own market structure bill. The proposal would give the SEC a bigger role and the CFTC a smaller one, and she cited three industry sources who have spoken with Democratic staffers. Republicans have called it a “messaging bill.”

Hill used the forum to warn about the gap between the two bills. Trump signed the GENIUS Act, the stablecoin law, in July 2025, but market structure legislation never followed. Hill said the SEC and CFTC cannot fill the gap with exemptive relief alone, because it does not give the market lasting legal certainty. “When you pass GENIUS without CLARITY, it’s like passing and creating a cell phone, but you have no cell network,” Hill said.

The stablecoin law takes effect Jan. 18, 2027, and Treasury is still working on its rules. Terrett reported Wednesday that Sens. Cynthia Lummis and Kirsten Gillibrand, joined by four other senators, asked Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent to extend a safe harbor to stablecoin issuers in states with rules in place before Jan. 18, 2028.

#5114. Swiss Bank Sygnum Says Bitcoin Could Reach New High Near $128,000

Sygnum Bank estimates that bitcoin could reach a new all-time high near $128,000. The Swiss digital asset bank laid out the scenario in its 2027 Sygnal outlook on Thursday, alongside a call that bitcoin and ether will both set new highs while the broader market stays narrow.

The report also frames the current downturn as the shallowest bear market for bitcoin to date. Sygnum puts the drawdown at around 54%, compared with declines of more than 70% in previous cycles, and says digital assets enter 2027 with lower structural volatility and stronger fundamentals.

The outlook arrives as bitcoin trades near $63,000 after six straight weekly losses and a drop below its 200-week moving average. Sygnum’s case is that the market has changed since earlier cycles, not that the slide is over.

Sygnum describes a more mature market. Volatility is increasingly driven by broader dollar liquidity and market structure, it said, and institutions have added new channels through ETFs, leveraged trading and crypto-backed credit. By the end of October, stablecoin supply stood near $298 billion and tokenized real-world assets exceeded $39 billion.

JUST IN: 🇨🇭 Switzerland's Sygnum Bank estimates that Bitcoin could hit a new high of $128,000. 👀Its 2027 outlook also calls this Bitcoin's "shallowest bear market yet". pic.twitter.com/KWyFALBAR8

The estimate rests on a rotation out of technology stocks. Digital assets are worth about 6% of the Nasdaq’s market value, and Sygnum calculates that if 0.75% of Nasdaq value, roughly $360 billion, moved into crypto, total market capitalization could rise by more than 60%.

In that scenario, bitcoin would reach a new high near $128,000. The report does not put a date on it and describes the figure as an estimate built on Sygnum’s own calculations, not a price target. It also notes that a sell-off in AI stocks could spill into crypto. Currently, Anthropic’s record $81 billion IPO is set to open about 25% to 30% above its $83 offering price, which has lifted AI and chip stocks, but worries about higher interest rates have pulled the Nasdaq back from an early 2% gain to about 0.3%.

Behind the price call is a shift in how institutions treat bitcoin. Sygnum predicts it will move out of the tactical sleeve of portfolios and into the “strategic reserve bucket,” competing with gold for capital that wants protection against currency debasement. BlackRock, Morgan Stanley and Bank of America each recommend allocations of 1% to 4%.

The report says 2027 should reward selection over broad market exposure. Sygnum expects adoption to concentrate in stablecoins, tokenized assets, bitcoin as an institutional macro asset and tokens with proven usage, and it believes investors will be rewarded for choosing assets with real usage and revenue.

The math is simple, according to the report. Bitcoin is worth about 6% of gold’s market value, so moving just 1% of gold’s value into it would lift its market capitalization by at least 17%.

Large holders have already shown that appetite. BlackRock has said security concerns are pushing bitcoin whales into its spot ETF, even as miners such as MARA have sold coins to raise cash.

Sygnum also expects tokens that return revenue to holders to outperform, stablecoin settlement at Visa to triple to $60 billion by the fourth quarter of 2027, and carry trades, which pair long spot with short futures, to beat Treasury bills. That comes after the Federal Reserve’s September rate rise to 3.75%–4.00% and 67 straight days of negative bitcoin funding rates in 2026. It describes regulation as clearer but still incomplete, with Europe’s MiCA regime now fully operational and U.S. agencies still advancing rules for crypto markets.

Fabian Dori, Sygnum’s chief investment officer and lead author, said volatility “has not disappeared; it has just migrated from the asset itself to its plumbing,” and that institutional adoption “has kept on building.”

“To understand the shape of 2027 markets, investors must look past price to fundamentals,” Dori said.

#5113. Fed officials saw October rate hold as a ‘close call,’ minutes show

The latest FOMC minutes suggest greater appetite for higher rates than the Fed’s 10–2 vote indicated, with markets now pricing in a December hike.

While the Federal Open Market Committee decided not to raise interest rates during its October meeting, some participants who supported holding rates steady considered the decision a “close call,” according to newly released minutes Wednesday.

The Federal Open Market Committee voted 10–2 in October to keep its benchmark rate at 3.75% to 4%. Fed Governor Lisa Cook and Cleveland Fed President Beth Hammack favored a quarter-point increase.

“The minutes suggest broader support for an October hike than the 10–2 vote alone implied — and that was before payrolls and CPI both came in hot,” Lewis Huang, an analyst at crypto exchange Bitget, told Cointelegraph.

Markets are now overwhelmingly expecting a December rate hike. CME FedWatch has put the odds of a December quarter-point hike at about 90% and a half-point increase at about 10% at the time of writing.

“With a December hike already fully priced in, the focus shifts to the size of the move and the policy path beyond December,” said Huang.

“Governor Waller has said a half-point increase should be considered; evidence that other officials were already open to that scale of tightening in October could push the odds of a 50-basis-point move higher,” he added.

Fed officials also raised concerns about ongoing inflationary pressures due to the AI build-out, with participants noting the demand for AI technology was pushing up the price of some consumer goods.

Related: Hot US inflation raises odds of half-point Fed hike in December

Officials separately discussed the financial risks if the returns on AI investments failed to live up to investor expectations.

“Most participants observed that AI-related debt had grown rapidly and that much of this growth had taken place through complex and opaque financing structures,” the minutes show. “A few participants remarked that AI-related exposures were becoming increasingly concentrated among a small number of firms.”

Fabian Dori, chief investment officer at Sygnum Bank, said markets are already pricing in a series of hikes into next year.

“That likely keeps long-term yields and the dollar firm, and the question for Bitcoin is whether debasement concerns and advancing institutional adoption can outweigh it,” Dori said.

#5112. Crypto’s $11 billion bet on Wall Street is running into a funding problem

Traders face sharply different funding costs for identical stock exposures, with Nvidia futures highlighting disparities between Binance and Hyperliquid.

In a Nov. 17 analysis, trading infrastructure firm Talos found that the funding-rate spread for perpetual futures of the same tokenized stock across venues averaged 23% for the 10 largest equity perpetuals by open interest.

Funding is the recurring payment traders exchange on opposite sides of a perpetual future. In Talos’s comparison, rates diverged even when the two contracts tracked the same asset, so the costlier venue one stretch can be cheaper another.

In a snapshot of settled Nvidia funding on Nov. 18, market data tracker PerpDexList showed cumulative settled funding for a long position at 0.3907% of notional over seven days on Binance.

Meanwhile, the equivalent figure for the Nvidia contract on Hyperliquid was 0.3347%.

Over 30 days, Binance longs paid a net 0.5834% and trade.xyz longs on Hyperliquid paid 0.6855%, showing what was paid on each contract before trading fees and any gains or losses on the position.

Cumulative funding paid by longs, as a percent of notional, in a Nov. 18, 2026 snapshot at 18:36 UTC. On Binance and trade.xyz, respectively, seven-day payments were 0.3907% and 0.3347%. Over 30 days they were 0.5834% and 0.6855%.

Source: PerpDexList Nvidia perpetual market snapshot

Binance Research’s Nvidia figures showed a 0.12% funding rate paid by longs on Nov. 16, after three smaller positive settlements on Nov. 12.

Talos puts open interest above $11 billion in November for perpetuals linked to traditional assets such as equities, indexes and commodities. That was about 18% of total open interest across the major venues it studied.

Binance and Hyperliquid together held roughly 77% of that segment, with shares of 39% and 38%, respectively.

To keep perpetual futures prices near the assets they track, venues use recurring payments between traders on either side. When funding is positive, traders betting on a rise pay those betting on a fall, and shorts pay longs when it turns negative.

The exchange can charge a separate trading fee for funding, so the amount transferred depends on the position’s notional value, which can exceed the collateral a trader has put up.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

An eight-hour window lines up the possible four- and eight-hour Binance schedules with hourly settlement on Hyperliquid.

The same holding period can include different numbers of funding payments on different venues.

The markers show timing only. Actual contract schedules and rates should be checked for the period being compared. The illustration does not show which side pays or the size of a payment.

Place scheduled payments at elapsed hours 4 and 8 for Binance’s four-hour schedule, hour 8 for its eight-hour schedule, and hours 1 to 8 for Hyperliquid. Hour zero is the start of an illustrative window.

The markers show timing only. Actual contract schedules and rates should be checked for the period being compared. The illustration does not show which side pays or the size of a payment.

When demand pushes the contract above its reference price on one venue, the premium used to calculate its funding can rise there. A second venue can have a different order balance and a different reference price, even though both contracts track Nvidia.

Under Binance’s TradFi contract rules, funding generally settles every four or eight hours, and the interest-rate input for stock contracts is zero. Outside the underlying market’s hours, Binance can derive the index from prices in its own order book and apply smoothing.

In October, Binance said it would omit disrupted overnight observations from one funding calculation for its US equity perpetuals after connectivity problems.

The Hyperliquid funding rules call for hourly transfers. In the builder-deployed markets that include trade.xyz, the premium compares the midpoint of two order-book prices, measured for a specified trade size, with the market’s reference price, known as an oracle price.

The trade.xyz funding formula applies a multiplier before the hourly payment. Separate books, reference prices, and funding inputs can all contribute to the spread. Talos’s comparison gives the size of the gap, while the exchange rules show how a gap can arise.

A trader could short the contract where funding pays more and go long on the other venue, since holding offsetting positions reduces exposure to stock price moves.

The funding difference can still shrink or reverse before a trader closes the positions, and the two futures prices can move apart while the stock exposure is hedged.

In its Nov. 18 snapshot, PerpDexList put the median cost of opening a $100,000 Nvidia position on trade.xyz at 2.25 basis points over the previous 24 hours, including the taker fee and slippage, the difference between the expected and actual execution price.

A trader also has to account for the other venue, the cost of closing both positions and the possibility that limited depth makes a larger order more expensive. Castle Labs’ venue study measured fees and slippage at multiple trade sizes and sampled weekday and weekend sessions separately for traditional-asset perpetuals.

If one position moves against the trader, gains on the other side sit in a separate account. The losing leg can still be liquidated if its margin falls too low.

A wider funding gap gives traders a reason to take the other side, but they need enough return to carry both positions through those risks.

#5111. Movie2K Defendant Could Hand Over 40,000 Bitcoin to End Trial—and Stay a Billionaire

Movie2K Defendant Could Hand Over 40,000 Bitcoin to End Trial—and Stay a Billionaire

The alleged operator of defunct movie piracy site Movie2K could walk away from his trial in Germany and remain a billionaire, under a deal offered by the judge in the case.

The presiding judge at the Leipzig Regional Court has offered to drop tax evasion and money laundering charges against the 43-year-old main defendant if he hands over 40,000 BTC, public broadcaster MDR reported last week. He would also have to pay €38 million ($42 million) in compensation for potential victims, according to MDR.

Saxony's Office of the Public Prosecutor General confirmed the offer to news agency dpa, tech outlet heise reported on Tuesday. The 40,000 BTC would be worth around €2.36 billion ($2.6 billion) at current prices, according to heise.

But prosecutors believe the defendant holds a total of about 56,000 BTC. If he accepts, he could keep around 16,000 BTC, worth roughly €940 million ($1 billion) today and more than €1 billion when the offer was made.

A co-defendant has been offered a deal to hand over 600 BTC and pay €17 million ($19 million), and his lawyer said she could accept it, according to MDR. The main defendant's lawyers are considering the offer. The trial resumes on December 3 and could end before Christmas.

Movie2K offered hundreds of thousands of pirated films before shutting down in 2013. Its operators made money from advertising and subscription traps, and invested much of it in Bitcoin when it traded far below today's prices.

The copyright violations are time-barred, so the trial, which began in February, has focused on money laundering and tax evasion. The judge has reportedly expressed doubts about proving some of the charges, in part because legally and illegally acquired Bitcoin may have been mixed in the defendant's accounts, heise reported.

The main defendant was arrested abroad in 2023, and in early 2024 handed over nearly 50,000 BTC. Authorities in Saxony sold the coins later that year, raising about €2.64 billion ($2.9 billion), which is held in a state judicial treasury account. The money still belongs to the defendant until there is a final ruling, according to heise. Germany's government was left holding less than $300 million in Bitcoin after the sales.

Blockchain analytics firm Arkham said last year that German authorities had failed to seize some 45,000 BTC linked to the site.

The offer comes days after German lawmakers approved new powers allowing customs officials to seize assets, including crypto, of doubtful origin without first opening a criminal case. Under that law, owners of significant assets would have to show they were lawfully obtained.
