#5076. Inside Bukele’s Casa Presidencial Dinner: Bitcoiners Discuss Energy, Immigration, and El Salvador’s Future

On November 10, 2026, President Nayib Bukele hosted roughly 30 Bitcoiners at Casa Presidencial during Bitcoin Histórico. Guests included Ricardo Salinas Pliego, Parker Lewis, Mike Peterson, and miners; discussions covered power generation for compute and immigration.

This November 10, in the midst of the Bitcoin Histórico conference taking place in the Historical Center of San Salvador, a group of Bitcoiners representing a wide range of sectors within the industry met with President Bukele.The dinner was organized by The Bitcoin Office, which is part of the Office of the President, and was led by Stacy Herbert and Max Keiser. The dinner went on from about 7:30 pm to 10:30 pm local time and included about thirty Bitcoiners, many regular visitors to El Salvador.

Among the guests was billionaire Ricardo Salinas Pliego, founder of Grupo Salinas, which owns Banco Azteca. Salinas is a known Bitcoiner, who has made public appearances with Keiser and Herbert in the past, and is speculated to be planning a run at the next Mexican Presidential election. Salinas met privately with the President besides the dinner and reportedly gifted him volumes one and two of Manuscripts of the Conquistadors, works curated and edited by Jorge Nikaido, Chief of Staff of Grupo Salinas.

Representing investors and founders were Parker Lewis, founder of Unchained Capital, Mike Peterson of Bitcoin Beach fame, Katie Ananina of Citizen X, and Aleks Svetski, entrepreneur and founder of Satlantis. Bitcoin miners also attended, such as  Beau Turner,  CEO of  Abundant Mines, Nacho Pauls, CCO of OCEAN Mining, and Adam Ortolf of Denver Bitcoin mining. Jon Atack, a Bitcoin core contributor, was also at the dinner alongside engineer Giacomo Zucco of Plan B Network, and AI tech entrepreneurs Ginger Gaetz of the ANIA AI initiative and Marc Seal, Founder of Aevonix.The academic and media side of the industry was also heavily represented, with none other than Michael Goldstein, also known as Bitstein and co-founder of the Nakamoto Institute. Eric Yakes, economist and author of The 7th Property, was there, as well as Efrat Feningson, independent journalist and podcaster. Local Bitcoiner Michael Ruiz also attended the dinner; he is the founder of Build in El Salvador (BIS), an increasingly popular kind of business club and Bitcoin meetup mix.

The guests arrived at the Casa Presidencial earlier in the evening and were chatting and networking while waiting for the president. Max Keiser, always a showman, arrived at the Casa Presidencial with a gift some might speculate carried a magical artefact. Photographed above, the case was said to contain a Stetson El Presidente cowboy hat, though some have speculated it might in fact have carried the Philosopher’s Stone.

Upon arrival, Bukele shook everyone’s hand and quickly invited them to take their assigned seats, dine, and relax- no big speeches or over-the-top formalities, Ananina told Bitcoin Magazine, describing the dinner as a kind of open mic.

“President Bukele’s table was adorned with bitcoin orange florals,” Herbert told Bitcoin Magazine; “we gathered anarchists, billionaire tycoons, Wall Street bankers, circular economy activists, VC investors, bitcoin miners, and content creators” at the country’s equivalent of the White House.

A three-course meal began to be served, with steak — of course — being the main course, followed by a three-layered chocolate dessert. In between were three hours of conversation which left a very strong impression on the individuals interviewed on the night. Bukele had his own microphone, while a couple of other mics were passed around. Six to seven major questions were asked throughout the night.

At some point, energy generation came up, and Salinas in particular engaged the topic with the President.

Baeu Turner, one of the three bitcoin miners at the dinner, told Bitcoin Magazine that Bukele described El Salvador’s electricity generation as “being roughly matched to the country’s current demand.” Bukele described plans to add more than a gigawatt of additional generation across multiple projects, including geothermal and generation using imported liquefied natural gas.

“What stood out to me,” added Turner, “was how directly he connected those development plans with demand for compute. Bukele said they can develop this power knowing there are users who can put it to work, whether that’s Bitcoin mining or other data centers. The demand could come from the Salvadoran government’s own operations or from outside companies bringing equipment and investment into the country.”

Other miners responded positively to that idea, including Salinas, who has deep investments and experience with energy production. His Grupo Dragón is involved in geothermal, wind, solar, and natural-gas generation across Mexico.

Immigration was also discussed. Spurred by Max Keiser’s enthusiasm about the country, where he roughly said that the world should recognize El Salvador’s arc and that people — bitcoiners presumably — should migrate; suddenly the topic was set on the table. Ruiz, whose family is from the region but was born and raised in the U.S., moved to El Salvador three years ago and has become a recognizable figure in the local Bitcoin community. He followed up with a question about immigration, in particular about gentrification and possible resentment from the local population towards Western migrants, pointing to very negative online comments about some Bitcoiners, such as being called “colonizers”.

Bukele asked back, “When you go out, walk around, and interact with Salvadorans, what are the interactions like?”, The question, to anyone that’s spent time in El Salvador in recent years, answers itself. Salvadorians are very welcoming, Ruiz told Bitcoin Magazine; “there’s a lot of love. People receive us with open arms, and they’re glad we’re here, and they’re happy”.

Immigration in El Salvador actually tends to be from regional countries like Honduras, Nicaragua, Guatemala, and Colombia, with smaller numbers reported from the United States. Bukele went on to dismiss concerns about immigration, joking that you could host the whole population of the world in El Salvador by matching the population density of Manhattan. This was not a statement meant to be taken as a goal or ideal, but rather to set the topic in context. He added that the country is obviously looking for the most capable and intelligent people, and even said that “all great empires were built with immigrants’ help”, which says a lot about the President’s mindset on the topic.

In response to concerns about gentrification, the President told the story of a fisherman and house caretaker who got a raise from $300 to $800 a month from an American who bought the property. As a result, the fisherman was able to buy a motor for his boat, which he used to fish and row by hand before that. The scale-up of his fishing business was so productive that the caretaker even got his son a boat, doubling his fishing fleet. The only people who complained about this growth in wages were the neighbours, so the story goes, who must have been feeling the pressure from their own villa’s caretakers, wanting a raise from meager wages.

At some point Efrat asked, “How come you are criticized around the world despite El Salvador’s GDP growth, a strengthening economy and popularity at home, which I saw firsthand talking to locals and visiting some of the areas once worst hit by gang violence?”

“Because I’m not part of their club,” the President allegedly responded, “I’m not the kind of ruler they like”. He added that there are evil people in the world who use mainstream media to criticize leaders like him and “call him names”, central bankers, including the ECB, came up as one of the forces behind the negative media campaign against Bukele.Beyond the clear hit pieces, he noted that many people in governments or the international political establishment are not evil per se, but are often ‘order followers’, who operate on certain narratives and resist new paradigms.

The recent wins for the country in terms of education reform were also discussed. Bukele allegedly said that many private schools are now integrating similar AI tutoring programs as the one led by his administration. The results made international news when the pilot program deployed in public schools over the past couple of years brought PISA test results to par with Germany and Switzerland.

When asked about their reflections on the presidential dinner and the Bitcoin Histórico conference in general, Max Kaiser shared his vision in a manner that merits being printed in full:

“Bitcoin Histórico promised two days of Excellence Only, as the Bitcoin Office likes to say, and it delivered big time. The National Palace was a regal setting to showcase the Renaissance 2.0 taking shape in El Salvador with presentations by the best Bitcoin minds and builders in the world. Audience members felt like they had been transported to a time and place found nowhere else in the world right now, where hope and happiness are in abundance. No doom.”

Stacy Herbert, whose presence and attention to detail were felt throughout the festivities, told Bitcoin Magazine, “We promoted Bitcoin Histórico as an elevated bitcoin conference experience with Philosopher King meets Renaissance 2.0 vibes. In every detail, my team and I achieved that, including with the official Bitcoin Office dinner at Casa Presidencial.” She added that guests were free to ask any question they wanted, and that no topic was off limits.

“They grilled the President for several hours. His wit, his charm, his intelligence – all unscripted – was there for all to see firsthand. And they will have walked away understanding why it is that El Salvador is winning under his leadership.” Herbert added. As far as attendees to the dinner who Bitcoin Magazine spoke with, both claims remain very much corroborated.

#5073. Bitcoin carry trades could beat Treasury bills in 2027, Sygnum predicts

Sygnum predicts that Bitcoin carry trades may offer higher returns than Treasury bills in 2027, following a sharp drop in funding rates that made the strategy less compelling in 2026.

Bitcoin carry trades could regain their edge over US Treasury bills in 2027 as demand for leverage recovers, according to digital asset bank Sygnum.

In its 2027 Sygnal Outlook report, Sygnum forecasts that the annualized premium on three-month Bitcoin (BTC) futures at the Chicago Mercantile Exchange would average more than 2% above three-month US Treasury bill yields next year.

A crypto carry trade involves buying Bitcoin while betting against its price through futures contracts. This helps limit exposure to Bitcoin’s price swings while also allowing traders to collect funding payments or futures premiums.

Fabian Dori, chief investment officer at Sygnum, told Cointelegraph that returns could recover as investors become more willing to take risks and fewer hedge funds compete for the same profits.

“The basis trade had become very crowded, with many hedge funds running it through spot ETFs [exchange-traded funds] against short CME futures,” Dori said. “Positioning data indicates that those funds have now unwound much of their CME short positions, so there is less capital competing for the same premium.”

Sygnum cited a 67-day stretch of negative Bitcoin funding rates in 2026. It also comes as crypto tech and infrastructure company Talos recently published research showing how much returns have fallen.

In its latest report, Talos said Bitcoin funding rates and futures premiums, which reached peak annualized levels of 20%-40% in early 2024, dropped to just 3%-5% this year. The current three-month US Treasury bill yield is around 4.25%, which means traders can earn about the same return from government debt without taking on the extra risks of crypto trading.

Bitcoin perpetual funding rates and the 30-day futures basis, annualized, 2024-2026. Source: Talos CM Market Data Pro

Sygnum’s forecast now raises the question of whether Bitcoin carry trades can make enough money to justify the risks, such as sudden drops in returns and higher trading costs. And while returns have started to improve, it’s unclear whether that will continue into 2027.

Bitcoin carry trades became popular after the launch of US spot Bitcoin ETFs in 2024, which gave larger investors an easier way to use the strategy. But as more investors entered the market, the returns available from these trades began to fall.

“A more mature market pays less for the same trade,” Dori said. “But it also attracts additional investors, which may increase the demand for leverage.”

According to Talos, Bitcoin funding rates turned negative briefly in the second quarter of the year. This meant that traders betting against Bitcoin through perpetual futures had to pay traders betting on higher prices — as opposed to collecting payments.

Related: Bitcoin ETF inflows leave institutional demand unclear: CoinShares

Still, there have been signs of a recovery: Talos found that crypto funding rates have started to rise again since August, potentially supporting Sygnum’s prediction. Coinbase also said in a Nov. 2 report that Bitcoin funding rates rose from around 4% annualized at the start of October to an average of 8.9% annualized in the last week of the month.

Coinbase questioned whether the increase reflected meaningful demand for leveraged Bitcoin trades; it cited weaker buying activity in the spot market.

“It’s less clear to us that the late rise in BTC leverage reflects conviction, given how little support it had from spot holders or the options market,” Coinbase’s report reads.

Sygnum expects wider institutional adoption to support demand for leveraged Bitcoin exposure in 2027. It pointed to factors such as growing institutional investment in Bitcoin, the growth of tokenization and rising demand for AI.

However, Dori warned that a sharp market downturn, a shortage of liquidity, or interest rates staying higher for longer could prevent carry trade returns from recovering. He also pointed to risks that come with increased leveraged trading. Even a hedged position can face margin calls, requiring traders to post more collateral or close their futures position.

“Carry can pay steadily for long periods and then turn quickly,” he said. “When positioning gets crowded and funding reverses, the unwind is typically fast, because the same leverage that pays the carry forces the exit.”

#5072. Google's Genie 4 Is Here, Letting Ultra Subscribers Build AI Worlds in Real Time

The company announced Wednesday that its latest world model is rolling out through Project Genie, a browser-based experiment for generating interactive environments. Genie 4 was unveiled in October, but access until now had been limited to trusted testers.

Instead of rendering a finished video from a prompt, Genie generates the scene as someone explores it. With the fourth model, users can type instructions midway through a session to change the weather, introduce a character, or steer what's happening without starting over.

Genie 4 also creates sound alongside the visuals, from wind and footsteps to dialogue that reacts to events in the scene. Google says the model improves the way people move and objects respond to physical forces, though those claims come from its own demonstrations rather than independent testing.

Genie has come a long way from the side-scrolling experiments Google showed in 2024. The first version learned from more than 200,000 hours of gameplay footage and could turn a single image into a simple playable 2D environment, as Decrypt reported at the time.

Those early sessions lasted just 60 seconds, and character controls or physical behavior could be unreliable. Google is now keeping Genie 3 available alongside the new model, with sessions extended to three minutes, support for 360-degree panorama images, and the ability to connect multiple generated worlds into longer journeys.

That three-minute limit applies to the updated Genie 3 experience; Google did not specify a comparable session length for Genie 4 in Wednesday's announcement. Users can choose between the models depending on whether they want Genie 4's stronger visuals and audio or Genie 3's longer explorations.

The technology is not the same as a traditional game engine that builds a stable level with fully defined rules. Genie predicts what a world should look and sound like as it changes.

Google acknowledges that objects can drift or change between views, complex physics remain difficult, and generated speech or text may be inaccurate. It says Project Genie's audio and video carry SynthID watermarks and that inputs and outputs undergo safety screening.

#5069. CryptoQuant says bitcoin is back in a bearish phase but has reached a ‘value zone’

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Onchain and technical indicators now place bitcoin back in a bearish phase, CryptoQuant said, noting that bitcoin demand growth is contracting at the fastest pace since July.

Bitcoin is back in a bearish phase, CryptoQuant said, noting that it has fallen below its 200-day and 365-day moving averages at $72,900 and $75,900, respectively.

Bitcoin hit a four-month low of about $61,000 on Nov. 15, wiping out the gains from a rally that began on Aug. 19, the onchain analytics firm said in a Tuesday report. Bitcoin is currently trading around $62,500.

"From a technical perspective, trading below both averages places Bitcoin back in the bearish regime," Julio Moreno, CryptoQuant's head of research, wrote in the report. "The averages that acted as support after the way up become resistance after the way back, and Bitcoin will hardly sustain a large rally until it crosses above them again."

Total bitcoin demand growth, including spot and perpetual futures, is contracting at the fastest pace since July 1, CryptoQuant said, noting that it needs to grow for bitcoin to recover and rally sustainably.

Total demand growth has fallen below −400,000 BTC on a 30-day basis, the weakest since the start of July, the firm noted.

"Demand growth has to stop contracting before a durable recovery can start. Price rebounds that are not accompanied by a turn in spot demand have little to stand on," Moreno said. "The first constructive sign would be the 30-day total moving back toward zero, led by spot buying rather than by new leverage in the perpetual futures markets."

Bitcoin holders also realized a net loss of 25,800 BTC during this recent sell-off, the largest daily reading since July 13, CryptoQuant said, noting that realized losses confirm that the decline is being driven by holders selling at a loss, not only by leverage.

Crypto analysts have also turned most bearish since November 22, 2025, with only 58% of analysts remaining bullish on bitcoin, compared to 77% on October 7, according to CryptoQuant's Bitcoin Analyst Consensus Index.

Meanwhile, CryptoQuant's Bull Score Index stands at 10 out of 100, an "extreme bear" phase, indicating "there are no conditions for a sustainable price recovery and rally."

As its price declines, bitcoin has entered a "value zone," as traders' unrealized profit/loss margin has fallen to −20%, the most negative since July 1, according to CryptoQuant.

"A −20% margin says Bitcoin is cheap relative to traders' cost basis, not that the decline is over," Moreno said. "Value zones define where bottoms form, and bottoming formations tend to last at least two months. With demand growth contracting and the Bull Score Index at 10, the conditions for a sustained recovery are not yet in place."

CryptoQuant also pointed to $62,000 to $65,000 as a range where long-term holders sold heavily and new investors bought. The firm said those buyers tend to defend the prices they paid, which could help bitcoin hold that range.

ANTon gives holders exposure to ANT share prices, but holders will not have direct ownership of the stock. It will join more than 450 other tokenized stocks and ETFs on Ondo Stocks, which the company says has passed $1 billion in total value locked.

#5061. Binance obtains $6.5 billion freezing order from Abu Dhabi court in bid to recover stolen crypto

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The Abu Dhabi court orders can be served onchain and provide a legal path for partners to return frozen funds, Binance said.

Crypto exchange operator Binance said it has obtained a $6.5 billion worldwide freezing order from the Abu Dhabi Global Market Courts as it seeks to recover crypto stolen in its Oct. 29 hack.

Justice William Stone SBS KC issued the order against unidentified people accused of carrying out the attack, Binance said Wednesday in a statement shared with The Block. The $6.5 billion is the amount covered by the injunction. Binance previously said about $2.85 billion of the roughly $6.34 billion in assets affected by the hack had been frozen or intercepted.

The court also granted interim orders over specific stolen assets and orders requiring assets to be handed over. Some of the interim orders apply to named defendants that have already frozen stolen assets. Binance does not allege wrongdoing by those parties.

Binance said some issuers, exchanges and other partners require a court order before they can release or return funds they have frozen. The orders can be served via blockchain, and the market will be notified as the list of wallets identified as holding stolen funds is updated.

Law firm Gibson Dunn is representing Binance in its efforts to recover crypto assets stolen in the hack. M. Kendall Day of Gibson Dunn called the $6.5 billion injunction "one of, if not the, largest publicly known freezing orders ever made."

"Alongside the worldwide freezing order against the perpetrators, Gibson Dunn has secured proprietary injunctions and delivery up orders on behalf of Binance to recover what was taken. Just as significant is how the orders will work in practice: they can be served via the blockchain, and the market will be notified of a constantly updating ‘living list’ of 26,000 wallet addresses identified as holding stolen assets, enabling those assets to be frozen rapidly even as they are laundered at pace," Day said.

Binance said it continues to work with law enforcement, issuers, exchanges and blockchain intelligence firms to trace and recover the stolen funds.

"We will continue using every lawful tool available to trace, freeze and recover stolen assets, support our partners in returning funds where possible, and keep those assets out of the hands of the attackers," said Dugan Bliss, deputy general counsel at Binance.

Tom Robinson, co-founder and chief scientist at blockchain analytics firm Elliptic, previously told The Block that frozen stablecoins could technically be reissued to Binance, but issuers would first want "a clear legal basis," such as a law enforcement request or a court order confirming the rightful owner. He said that process could take weeks or months. At the time, Robinson estimated that about $3.3 billion remained in bitcoin, ether, and XRP, "which no one can freeze."

Robinson also said tools that trace funds across blockchains help investigators flag them to exchanges and issuers faster. "Each time the funds move, there is a chance to catch them," he said.

#5059. Bitcoin retests $62K as Nasdaq futures rise ahead of Anthropic debut

Bitcoin climbed back above $63,500 after its second dip to $62,000 this week, while Anthropic priced its record $81.3 billion IPO at $83 a share.

Bitcoin (BTC) rebounded from $62,000 on Thursday as US stock futures rose after Anthropic priced its record $81.3 billion IPO.

BTC/USD fell to $62,000 shortly before Wednesday’s daily close, testing the level for the second time this week, TradingView data showed.

BTC/USD one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Anthropic priced 980 million shares at $83 each on Wednesday, above its initial range of $70 to $78. The $81.3 billion offering surpassed the $75 billion record SpaceX set in June. Anthropic shares are set to begin trading Thursday on the Nasdaq under the ticker ANT.

Nasdaq 100 futures were up about 1.1% early Thursday. In Seoul, the Korea Composite Stock Price Index (KOSPI) briefly reached 5,100.

KOSPI one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

“All of us have a sincere conviction that the AI exponential will generate so much value that, in the long run, it will far outweigh any shorter-term economic sacrifices we make as part of our mission,” Anthropic wrote in its founders’ letter.

Bitcoin saw modest relief from sell-side pressure during the Asia trading session, returning to circle $63,500.

Related: Here’s what happened in crypto today

Wednesday saw net outflows of $306.5 million from the US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs), the largest single-day net outflow since Nov. 6, according to Farside Investors.

US spot Bitcoin ETF netflows (screenshot). Source: Farside Investors

Older coins were also moving at a loss, according to an analysis by XWIN Japan published on CryptoQuant.

The analysis tracked Bitcoin that had stayed put for more than 155 days. Over the seven days through Tuesday, those coins moved at prices about 6% lower, on average, than when they last moved.

Bitcoin LTH-SOPR chart. Source: CryptoQuant

Moving Bitcoin between an investor’s own wallets also counts as a transfer, so some of those coins may never have been sold.

Short-term holders also moved coins at a loss. Their ratio fell to 0.955 on Monday, its lowest since February, Cointelegraph previously reported.

#5056. Senators: Treasury must ensure ‘equal footing’ for stablecoin issuers in GENIUS Act implementation

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Some states, like New York and Wyoming, have comprehensively developed rules for digital assets and stablecoins over the years.

A bipartisan group of senators is pressing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent to grant a safe harbor for stablecoin issuers operating under state regulatory frameworks, saying without it, state regulators would not be on equal footing with federal regulators.

In a letter to Bessent, Sens. Cynthia Lummis, R-Wyo., Kirsten Gillibrand, D-N.Y., Angela Alsobrooks, D-Md., Pete Ricketts, R-Neb., Kevin Cramer, R-N.D., and Mark Warner, D-Va., voiced their concerns about that disparity as federal agencies work on implementing a law called the Guiding and Establishing National Innovation for U.S. Stablecoins, or GENIUS, which created a federal regulatory framework for stablecoins.

The law allows stablecoin issuers with $10 billion or less to be regulated by states as long as that state's rules are "substantially similar" to what the federal government has in place. The law includes language about a January 2028 deadline where states must submit certifications that they are similar to federal standards. Treasury should also create a safe harbor for state issuers during that transition before the deadline, the lawmakers said, pointing to language in place that allows issuers that are awaiting the federal government's sign-off to get a safe harbor.

"Treasury’s current proposal would offer stablecoin issuers with pending applications to federal regulators a safe harbor as the GENIUS Act requirements come into effect, if such a regulator grants a waiver, without a corresponding protection for state issuers," the lawmakers said.

Some states, like New York and Wyoming, have comprehensively developed rules for digital assets and stablecoins over the years. Those states and others would be considered "substantially similar" to the federal law, they said.

"For Treasury to finalize this proposal in a way that reflects Congressional intent, it must allow issuers to choose from state or federal charters, as contemplated by the GENIUS Act, without the risk of business disruption or uncertainty," the lawmakers added.

Sens. Lummis, Cramer, Alsobrooks and Gillibrand previously wrote to Bessent in June asking for the Treasury Department to preserve states' ability to regulate stablecoins and to set a definitive timeline for stablecoin issuers to be issued by states, creating uncertainty, they said at the time.

Federal agencies are still working to implement the GENIUS Act, with the Treasury's Office of the Comptroller of the Currency the first out of the gate with its final rule set to go into effect in January.

#5052. Standard Chartered, StraitsX, OCBC complete trial of tokenized deposit interoperability

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The examination tested a shared model where StraitsX’s settlement infrastructure coordinated payments between OCBC's and Standard Chartered's separate tokenized deposit ecosystems.

StraitsX, OCBC and Standard Chartered have completed a proof-of-concept examining interoperability between tokenized deposit networks issued by different banks.

According to the press release, the POC was conducted under the Monetary Authority of Singapore's BLOOM initiative, a public-private collaborative project to develop future payment settlement infrastructure.

The examination tested a shared model where StraitsX's settlement infrastructure coordinated payments between OCBC's and Standard Chartered's separate tokenized deposit ecosystems.

"Tokenized deposits have the potential to enable near-instant and programmable money movement, but their broader utility will depend on how effectively different banking ecosystems can connect," said Tianwei Liu, CEO and co-founder of StraitsX. "We are exploring how shared settlement infrastructure can support that interoperability while reducing the need for bilateral arrangements and prefunded liquidity between individual participants."

The companies pointed out that tokenized deposits currently represent claims solely against their issuing banks and therefore cannot move directly between ecosystems that may use different platforms or standards.

The POC addressed this issue by coordinating payment initiation, compliance screening, liquidity checks, settlement and confirmation across the two banks' systems. This arrangement reduced the need for bilateral prefunding compared with conventional models, the release said.

While tokenized deposits enable around-the-clock deposit transfers, the companies pointed out that different ecosystems need to be aligned to fully unlock that potential.

In controlled tests, the parties completed U.S. dollar payment flows outside standard banking hours, demonstrating that interoperable infrastructure could allow "always-on" value transfer across different ecosystems within minutes.

Any further development, as well as commercial implementation, would require assessment of legal, regulatory, operational and commercial arrangements, including settlement finality, liquidity management, participant responsibilities and financial-crime controls, the companies said.

StraitsX holds a Major Payment Institution license under the MAS and issues the XSGD and XUSD stablecoins. OCBC is Singapore’s longest-established bank and the second-largest financial services group in Southeast Asia by assets.

#5049. The 9 Best Socks for Men, Tested by Style Editors

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

Put your best foot forward with a pair that combines form and function.

SOCKS MAY BE an afterthought, but comfort starts from the ground up. Your feet carry you all day, and the right pair keeps them warm, dry, blister-free, and supported—whether you're running miles, commuting to the office, or dressing up in loafers. The best socks stay put, wick away moisture, prevent hot spots, and hold shape long after laundry day. And we would know: The Men’s Health team has been testing since 2018—well before the pandemic reset everyone’s wardrobe—wearing them to the gym, on hikes, across long travel days, and through weekend outings. Check out the criteria we considered, or use the table of contents to find your perfect pair.

For years, the Men's Health team has relied on Dickies for its solid, no-fuss, workwear-meets-streetwear styles. In addition to well-built work pants, the brand's socks can go toe-to-toe (get it?) with just about every pair on the market, too. The fabric blend—71% cotton, 27% polyester, and 2% spandex—is an amazing trifecta, meaning they’re moisture-wicking, quick-drying, and odor-resistant. Plus, a little stretch goes a long way, ensuring the sock moves with you, not against you.

We found they're incredibly comfortable for casual wear and working out, thanks to their performance technology and superior arch support. They hit that perfect middle ground—they're not super thick, but they're not paper-thin either. There's enough cushioning without making your feet feel bulky or overheated.

Many of our testers didn't mind wearing them with boots, since the ventilation channels keep air flowing around the clock. That said, they really come in handy for guys on their feet 24/7—construction workers, mechanics, warehouse personnel, etc. No slipping, no bunching, just all-day support. As for the price, it’s hard to complain when you can stock up on three packs for under $50.

These socks from Mack Weldon look cool in a way that works just as well with a suit as with jeans and sneakers. Besides looking nice, the best part about these socks is their odor-fighting properties. Mack Weldon infused the Pima cotton with silver fibers that are naturally antimicrobial. They're using a little basic chemistry to keep your feet smelling good all day. We also love how they stay on and won't slide down, even after a whole day of wear, thanks to the stay-put cuff on these socks.

In our testing, we found that Mack Weldon’s crew socks are also great for walking, thanks to their cushioned footbed. The extended length provides extra coverage and warmth for your legs on cooler days, too. And since both the toe and heel are seamless and the fabric is ultra-soft, the comfort of these socks will keep your legs and feet feeling good all day long.

There isn't a sock quite like a Gold Toe. While holding the throne as the ultimate dad sock for the past 80 years, they have also started to make their way back into fashion in a big way. These updated classics, for instance, are made from a slightly thick blend of wool and nylon, and boast AquaFX Moisture Control that helps wick away sweat throughout the coverage area to help you stay fresh, cool, and dry. The ribbed construction also provides extra support and warmth, while the stretch will help the socks fit perfectly on your feet. We also love the reinforced toe that ensures no hole, wear, or tear will happen for a long time.

In my testing, I found that while these indeed are cushy and warm—and thus work great as winter socks—they are quite thick, which is why I love wearing them to fill up the space in my boots or dress shoes, but sometimes for sneakers and athletic shoes, the thickness can feel very tight. And unlike other socks, these don’t slide down even on large calf muscles.

No-show socks exist for one simple reason: to give you that clean, sockless look without the sweaty, blister-filled nightmare of actually going sockless. Enter this athletic, moisture-wicking pair made with Coolmax fiber, which helps keep perspiration in check when you're cycling, lifting, or running. Speaking of those activities, we also appreciate the seamless toe closure to prevent yarn chafing and discomfort while on the move. The light compression comes in handy, especially for runners, by stimulating blood flow and providing a snugger fit.

Aside from sport use, we found them to offer durability and excellent cushioning for casual wear. Bonus: Mesh ventilation on the instep ensures superb breathability from toe to heel. Plus, the three-pack is available in a variety of bold color pairings (orange, green, and blue).

Ask any sock aficionado their favorite sock brand, and there's a good chance they’ll say Bombas. If you’re wondering what makes its socks so special, the answer lies in the ultimate combination of smart design details and high-quality fabric. The brand takes all your needs into consideration with extra-long staple cotton fabric, which is longer and smoother than regular cotton. That means it's softer, stronger, and more durable—it resists pilling, holds color better, and feels luxuriously comfy against your skin.

On top of that, it’s made with seamless toe construction to eliminate pressure points, and even a little fabric tab at the back to prevent pesky, painful blisters. Plus, the honeycomb support system gives your arch a snug, supportive hug—keeping your feet feeling secure and energized all day. Overall, it helps distribute pressure evenly, so you're not dealing with sore, tired feet, whether you're running errands or running miles.

In our testing, we found that these are—by far—the comfiest ankle socks we've tried. We love that their stretchiness doesn’t seem to decrease despite tons of wear and washing. They’re also very versatile, great for walking thanks to the cushioned footbed, as well as training at the gym—although they’re not as robust as actual workout socks for training.

If there were a superhero version of a no-show sock, it would be this Balega option, and we especially approve of its usage in running. Made with 200-needle-count yarn and a fine-gauge knitting process, it results in smoother, denser, and more durable fabric. Off the track, however, our testers found that these socks are also great for sports, working out, yard work, or simply enjoying the comfort of your own home. The ventilation panels in the fabric ensure optimal airflow, while the high-heel tab and deep heel pocket prevent slipping—and we love that they're extra stretchy—meaning they’re perfect for anything involving heavy foot motion.

Overall, with 360-degree arch support, seamless toe construction, and a 3D-knitted heel, these Lululemon socks have just the right amount of stretch and cushion for a supported, frictionless fit. We've noticed a lower risk of chafing when we sport these during high-intensity workouts or runs. The heel is also complete with an extra-long fabric tab, so not only will you avoid a blister on your Achilles, but you also won't have to worry about these slipping down while you run.

Compression socks support and stabilize your muscles, while boosting circulation by directing blood back to your heart. That’s why this pair from Hanes is totally worth it. For starters, the cotton-polyester-nylon blend is super soft and comfortable against the skin. The cushioning offers extra padding and softens each step, so your feet and joints take less of a beating from hard-hitting moves—think running, jumping, or intense workouts. These socks also feature a performance fabric treatment that wicks away moisture and regulates temperature to keep your feet cool as a cucumber.

Plus, the arch compression keeps your foot firmly in place, which is a lifesaver if you overpronate or struggle with heel issues. Our fitness editors have worn these for leg recovery after intense workouts and running the New York Marathon. After slipping them on, wearers noticed their calves and arches instantly felt refreshed.

A past winner of our Outdoor Awards, Swiftwick socks are truly great for performance, especially for hiking and trail running. The Proprietary GripDry Fiber at the heel and forefoot works almost like a shoe outsole's traction to keep you steady—it’s got enough grip for anyone who wants to deadlift sans sneakers. The AnkleLock technology provides extra ankle stability, so the fit is super secure at all times. Apart from temperature regulation, the Merino wool blend fabric is also made to keep you blister-, odor-, and sweat-free.

In fact, our testers—some of whom are ultramarathon athletes—have even worn this pair to chase an insane 43-mile mountain record on the tallest volcano in North America. They claimed the socks performed flawlessly with no cold feet, no discomfort, and no holes. In terms of breathability, the mesh upper keeps your toes feeling cool as a cucumber. The flex channels enhance mobility, meaning your socks move naturally with your foot instead of feeling restrictive, which is crucial when tackling uneven terrain on the trails. And the Y-shaped heel works hand-in-hand with these channels, creating a contoured, stay-put fit that keeps the sock in place.

Great socks really come down to what they’re made of. The right material depends on where you’re wearing them and what you’re doing, whether that’s hitting the gym, heading to the office, going to a fancy dinner, or braving the elements.

Depending on your personal preference, the climate, the desired use, as well as your own feet, both thin and thick socks have their own advantages. Thin socks are usually more breathable and better at wicking away sweat and are thus great for combating foot odor. Thin socks are also preferred for warm weather. On the other hand, thick socks are your winter socks, providing better insulation and warmth. Thick socks often have better underfoot cushioning and padding as well, meaning they will help reduce friction in shoes or during long walks. Thick socks can sometimes add an entire size to your feet and make your shoe-wearing experience much more unbearable—unless you always size up for footwear, not to mention overheat your feet.

Keep in mind that socks that are too thin might not lend adequate protection, leading to blisters and foot pain. We recommend midweight socks because they can provide a good balance of comfort and support. A sock's thickness also denotes its weight, which is very personal. Some people like a heavier-weight sock with cushion to wear with a casual or lug sole shoe while others prefer a lighter-weight sock with their oxfords or loafers.

Sock height changes how your whole outfit reads and how your socks actually perform. The rise determines how much skin you’re showing (or not), how well your legs are protected, and whether your proportions look intentional or off. In other words: height matters. The “right” sock height ultimately comes down to personal taste—but it should always make sense with your shoes. Think about the shoes you’re wearing, the break of your pants (or shorts), and how much coverage you actually want. The right sock height can sharpen your proportions, prevent awkward gaps, and make the whole fit feel polished. When the proportions are off, the whole look feels off. Here’s how the main heights break down:

The best socks let your feet stay cool and breathable throughout the workday. Which is why you should preferably look for socks with moisture-wicking, arguably the most important feature in socks. Having moisture-wicking socks is extra crucial if you're wearing them for sports, long walks, or any hardcore outdoor activities because wicking away sweat and keeping your feet dry are keys to reducing the risk of blisters and fungal infections.

We’ve been testing socks since 2018, and over the years, the Men’s Health team has rotated through more than 50 pairs across daily commutes, heavy leg days, long-haul flights, winter hikes, and 20,000-step city weekends. If a sock slid down, trapped sweat, thinned out at the heel, or lost its shape after a few washes, it didn’t make this list.

We evaluated what actually matters in real life: comfort, breathability, durability, and fit. That meant paying close attention to materials—soft but sturdy cotton for everyday wear, moisture-wicking synthetics that hold up in the gym, and Merino wool that regulates temperature without overheating. Construction was non-negotiable. We looked for seamless toes that prevent rubbing, supportive arch bands that don’t feel restrictive, and cuffs that stay put without strangling your calves. And because socks shouldn’t fall apart after a season, we repeatedly washed and wore each pair to make sure they didn’t sag, pill, or thin out at the heel and toe.

We also tapped menswear expert Ted Stafford, who has styled countless MH covers and editorial shoots, to weigh in on proportion, height, and how socks should work with everything from tailored trousers to retro sneakers.

#5048. Kraken parent Payward to acquire Tribe Capital to expand private-market offerings

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The deal will add private-market sourcing, underwriting, and fund management capabilities to Payward's platform.

Kraken parent company Payward has signed an agreement to acquire Tribe Capital, a venture capital firm and asset manager with $2.7 billion in assets under management.

In a Wednesday statement, Payward said the deal will add private-market sourcing, underwriting, portfolio construction, and fund management capabilities to Payward's platform, expanding the range of investment products available across the company's client base.

Under the deal, Payward plans to develop new private-market offerings through Tribe's existing platform. Tribe is expected to operate within Payward's asset management business, according to the statement.

Tribe Capital, co-founded and chaired by Kraken Co-CEO Arjun Sethi, invests in early- and late-stage private companies across sectors including AI, advanced software, and financial infrastructure.

"Joining Payward gives us the opportunity to bring Tribe's investment approach to a global financial platform," Jonathan Hsu, co-founder of Tribe Capital, said in the statement. "We see a strong fit between our investment approach and Payward's infrastructure, distribution, and product-building capabilities."

The acquisition is expected to close in the first quarter of 2027, subject to customary closing conditions. The company did not disclose the financial terms of the deal.

The Block has reached out to Payward for further information.

Payward continues to expand its offerings through platforms including Kraken and xStocks. Last week, the company added more than 200 tokenized UK and European stocks through its xStocks framework, taking its number of tokenized assets past 1,500.

Payward also announced last week that it plans to introduce prediction markets for U.S. clients and offer equity perpetuals that run on its own exchange, clearinghouse, and broker.

In the third quarter of this year, the company reported $60 million in adjusted EBITDA, up from $23 million in the second quarter, but down 66% year over year, according to the private company's financial highlights released last week. Its total platform margin rose to $487 million in Q3 from $436 million in the previous quarter, but fell 12% year over year.

#5046. BTCPay Server issues emergency update for 5 security flaws

The EU’s cybersecurity service said the flaws could let attackers to run malicious code remotely on vulnerable servers without logging in.

BTCPay Server issued an emergency update to fix five security flaws in Nginx, urging operators of its self-hosted Bitcoin payment software to install it as soon as possible.

The vulnerabilities affect Nginx, the software that routes incoming web traffic to BTCPay’s payment application. The developers behind the Bitcoin payment software said the update installs Nginx version 1.31.9.

According to a Wednesday advisory from the European Union’s cybersecurity response team, CERT-EU, the flaws could allow attackers to remotely run malicious code on vulnerable servers without logging in.

Researchers demonstrated how to exploit one vulnerability, but CERT-EU said no attacks involving any of the five flaws were reported as of Wednesday.

BTCPay said operators can install the fix through the maintenance settings or their Docker installation. Those using node platforms should update when their platforms make it available. The project also pointed operators unable to update immediately to a temporary workaround.

The emergency update follows two earlier BTCPay Server security incidents involving a separate vulnerability that attackers exploited to steal crypto.

In August, attackers exploited a flaw to obtain credentials for Lightning Network nodes and steal funds. In November, BTCPay reported Bitcoin and Litecoin hot-wallet thefts on outdated software, saying attackers had used a different method to exploit the August flaw.

#5045. Palmer Luckey Says He’s Building an Offshore Haven for Industry—And 'Illegal' Medicine

Palmer Luckey Says He’s Building an Offshore Haven for Industry—And 'Illegal' Medicine

Palmer Luckey, the billionaire founder of Oculus and defense technology company Anduril, is spending over a billion dollars to have a private island where he can do things without worrying about U.S. laws.

He wants to build a massive industrial platform in the ocean where American businesses can operate without some of the regulations they face on land. And he has apparently been working on the idea for nearly a decade.

In an X post published Wednesday, Luckey revealed a project called SLAB, a proposed seastead with a 1,000-year design life that he says could support industries ranging from metal processing and oil refining to medical research and treatment.

Selling Oculus to Facebook in 2014 gave me money for helicopter flight lessons and developing a seastead called SLAB with a 1,000 year design life.

The goal was to be cheaper than land and absent state/local laws that cripple critical American industries like metal processing,… pic.twitter.com/aJkd0eOgLF

— Palmer Luckey (@PalmerLuckey) November 18, 2026

His goal, he wrote, is to create an industrial base cheaper than land and free from state and local laws that he believes are crippling critical American industries. Luckey described the project as a way to strengthen the United States rather than escape it, calling it a "relief valve" for outdated regulations.

“Me and a few close friends cashed it up at a $1B valuation back in 2017, and while it still has a long way to go, there has been significant progress towards my goal of reviving manifest destiny and westward expansion,” he wrote.

Luckey said he used proceeds from Facebook's 2014 acquisition of Oculus to finance the project.

The announcement included an AI-generated illustration of a gigantic offshore platform, rather than photographs of an operating facility so it’s hard to know which type of endeavor he’s dedicating his time and money to.

Luckey said he decided to disclose SLAB after learning that journalists were preparing to describe it as a floating AI data center. He rejected that characterization as incomplete, saying the project was considerably older and "way cooler than that."

Luckey provided a more specific indication of his ambitions in a heated exchange with journalist Joshua Topolsky, who questioned whether creating an offshore community was a sensible way to modernize medical research.

Topolsky argued that regulatory reform could happen without constructing an island in the ocean and challenged Luckey to identify which existing rules had prevented him from pursuing medical advances.

Luckey responded that he would rather build a place where people could save their families with "safe but currently illegal medicine ASAP" than wait for lawmakers to reform the system.

He accused political interests and large pharmaceutical companies of obstructing change, arguing that conventional reform would require legislation capable of securing support from 60 senators.

Luckey's plan also faces a significant legal complication: moving an operation into the ocean does not automatically place it outside American jurisdiction.

When another X user asked how close SLAB could operate to San Francisco without falling under its jurisdiction, Luckey answered, "Three miles."

That generally corresponds to the boundary of California's state-controlled coastal waters, according to the U.S. Bureau of Ocean Energy Management.

That said, federal jurisdiction extends considerably farther. The United States has a 12-nautical-mile territorial sea and a 200-nautical-mile exclusive economic zone, where it retains authority over certain economic activities and the construction and use of artificial islands, according to NOAA guidance.

The applicable rules would depend on SLAB's location, whether it floats or is attached to the seabed, and how it is registered. Even vessels operating on the high seas remain subject to applicable maritime laws and the jurisdiction of their flag states, so it’s not as if he’d be living in an anarchist fairy tale.

Medical research presents its own complications, if that’s what he’s actually doing. The FDA requires foreign clinical studies submitted in support of U.S. drug approvals to meet good clinical practice standards, including independent ethical review and informed consent. Conducting treatment offshore would not, by itself, make an unapproved drug legal for sale in the United States.

Previous seasteading experiments have encountered similar jurisdictional disputes. A floating community off Thailand triggered a confrontation with authorities in 2019, while a subsequent project in Panama adopted boat registration to operate within an existing legal framework.

This is all, of course, speculation. Luckey has not publicly identified SLAB's proposed location, the specific regulations it would avoid, or the medical treatments he hopes to make available. He has also not announced a construction deadline or provided independently verifiable details of the project's current physical development.

#5042. Ledger expands hardware hack investigation to Germany, UAE as new cases emerge

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The company confirmed that the new cases share the same kind of physical modification as tampered devices found across Southeast Asia.

Crypto hardware wallet maker Ledger has expanded its investigation into a physical supply-chain tampering campaign to Germany and the United Arab Emirates after identifying suspicious devices in Berlin and receiving loss reports in Dubai.

In a Wednesday update, the company said newly emerged cases were "the same incident" as ones involving tampered devices in Southeast Asia. It explained that it has confirmed the same kind of physical modification designed to capture the recovery phrase during setup.

"There is no flaw in Ledger's secure chip or operating system, and no confirmed case involving a device bought on ledger.com," Ledger stated in its update.

Ledger noted that it has progressed its investigation into reports of "a few tampered units" localized to Berlin, and urged anyone who purchased a device in the city from January to reach out to its support team.

In the UAE, the team has received reports of two tampered units in Dubai, and is investigating how they entered circulation. The company has also identified one case involving a newer model, the Ledger Nano Gen5, which was impacted in Southeast Asia.

Two X users, Rewinskyx and newbabylonorder, claimed to have lost a combined $604,000 in crypto from devices purchased at an authorized reseller in Dubai. The former reported losing about 1,300 SOL Solana (SOL), while the latter said 507,000 USDT Tether (USDT) was drained.

On Wednesday, Bitquery reported that roughly $1.68 million was stolen from 16 wallets in Dubai and Berlin, following the blockchain analytics firm's claim last month that the campaign drained about $102 million from 335 wallets in Southeast Asia.

Ledger has not officially confirmed the amount of losses from the supply chain tampering. The Block has reached out to Ledger for comment.

The expanded probe follows an investigation that began last month in Southeast Asia, where Ledger users reported unauthorized cryptocurrency drains. It was later identified that customers of authorized reseller CryptoBilis were targeted.

In a previous disclosure, Ledger stated that threat actors acquired genuine devices, installed additional custom-built hardware components to transmit recovery phrases externally, and reintroduced the units into reseller channels.

"As we continue our investigation, we expect it is possible that additional devices will be found, and we are working with partners and authorities to find affected devices as quickly as possible," Ledger said in the latest update. "This continues to be a small number of devices overall, and we do expect more to be discovered in our investigation and via social media."

#5039. ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and Grok Helped Plan Terror Attacks in Government Tests

ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and Grok Helped Plan Terror Attacks in Government Tests

Four of the most widely used AI chatbots helped government investigators develop potentially deadly attack plans in less than 12 hours, despite safeguards intended to stop exactly that kind of request.

OpenAI's ChatGPT, Anthropic's Claude, Google's Gemini and Grok all supplied usable information during covert testing by the Government Accountability Office, according to a report released Wednesday. Investigators posed as attackers, then checked the responses against real locations, available materials, and expert assessments.

Two systems assisted with plans involving improvised explosives in crowded U.S. cities. Two others supplied information for attacks on electrical infrastructure, according to the watchdog, which withheld the models' assignments and operational details to prevent copycat attempts.

Senator Maggie Hassan, the New Hampshire Democrat who requested the investigation, warned that a terrorist could obtain "a concrete playbook for how to carry out a deadly attack" in less than 12 hours, according to her statement.

The chatbots initially activated protections, including refusing requests, removing harmful information or suspending accounts. Investigators bypassed those barriers with common jailbreak techniques, which manipulate an AI system into ignoring its safety rules.

The GAO checked suggested locations, researched whether materials were available and consulted specialists about the plans' feasibility. Some answers contained errors, including misplaced roads on a map, and investigators did not construct explosives or execute an attack.

In the report, "viable" means somebody would likely be able to follow the information supplied, not that an attack was demonstrated to succeed. Each model was tested on one assigned scenario, and the agency said the results were not statistically representative or suitable for ranking the companies.

OpenAI and Google said the tests involved older models, while Anthropic acknowledged that determined users can sometimes extract harmful material. The companies described continuing improvements to safety systems, though the GAO concluded those protections failed in its tests.

This is not really new. The findings follow a previous research showing eight of 10 popular chatbots assisted simulated teenage users planning violent attacks. The separate study tested 720 responses and found the bots provided actionable help roughly three-quarters of the time.

The GAO findings arrive amid outages affecting banks, Microsoft services and the National Security Agency. Officials say the disruptions were separate incidents, not evidence of a coordinated AI-driven cyberattack, even as the rush to repair software flaws has created its own problems.

Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton connected the NSA's outage to patching work. Officials described the recent breakdowns as maintenance-related, although individual organizations offered different explanations for their failures.

AI tools now find vulnerabilities faster than human teams can test and deploy fixes. Last month, the AI-assisted discovery of critical PHP flaws pushed website operators into an urgent patching campaign; the software powers a large share of the web.

The sheer volume is becoming another problem. VulnCheck data reveals there have been more than 100,000 software vulnerabilities logged this year by November 13, more than twice the total for 2025. Engineers still have to evaluate and deploy fixes without breaking the systems they are meant to protect.

That acceleration cuts both ways. Defenders gain powerful tools for finding weaknesses, but hasty repairs can disrupt essential systems, and malicious users may gain faster access to attack planning.

Hassan said she will make AI safety legislation an early priority when she takes over the Senate Homeland Security and Governmental Affairs Committee in January. She plans hearings examining risks to public safety and critical infrastructure; the GAO itself made no formal policy recommendations.

Selling pressure also extended to US spot Ether (ETH) ETFs, which recorded around $40 million in outflows Wednesday, bringing their 2026 net withdrawals to roughly $895 million.

#5032. Can Testosterone Add Inches to Your Penis?

There are a lot of misconceptions about what testosterone boosters can and can’t do.

Welcome to Testosterone HQ—Men's Health's guide to the exciting, complicated, and revolutionary world of testosterone. For everything you need to know about T, click here.

IF TESTOSTERONE HELPS increase your penis size during puberty, surely taking TRT will help you get a bigger package?

Unfortunately, testosterone works a bit differently once you’re out of your late teens. Here’s what to know about that.

Testosterone, which is produced by your testicles, “plays a key role in the development of characteristics like libido, and growth of the penis and testicles,” says Jamin Brahmbhatt, MD, urologist at Orlando Health. The hormone surges in your body during your tween and teen years to achieve all of these changes (along with deepening of the voice, facial hair, and so on).

The “blast” of testosterone is usually over by your mid- to late teens. Reproductive urologist Paul Turek, MD, says this is because fertility is achieved as the testicles and penis reach adult size.

YOUR PENIS DOES go through some interesting changes as you age, but it doesn’t get bigger.

After testosterone stops surging in your late teens, your sexual development is set for life. Genetics are the main reason your penis grows to its permanent size. “You’re born with the size already determined,” says Dr. Brahmbhatt.

Now testosterone is still being produced in your body when you’re an adult, but it won’t have any further effect on penis size. That biological fact holds true no matter your penis length. One study done by researchers at the University of Utah School of Medicine found that stretched penile length did not have any association with testosterone levels in adult men.

Since the penis consists of spongy tissue, not muscle, there’s no way to build it up on your own. “There are no safe and effective methods for permanently increasing penis size,” says Dr. Brahmbhatt. Penis pumps might help with stronger erections, but they do nothing for overall size.

WHILE TESTOSTERONE HAD an effect on your penis length early on, more won't help now. It might even hurt to take any sketchy T boosters or penis enlargement pills that promise to increase your girth or length. You have no idea whether they’re safe to take or whether they contain harmful ingredients.

A Stanford University study found that the average erect penile length has grown 24 percent worldwide over the past 29 years. The researchers report this is not due to an increase in testosterone levels.

Any change in development in a short amount of time is concerning. One of the authors of the study noted that chemical exposure could be interacting with the hormone makeup of guys—which may also explain lower sperm counts, increases in testicular cancer rates, and lower T levels in men nowadays.

The bottom line is that size doesn’t matter. Your penis health—especially function—does. If you notice any changes in how your penis looks or functions—patchy spots, bumps, curves—definitely don’t ignore them.

And while it’s natural to be curious about how your penis stacks up with other guys, don’t worry. The average penis length is not as big as you think.

Lisa is an internationally established health writer whose credits include Good Housekeeping, Prevention, Men’s Health, Oprah Daily, Woman’s Day, Elle, Cosmopolitan, Harper’s Bazaar, Esquire, Glamour, The Washington Post, WebMD, Medscape, The Los Angeles Times, Parade, Health, Self, Family Circle and Seventeen. She is the author of eight best-selling books, including The Essentials of Theater.

#5031. Sygnum sees bitcoin carry trades beating Treasury bills in 2027 as funding recovers

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Bitcoin funding stayed negative for 67 consecutive days into May before recovering to about 8% annualized by the end of October.

The Swiss bank expects spot-and-short-futures trades to pay more than Treasury bills on average in 2027, and sets a bar of more than two percentage points above the three-month T-bill for the thesis to hold.

Sygnum expects the strategy of buying bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) on the spot market while shorting futures to generate higher average returns than U.S. Treasury bills next year.

Carry strategies combine long spot exposure with short futures contracts. The trade had a difficult run in 2026, when bitcoin's 30-day average funding rate stayed below zero for 67 consecutive days through May.

In its 2027 Sygnal outlook report, the Switzerland-based bank said the annualized basis on CME bitcoin futures had fallen to about 3% by August, below the 3.8% yield on two-year US Treasuries. Hedge funds trading on CME also turned net long for the first time in years as the basis trade stopped paying.

Bitcoin funding rates had recovered to about 8% annualized by the end of October, while yields on sUSDe stood at roughly 5.2%, according to the report.

Sygnum also based its outlook on a potential interest rate hike in the U.S. The bank said fed funds futures imply at least one further 25-basis-point hike through the end of 2027, up from the 3.75% to 4% the Federal Reserve set on Sept. 16.

From the bank's perspective, higher interest rates are likely to raise the return bitcoin carry trades must generate to compete with Treasury bills, while the demand for leverage, alongside wider digital asset adoption, should keep funding rates and futures basis mostly positive next year.

"With advancing digital asset adoption, Bitcoin going from policy to portfolio, tokenization increasing blockchain infrastructure at two speeds, and the AI demand flywheel continuing, we expect demand for leverage to keep Bitcoin funding rates mostly positive through 2027," the bank wrote.

The outlook rests on two indicators: the annualized three-month CME Bitcoin futures basis and the open-interest-weighted average funding rate. Both would need to average more than 2 percentage points above three-month Treasury bill yields in 2027 for Sygnum's forecast to hold.

Meanwhile, the bank would consider its forecast wrong if either average carry or funding returns fell below Treasury bills for two consecutive quarters.

#5030. Ledger Confirms Tampered Bitcoin Wallets in Berlin as Spy Implant Probe Spreads Beyond Asia

Ledger Confirms Tampered Bitcoin Wallets in Berlin as Spy Implant Probe Spreads Beyond Asia

The tampered Ledger wallets aren’t just a Southeast Asia problem anymore.

Ledger said Wednesday it has confirmed tampered hardware wallets in Berlin and is investigating reports of two more in Dubai, expanding a probe it had previously described as a localized incident in Southeast Asia.

“Our findings since then show tampered devices outside Southeast Asia. It is the same incident,” the company said in an update on its support site.

The investigation began last month after customers who bought devices from reseller CryptoBilis reported losses. Onchain investigators traced a suspected drain of about $87 million from hundreds of victim wallets across Ethereum, Tron, and Bitcoin.

Ledger said at the time that all confirmed cases were tied to CryptoBilis, and it later asked resellers in seven Asian markets to pull devices from sale.

Every device Ledger has confirmed shows the same kind of physical modification, designed to capture a user’s recovery phrase during setup. A recovery phrase, typically a string of 24 words, is the master key to a crypto wallet: anyone who has it can drain the funds.

Our investigation into the hardware implant incident we disclosed Oct 15th is ongoing, and we published an update: https://t.co/wx6mfQPr99

There is no flaw in Ledger's secure chip or operating system, and no confirmed case involving a device bought on https://t.co/vQ4QWa1D0f

Ledger maintains there’s no flaw in its secure chip or operating system and no confirmed case involving a device bought on ledger.com.

In Germany, Ledger said a few tampered units were localized to Berlin, and it urged anyone who bought a Ledger device there since January 2026 to contact support.

In Dubai, it’s working with distribution partners to learn how two suspected units entered circulation. Those devices were bought at a Virgin Megastore location, according to multiple reports. Ledger also found one affected Nano Gen5 among the Southeast Asian resellers it previously paused.

The company expects more tampered devices to surface, though it says the number remains small overall. Its Donjon security team is examining suspected devices, and Ledger has asked resellers to pause sales wherever inspections are ongoing.

Customers worried about a device should stop using it, Ledger said. It advises moving funds to a new wallet created on a device bought directly from ledger.com, without reusing the old recovery phrase or passphrase, since a tampered device can capture both. It also recommends filing a report with local authorities.

For a limited time, Ledger is offering physical inspections, including X-rays, at its factory in Vierzon, France.

“We will never ask for your recovery phrase,” the company said on X.

The last few months have been a rough stretch for hardware wallet markers overall. Rival manufacturer Trezor said this week that hackers who breached a third-party shipping partner likely took information tied to about 27,000 orders. That’s the company’s third such breach in as many months.

#5028. Singapore’s MAS issues $77 million in tokenized bills settled via CBDC

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The central bank said the bills were issued natively on its Singapore Dollar Test Network and settled in wholesale CBDC.

The Monetary Authority of Singapore announced Thursday that it issued S$100 million ($77.5 million) of four-week tokenized MAS Bills on Nov. 6, as the country steps up its tokenization drive.

In a statement released Thursday, the central bank said the bills were issued natively on its Singapore Dollar Test Network and settled in wholesale central bank digital currency (CBDC). The bills will mature on Dec. 4.

Notably, five primary dealers — DBS Bank, HSBC, OCBC, UOB, and Standard Chartered — participated in the auction, which drew applications more than twice the issuance size.

The issuance builds on the 2025 live trial of interbank overnight lending transactions settled via wholesale CBDC. "This paves the way for secured lending transactions involving tokenized assets to take place on the Singapore Dollar Test Network," the MAS said.

The Singapore Dollar Test Network is a shared ledger infrastructure that enables financial institutions to test the settlement of tokenized financial assets using wholesale CBDC, according to the statement. It will be renamed to SG Digital Ledger, the MAS said.

"The live issuance allowed us to test a new method of issuance, and further enhance our in-house distributed ledger infrastructure," said Chia Der Jiun, Managing Director of MAS.

In the next phase, the MAS plans to scale tokenized MAS Bills, expand their use for collateral management, and onboard more institutions.

"MAS aims to demonstrate how tokenized securities can be used more widely in mainstream financial market activities, potentially achieving greater settlement efficiencies in the process," said the central bank.

In an accompanying statement released Thursday, Lim Soon Chong, group head of global transaction services at DBS Bank, said that the initiative has provided "greater confidence" for financial institutions to transact in tokenized assets.

"Over time, more robust infrastructure can help catalyze trading activity and deepen liquidity, supporting the progression of tokenized assets from issuance and experimentation toward more active secondary markets," said Lim.

The issuance comes as Singaporean financial institutions accelerate their adoption of tokenized assets, with MAS' Chia saying that tokenization has "moved beyond experiments."

On Thursday, Standard Chartered announced an expansion of its tokenized deposits solution to enable near real-time transactions across Singapore and Hong Kong. The bank said eligible clients can now utilize commercial bank deposits within digital treasury and investment workflows.

Meanwhile, StraitsX, OCBC, and Standard Chartered said Thursday that they have completed a proof-of-concept testing interoperability between tokenized deposit networks issued by different banks.

Earlier this week, DBS Bank and Kinexys by JPMorgan also launched an interoperability solution designed to enable institutions to make instant, around-the-clock transfers of USD tokenized deposits between the U.S. and Singapore.

#5026. Ant International launches protocol for real-time settlement between tokenized deposits and money market funds

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Ant launched the Whale Orchestration Protocol under Singapore's MAS BLOOM initiative to enable settlement between tokenized deposits and tokenized money market funds.

Singapore-based fintech firm Ant International, which began operating independently following a 2024 reorganization of Ant Group, has launched the Whale Orchestration Protocol, an upgrade to its blockchain-based wholesale settlement platform.

In a Thursday press release, the company said the protocol was developed under the Monetary Authority of Singapore's BLOOM initiative to enable delivery-versus-payment transactions between tokenized deposits and tokenized money market funds.

Specifically, the protocol operates through WhaleRTP, Ant's blockchain-based wholesale settlement platform for real-time treasury and liquidity management. "This unlocks immediate liquidity for the company's real-time global treasury management, allowing idle capital to be deployed in real time," the company said.

Ant International also said it has completed pilot transactions with DBS and Franklin Templeton after the two integrated their respective Treasury Tokens and Tokenized Money Market Fund solutions into its WhaleRTP platform.

"As a full-stack financial solutions provider, 24/7 real-time liquidity management is essential for our business," said Kelvin Li, general manager of platform tech and SVP at Ant International.

Ant International is among the institutions participating in the BLOOM initiative, which the Singaporean central bank launched in October 2025 to develop approaches for settlement using tokenized bank deposits and stablecoins.

The launch comes as Singapore's central bank and major financial institutions step up efforts to develop tokenized financial infrastructure. The MAS announced Thursday that it issued S$100 million ($77.5 million) of four-week tokenized MAS Bills on Nov. 6. The bills were issued natively on its Singapore Dollar Test Network and settled in wholesale CBDC.

Also on Thursday, Standard Chartered announced an expansion of its tokenized deposits solution to enable near real-time transactions across Singapore and Hong Kong.

StraitsX, OCBC, and Standard Chartered also said on the same day that they have completed a trial examining interoperability between tokenized deposit networks issued by different banks.

Earlier this week, DBS Bank and Kinexys by JPMorgan also launched an interoperability solution designed to enable institutions to make instant transfers of USD tokenized deposits between the U.S. and Singapore.

#5022. 1inch integrates Hyperliquid’s perpetual futures contracts into its dApp and wallet platform

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Users can fund a perps position with a token they already hold on 1inch, which will be routed to USDC and deposited to a balance on Hyperliquid.

1inch (1inch) announced today that Hyperliquid-powered perpetual futures (perps) are now available to users in eligible regions via the 1inch dApp and Wallet.

The DeFi platform said in a press release that by adding perpetual futures, it seeks to strengthen its offerings to make the ecosystem more holistic. Hyperliquid Hyperliquid (HYPE) markets are available to dApp and Wallet users on Android, with plans to roll out the 1inch Wallet app on iOS in the "next couple of weeks," according to a 1inch spokesperson.

The statement said that 1inch provides the interface to the markets run by Hyperliquid, which cover crypto, stocks, commodities, and foreign exchange. Users can fund a perps position with a token they already hold on 1inch, which will be routed to USDC and then deposited into a balance on Hyperliquid.

"Our goal is clear: whatever users want to do in DeFi, they should be able to do it through 1inch, while maintaining full control of their assets," said Sergej Kunz, 1inch co-founder.

One of the Hyperliquid perps contracts available on 1inch is ANT, which tracks the anticipated price of one Anthropic Class A share as the AI frontier lab prepares to go public later on Thursday.

The 1inch dApp and Wallet on Android allows users to place a market order, a limit order, or a time-weighted average price (TWAP) order. They can attach a take-profit or a stop-loss at entry, or add one later. Users can also add to a position, reduce it, or close it.

The two apps differ in default margin settings. The 1inch Wallet sets isolated margin as the default so that only the position's margin is at risk. The dApp, on the other hand, sets cross-margin as the default, so the balance on Hyperliquid can back open positions.

Since its launch, major DeFi platforms — including Uniswap, Morpho, and Curve — have integrated Hyperliquid's perps market. Kraken parent Payward is also set to be the first CFTC-regulated deployer of Hyperliquid's HIP-3 markets.

According to a recent report from Hyperliquid Research Collective, the derivatives market platform closed out the third quarter with a record $16.8 billion in open derivatives positions, surpassing centralized exchanges such as Bybit, Kraken and OKX in open interest.
